# Appendix — full finding set (Yocto → Buildroot port)

Raw output of the eight-domain comparison behind [PORTING-FROM-YOCTO.md](PORTING-FROM-YOCTO.md). Kept verbatim so
nothing is lost to summarisation; the main document is the curated, ordered version and should be read first.

Verdicts: **CONFIRMED** upheld by an independent adversarial verifier · **corrected** amended by the verifier (the
amended text is what appears here) · **unverified** single reading, confirm before acting.

Paths in `evidence` are absolute as emitted by the investigating agents.


---

## UEFI boot chain and bootloader

The two systems occupy the same firmware slot (\EFI\BOOT\BOOTAA64.EFI on a GPT ESP loaded by Radxa's EDK2 in SPI NOR) but put different payloads there: the proven Yocto system ships oe-core's systemd-boot plus Type 1 loader entries pointing at a raw kernel Image + initramfs on a 512 MiB ESP, while we ship Buildroot GRUB 2.12 with grubenv-based A/B and kernel+DTB inside each slot's squashfs on a 32 MiB ESP. The "signature" problem the user remembers was NOT a UEFI Secure Boot signature check: nothing in the Yocto tree is ever signed (UKI_SB_KEY/UKI_SB_CERT are never set, no sbsign/shim/MOK/db-KEK-PK anywhere), and an unsigned bootaa64.efi boots on the real board. What actually broke was EDK2's measured-boot path (MeasureBoot/TrEE, MeasurePeImgAll/HashLogExtendEvent) failing on the 47 MB UKI PE image and aborting boot into a watchdog reset; it was fixed in commit 3bb7a7d by abandoning the …


### [BLOCKER] GRUB boots the kernel via firmware LoadImage() — the same EDK2 path that aborted on the 47 MB UKI; our Image is 39 MiB  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Quotes are verbatim and correct. Two additions that change the interpretation: (1) the failing artifact is /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/build/tmp/deploy/images/radxa-dragon-q6a/q6a.efi = 53,047,296 bytes (50.6 MiB), and the raw Image the fix ships is Image--6.18.37-r0-...bin = 43,137,536 bytes (41.1 MiB) — i.e. the PROVEN-WORKING Yocto kernel Image is LARGER than our 41,142,784-byte Image. So size alone is not what distinguishes pass from fail on this board; the loader path is. (2) The bbappend's mechanism claim is technically accurate for this board, which I verified in systemd source (oe-core/meta/recipes-core/systemd/systemd_259.5.bb; esp-qcom-image.bb has PACKAGE_INSTALL = "systemd-boot"): systemd v259 src/boot/linux.c linux_exec() only calls BS->LoadImage (load_via_boot_services()) when `secure_boot_enabled() && (shim_loader_available() || (shim_loaded() && security_override_available()))`; otherwise it hand-loads the PE sections with xmalloc_pages+memcpy and jumps to the entry point directly. Secure Boot is off on this board, so Type 1 entries genuinely neve…

**Ours today:** Confirmed in full, with exact evidence: nerves_defconfig:57 BR2_TARGET_GRUB2=y; buildroot 2026.05 boot/grub2/grub2.mk:7 GRUB2_VERSION = 2.12; grub.cfg:84,89 boot the kernel with `linux ($nerves_disk,gptN)/boot/Image ...` → grub_linux_boot() → grub_arch_efi_linux_boot_image(), grub-core/loader/efi/linux.c:211 `b->load_image(...)`, :215 `grub_error (GRUB_ERR_BAD_OS, "cannot load image")`, :238 `b->start_image()` — no non-x86 fallback (the handover path is #if defined(__i386__)||defined(__x86_64__) only). Kernel size verified independently: unsquashfs -ll of disk.img sector 67584 → squashfs-root/boot/Image = 41,142,784 bytes. CONFIG_EFI_STUB=y (linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:119), so it is a PE and GRUB's header check (linux.c:99-121, requires only MZ + native PE32+ magic) passes. Grep of the whole repo (excluding deps/_build) finds no systemd-boot, no zboot, no alternate loader — the gap is real, nothing exists under another name, and no .md in the repo mentions LoadImage/MeasureBoot/UKI, so it is also undocumented. One nit: the Tcg2 lines are at test/qemu-run.trimmed.log:27 and :29, not 24…

**Why it matters:** If the Q6A EDK2's MeasureBoot failure is size/PE-driven rather than specific to ukify's section layout, GRUB will fail exactly like the UKI did — LoadImage returns an error, GRUB prints "cannot load image" and the board never boots. GRUB has no fallback path, so there is no graceful degradation. This is the single highest-risk item in the whole port and it is completely invisible to our QEMU test (QEMU's EDK2 has no TPM: test/qemu-run.trimmed.log shows `Tpm2SubmitCommand - Tcg2 - Not Found`, so measured boot never runs).

**Action:** 1) Keep item 1 as-is (first bench test: watch serial for "Booting slot A" then either the kernel banner or "cannot load image"/silent ~30 s watchdog reset; record in BRINGUP.md). 2) FIX the config symbols: `BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_CUSTOM_CONFIG_FILE` exists (buildroot-2026.05/linux/Config.in:212) but `depends on BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_CUSTOM_CONFIG` (:199), which is a Kconfig choice member — so you must add BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_CUSTOM_CONFIG=y AND delete nerves_defconfig:40 BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y, otherwise the CUSTOM_CONFIG_FILE line is silently ignored. Drop the arbitrary "<20 MiB" target: the proven-good Yocto system boots a 41.1 MiB raw Image, so shrinking is risk-reduction on an unproven hypothesis, not a known fix, and pruning arm64 defconfig is expensive and regression-prone. 3) Cheaper, higher-leverage size mitigation the finding missed: EFI zboot. Add CONFIG_EFI_ZBOOT=y (and a compressor, e.g. CONFIG_EFI_ZBOOT_COMPRESS_GZIP) to linux-dragon-q6a.fragment and build arch/arm64/boot/vmlinuz.efi (~15 MB — cf. the Yocto tree's Image.gz at 15,359,901 bytes for the same kernel). Note BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_VMLINUZ_EFI (linux/Config.in:325) is `depends on BR2_loongarch64`, so it CANNOT be used on aarch64: use BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_IMAGE_TARGET_CUSTOM=y + BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_IMAGE_TARGET_NAME="vmlinuz.efi" (+ BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_IMAGE_NAME if the installed name must stay "Image"), and update grub.cfg:84,89 and post-build.sh accordingly. This cuts the measured payload by ~2.6x with a one-line kernel-config change; still a hypothesis, but far cheaper than a defconfig rewrite. 4) Escape hatch is more expensive than stated: BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_BOOT (package/systemd/Config.in:113) `depends on BR2_i386 || BR2_x86_64` and selects BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_EFI + gnu-efi, so on aarch64 it needs a genuinely new Buildroot package that builds only systemd's src/boot for arm64 under BR2_INIT_NONE; and fwup.conf BOOT_PART_COUNT=65536 (32 MiB ESP, confirmed by fdisk on test/work/disk.img) cannot hold two ~39 MiB kernels + initrds — that needs ~192-256 MiB and a partition-layout change that is not an in-place OTA (re-flash only). Document that in BRINGUP.md as a design decision rather than as a drop-in fallback.

**Uncertainty:** I could not determine the exact failure threshold or root cause inside the board's EDK2. The Yocto kernel Image is also tens of MB and boots fine when loaded by systemd-boot, so the trigger may be ukify's PE section layout (.linux/.initrd/.dtb sections) rather than raw size — in which case GRUB's LoadImage of a plain arm64 Image would be fine. I also could not confirm from source whether systemd-b…

### [BLOCKER] Our grub.cfg overrides the firmware-provided device tree; the proven system deliberately uses the EDK2 DTB  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Accurate; three refinements. (a) KERNEL_DEVICETREE is NOT globally empty for this board — radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:19 sets `KERNEL_DEVICETREE = "qcom/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb"` at machine level (so the DTB is built and lands in the rootfs); the blanking is scoped to the ESP image recipe (esp-qcom-image.bb:17). The point stands: nothing on the boot path loads it. (b) The spinor DTB payload is inert not because QCOM_DTB_FILE is blanked — it keeps its default `dtb.bin` (image_types_qcom.bbclass:16) — but because the whole spinor block is gated on QCOM_BOOT_FILES_SUBDIR / QCOM_PARTITION_FILES_SUBDIR_SPINOR, which the machine conf empties (lines 30-32), and on QCOM_DTB_DEFAULT + a `dtb-*-image.vfat` that is never deployed (bbclass:57-62). (c) Add the decisive proof the entries are live on hardware: q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:141 `do_image_wic[depends] += "esp-qcom-image:do_image_complete q6a-boot-env:do_deploy"` and files/wic/q6a-ab.wks.in:5 rawcopy of `esp-qcom-image-${MACHINE}.rootfs.vfat`, plus commit 3bb7a7d which authored q6a-a.conf/q6a-b.conf. Note also QCOM_ESP_IMAGE = "" (conf:50) on…

**Ours today:** Accurate as written. Add two facts that strengthen it: (1) the DTB really is built and installed — nerves_defconfig:42-43 (`BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_DTS_SUPPORT=y`, `BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_INTREE_DTS_NAME="qcom/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a"`) put it in $BINARIES_DIR so post-build.sh:52-54 always copies it, i.e. the override is effective, not a silently-skipped conditional; (2) test/grub.cfg.qemu:5-8 already implements and documents the correct firmware-DTB behaviour and is the config used by the ONLY boot we ever proved (test/qemu-smoke.sh, GOAL.md "headline goal MET"). So the `devicetree` line in the HW grub.cfg has never been executed anywhere. BRINGUP.md:51 already harvests `/sys/firmware/fdt`, so the proposed bench check is a small extension of an existing step, not new tooling.

**Why it matters:** The firmware DTB carries boot-time state a statically built DTB cannot: the memory map and reserved-memory carveouts that must agree with what XBL/TZ set up, DDR size/SKU fixups, and board revision differences. A mismatch does not produce a clean error — it shows up as SMMU faults or remoteproc (ADSP/CDSP) load failures, i.e. exactly the NPU symptoms this system exists to avoid. Additionally, GRUB registers `devicetree` via grub_register_command_lockdown (fdt.c:175), so if Secure Boot were ever enabled the command silently disappears and the boot would change behaviour with no diagnostic.

**Action:** 1) grub.cfg: replace the unconditional `devicetree` calls with a grubenv-gated debug override rather than deleting them outright — keep `set nerves_dtb="/boot/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb"` (line 77) and inside each slot branch use `if [ "$force_dtb" = "1" ]; then devicetree ($nerves_disk,gptN)$nerves_dtb; fi`. Default is unset -> firmware DTB is used; a bench operator can recover a no-FDT board with `grub-editenv /EFI/BOOT/grubenv set force_dtb=1` instead of re-flashing the ESP. `test` is already in BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI. 2) Rewrite grub.cfg:15-18 to state that the SPI-NOR EDK2 publishes the board DTB via the EFI_DEVICE_TREE_GUID configuration table and the kernel EFI stub consumes it, so GRUB must not install one; cite the proven Yocto Type 1 entries. Drop the now-irrelevant Secure Boot warning about `devicetree` (or keep it attached to the force_dtb debug path only). 3) Do NOT remove `fdt` from BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI (nerves_defconfig:58): grub-core/loader/efi/linux.c calls grub_fdt_load()/grub_fdt_install() unconditionally, so the `linux` module needs it regardless. 4) Fix the docs that assert the wrong model: fwup.conf:6-9 (and the "kernel+DTB" wording at fwup.conf:51,65 / fwup-ops.conf:65), README.md:41-46 and 59, GOAL.md:37, 77-78, and the Armbian note at GOAL.md:105-110 — mark that note as superseded rather than deleting it (it is the reason a debug override is retained). 5) post-build.sh:49-54: keep installing the DTB, add a comment that it is a debug/comparison artifact loaded only when force_dtb=1. 6) Surface the design consequence explicitly in GOAL.md/README: the DTB leaves the atomic A/B upgrade unit. Kernel and DTB can now skew, so any future kernel bump that requires a newer DT binding cannot be delivered by OTA — it needs an SPI-NOR EDK2 update via Radxa's edl-ng path. This is a real constraint the Yocto system also lives with. 7) BRINGUP.md: extend the existing `cp /sys/firmware/fdt` step (line 51) into an explicit A/B comparison — `dtc -I fs -O dts /proc/device-tree | head`, `cat /proc/device-tree/model`, and diff the reserved-memory nodes against images/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb — to record which DTB is live on the first bench boot, and record the EDK2 build string while there (GOAL.md's frozen tuple still has "record from bench boot log" blank for the UEFI firmware row).

**Uncertainty:** Armbian's qcs6490 family reportedly uses a `grub-with-dtb` extension that does install its own DTB, which conflicts with the user-confirmed "DTB must come from UEFI" rule. I could not inspect Armbian's config from here. The proven-on-hardware Yocto system is the stronger evidence, so I recommend following it, but the tension should be recorded rather than erased.

### [MAJOR] GRUB's grubenv write path (EFI Block I/O) is unproven on this board — silent loss of A/B rollback if it fails  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Accurate, with one precision fix. Real lines from /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select: 54: mount_esp && bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default "q6a-${active}.conf" 55: mount_esp && bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-oneshot "q6a-${active}.conf" 60: mount_esp && bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-oneshot "q6a-${active}.conf" 85: mount_esp && bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default "q6a-${booted}.conf" 22: printf '%s %s %s\n' "${disk}p4" 0x0 "$ENV_SIZE" > /etc/fw_env.config 25/31/59/65-67: fw_setenv nerves_fw_devpath / *_part0_devpath / q6a_boot_attempts / nerves_fw_active / nerves_fw_validated Precision fix: the bootloader is systemd-boot with BLS entries (meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image.bbappend:32-33 installs /loader/loader.conf + /loader/entries/q6a-a.conf; loader.conf:1 = "default q6a-a.conf"). bootctl set-default/set-oneshot write the LoaderEntryDefault/LoaderEntryOneShot EFI variables (the ESP is mounted only so bootctl can locate it), and systemd-boot clears the oneshot variable at boot — …

**Ours today:** Correct as stated, with one strengthening and one weakening: STRONGER than claimed: save_env is not merely "shown to work in QEMU by a 1024-byte file listing". There is affirmative on-media proof from a real run: test/work/rollback-serial.log:13 shows "Slot was not validated after its first boot - falling back!" / :14 "Booting slot A", and test/work/grubenv.probe (the ESP copy read back after that boot) contains `boot=0 / booted_once=1 / validated=1` while test/work/grubenv.crafted (what was put there before the boot) contains `boot=1 / booted_once=1 / validated=0`. Nothing in userspace rewrote it on that path — fwup-ops.conf reconcile.a (281-290) touches only uboot-env, and autovalidate then hits autovalidate.skip because reconcile already set nerves_fw_validated=1. So GRUB's raw-sector write via EDK2 + virtio-blk BlockIo is demonstrated; only Radxa's SD/eMMC BlockIo is untested. WEAKER than claimed: "auto-rollback is dead" overstates it. If save_env fails, a bad slot that still reaches userspace is unaffected (qcom-coldplug.sh:22-25 -> `fwup -t autovalidate` writes grubenv from Lin…

**Why it matters:** grub.cfg:48-62 implements boot-once semantics by writing booted_once=1 before handing off. If that write silently fails on real hardware, booted_once never persists, so a freshly written unvalidated slot gets retried forever and the fallback branch at grub.cfg:49-57 is never reached — auto-rollback is dead while every test still appears to pass, because the happy path (validated=1) needs no write at all. This is the worst kind of failure: invisible until an OTA bricks a field device.

**Action:** Keep the bench test, but fix three defects in the procedure (otherwise step 3 silently tests nothing): (1) ISOLATE save_env FIRST — no OTA needed. Boot the factory image (ships grubenv_a_valid: boot=0 booted_once=1 validated=1, fwup.conf:208). Then from the target: `mount -t vfat -o rw /dev/rootdisk0p1 /mnt` and overwrite /mnt/EFI/BOOT/grubenv with a 1 KiB plain-text GRUB env block containing `boot=0`, `booted_once=0`, `validated=0` (exact format at test/qemu-rollback.sh:145-155: "# GRUB Environment Block\n" + KV lines + '#' padding to exactly 1024 bytes; busybox printf/head -c is enough), `umount /mnt`, reboot. After that boot, re-mount the ESP read-only and `cat`/`head -c 200 /mnt/EFI/BOOT/grubenv` — if it now reads `booted_once=1`, GRUB's save_env reached the media through Radxa's EDK2 BlockIo. That single check is the whole proof and needs no fwup task. (2) MUST disable autovalidate before any trial-boot variant of the test. fwup.conf:191 sets `nerves_fw_autovalidate=1` in the factory image, and qcom-coldplug.sh:22-25 runs `fwup -t autovalidate` on every boot, which writes grubenv_b_valid to the ESP from Linux (fwup-ops.conf:328-339). So after `fwup -t upgrade.b` + reboot, grubenv becomes validated=1 via the Linux path and the second reboot can never reach the fallback branch — the test would "pass" while proving nothing about save_env. Run `fw_setenv nerves_fw_autovalidate 0` (or craft the trial grubenv directly, as in (1)) before the reboot that is supposed to burn the one try. (3) Do NOT plan to verify with `grub-editenv` on target — Buildroot's grub2 is a bootloader package; it installs host tools and image artifacts only, nothing named grub-editenv lands in the rootfs (post-build.sh:7-13 explicitly looks for it in $HOST_DIR). grubenv is plain text, so `cat`/`head -c` on the mounted ESP, or reading the card on the host, is the verification. Mitigation-if-it-fails, as stated, is sound and needs no new BR2 symbol: make grub.cfg read-only (drop both save_env calls at grub.cfg:56,60), keep slot selection in grubenv but let only Linux write it (fwup fat_write, as fwup.conf:283,343 and fwup-ops.conf:154-183 already do), and keep the attempt counter in the uboot-env KV block (fwup.conf:161-164) owned by qcom-coldplug.sh — noting GRUB cannot read that KV block, so a slot that never reaches userspace loses automatic rollback. Worth adding: the Yocto tree proves runtime EFI-variable writes work on this board (bootctl set-oneshot at q6a-boot-select:55,60), so a third option is an EFI-variable-based selector rather than a disk write.

**Uncertainty:** I did not read GRUB's envblk/blocklist write implementation to confirm the exact requirements (contiguity, sector alignment) beyond the fact that the file is a single preallocated 1 KiB block. Whether Radxa's EDK2 SD BlockIo supports writes is unknown.

### [MAJOR] SPI-NOR firmware version floor (6.0.260120) and on-device flash tooling are absent from our system  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Shipped in the image (commit ad69735 "Ship edl-ng + SPI boot firmware 260120 for offline on-device flash"). q6a-spi-firmware_260120.bb fetches https://dl.radxa.com/dragon/q6a/images/dragon-q6a_flat_build_wp_260120.zip (sha256 cf23a1742ae5d51c451947d82cb21fd3f1c10bbcd621d611f55520673e3f90ca), installs flat_build/spinor/dragon-q6a/ to /usr/share/q6a-spi-firmware/dragon-q6a and the helper to /usr/sbin/q6a-spi-flash; RDEPENDS = "edl-ng dmidecode". files/q6a-spi-flash pins TARGET_VERSION="6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1", reads the current version with `dmidecode -s bios-version`, exits early if equal, and otherwise runs `edl-ng --hostdev-as-target /dev/mtd0 rawprogram rawprogram0.xml patch0.xml` from the firmware directory. edl-ng_1.5.0.bb installs a prebuilt arm64 binary (edl-ng-linux-arm64.zip, sha256 ab8a6cd4...) plus libSystem.IO.Ports.Native.so. README.md:82-88 states the requirement bluntly: firmware must be >= 6.0.260120 or "the DSP reject unsigned protection domains (0x80000600) and QNN is unusable".

**Ours today:** Correct that we ship nothing (grep-verified: no package/edl-ng, no package/q6a-spi-firmware, no payload, no BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE). Three refinements. (a) The capability is not absent, only the on-device path: BRINGUP.md:19-28 flashes the identical rawprogram0.xml/patch0.xml from a host with the board in EDL mode (edl-ng v1.4.1, `--memory spinor --loader prog_firehose_ddr.elf`, Type-A<->Type-A cable). (b) The floor is half-documented already — BRINGUP.md:31-32 "Armbian notes the **latest UEFI firmware is required**"; what is missing is the exact string and the 0x80000600 linkage. (c) The claim omitted its own best evidence: our tree attributes 0x80000600 *exclusively* to cdsp.mbn<->fastrpc_shell version mismatch in README.md:105-111, blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md:41-42 and package/qcom-dsp-shell/Config.in:16, never to SPI firmware — so an under-version board will send us re-pinning DSP blobs. (d) `dmidecode` is not actually needed: DMI/DMIID are already compiled in (arm64 defconfig via BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y at nerves_defconfig:40 + CONFIG_EFI=y at fragment:11…

**Why it matters:** This is a boot-chain prerequisite we cannot detect or fix from our image today. An under-version board will boot but the NPU (the entire point of this system per GOAL.md) will fail with 0x80000600, and the failure will look like a blob/version mismatch rather than a firmware problem. Worse, the recovery path we document requires physically re-cabling into EDL mode, whereas the proven system upgrades in place over the running OS. Note the terminology trap: the "unsigned" in "unsigned DSP protection domains" refers to Hexagon PD signing, NOT UEFI Secure Boot — this is very likely the second source of the user's "signature" memory.

**Action:** Do first (cheap, zero risk, fixes the real trap): 1. Record the floor and the ambiguity. Add to README.md next to the existing 0x80000600 paragraph (README.md:105-111), to blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md:41-42, and to package/qcom-dsp-shell/Config.in:16: "0x80000600 has TWO causes — cdsp.mbn/fastrpc_shell version mismatch, OR SPI boot firmware older than 6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1 (older UEFI makes the DSP reject unsigned Hexagon protection domains). Check `cat /sys/class/dmi/id/bios_version` before touching the blobs." Fill GOAL.md:103 and BRINGUP.md:106 with the required string instead of "latest Radxa snapshot"; keep the Observed column blank. 2. Boot-time warning in rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh (before the fastrpc daemons start, after step 1's udev coldplug): read /sys/class/dmi/id/bios_version and print a loud line if it is not 6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1 or newer; wrap in `|| true` like the rest of the script so it can never block the app. No dmidecode required. 3. BRINGUP.md: keep the host-EDL section but bump edl-ng to a current release, add the floor, add "read the current version with `cat /sys/class/dmi/id/bios_version`", and add the fastrpc_test -a v68 acceptance check from radxa-q6a-yocto/README.md:90-97. Optional, and a decision to surface rather than a task: 4. BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE=y in nerves_defconfig is valid (buildroot-2026.05, `depends on BR2_aarch64`) but buys nothing over sysfs. Skip unless you want SMBIOS field dumps. Do NOT add CONFIG_DMI/CONFIG_DMIID to linux-dragon-q6a.fragment — already on by default. 5. Before proposing package/edl-ng + package/q6a-spi-firmware, resolve three unknowns the Yocto recipe hides behind INSANE_SKIP: (i) does the .NET self-contained edl-ng bundle run on our minimal glibc rootfs — needs BR2_INSTALL_LIBSTDCPP, probably BR2_PACKAGE_ICU unless published InvariantGlobalization, and a writable DOTNET_BUNDLE_EXTRACT_BASE_DIR (our /tmp is tmpfs); (ii) the unzipped spinor payload size — it lands in BOTH rootfs.a and rootfs.b (fwup.conf:65-67, 2 GiB each) and inflates every OTA .fw for a once-per-board tool; (iii) licence — use `Q6A_SPI_FIRMWARE_LICENSE = PROPRIETARY` and `Q6A_SPI_FIRMWARE_REDISTRIBUTE = NO`, and confirm redistribution of dl.radxa.com content is acceptable. If any of those fails, the /data-side or host-side flash plus the boot-time warning is the right answer and items 1-3 already cover it. The Yocto helper itself is plain POSIX sh, so if you do port it the script is a 1:1 copy to rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/q6a-spi-flash with the same TARGET_VERSION a…

**Uncertainty:** I did not confirm that the arm64 defconfig we build on top of enables CONFIG_DMI/CONFIG_DMIID, so `dmidecode` availability on our image is unverified. I also did not verify that on-device flashing through /dev/mtd0 works with our kernel's QSPI NOR driver (the Yocto system proves it works with theirs).

### [minor] Buildroot has no aarch64 systemd-boot — the proven system's bootloader is not a config flip for us  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Quotes are accurate. /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image.bb lines 3-5 are literally `PACKAGE_INSTALL = " \` / ` systemd-boot \` / `"`. loader.conf is exactly the four lines `default q6a-a.conf` / `timeout 3` / `console-mode keep` / `editor no`. q6a-boot-select:54-55 and :85 do call `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default`/`set-oneshot`, which are EFI-variable operations (LoaderEntryDefault / LoaderEntryOneShot). MISSING CONTEXT THAT CHANGES THE RISK CASE: the claim frames systemd-boot as the hedge against "GRUB failing the LoadImage/MeasureBoot test". The Yocto tree's own record (commit 3bb7a7d "Boot via Type 1 entries (raw Image+initramfs): UKI breaks EDK2 MeasureBoot", and the comment in meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image.bbappend) says the failure was specific to a 47 MB UKI PE image measured by firmware LoadImage: "the board's EDK2 MeasureBoot/TrEE fails to measure the 47MB UKI PE image and aborts boot (hang + watchdog reset). systemd-boot loads raw Images with its own loader, no firmware PE measuremen…

**Ours today:** Two of the three "our side" assertions hold; the third is wrong. HOLDS: BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_BOOT is unreachable for us. Verified in deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/systemd/Config.in — line 113 `config BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_BOOT`, 115 `depends on BR2_i386 || BR2_x86_64`, 117 `select BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_EFI`, 160-164 `BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_BOOT_EFI_ARCH` with only ia32/x64 defaults. nerves_defconfig:21 is `BR2_INIT_NONE=y`. Grep of the whole tree for systemd-boot/bootctl/bootaa64 (excluding deps/.git) returns only post-build.sh:38 and fwup.conf:97,98,206 — all GRUB's bootaa64.efi. So yes: no Buildroot-built systemd-boot. IMPRECISE: the claim says `select BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_EFI` is "i.e. the systemd package itself". It is not — BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_EFI (line 166) is a sub-option "enable EFI support". The actual gate is line 108 `if BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD` combined with line 18 `depends on BR2_INIT_SYSTEMD`. Conclusion survives; the cited mechanism is wrong. WRONG: "systemd-boot's per-boot slot selection … depends on EFI variables … which we have no analogue for." We already hav…

**Why it matters:** This is the fallback plan if GRUB fails the LoadImage/MeasureBoot test on hardware, and it is not cheap — it needs a new out-of-tree package. Worse, systemd-boot's per-boot slot selection in the proven system depends on EFI variables written by `bootctl set-oneshot`/`set-default` from a systemd unit (q6a-boot-select:54-55,85), which we have no analogue for. Surfacing the cost now prevents discovering it under bench pressure.

**Action:** Keep GRUB. Replace the claim's escape-hatch plan with the following, and note that a fallback direction is ALREADY recorded (BRINGUP.md:79-82 — "fall back to keeping Radxa's embloader on the ESP and generating BLS entries (documented fallback, not yet implemented)"). Concretize it in that same bullet: Preferred hatch #1 (cheapest, already noted): Radxa's own embloader/extlinux already present on the vendor ESP + BLS/extlinux entries. No new Buildroot package. This is the path the vendor image uses (referenced by Yocto commit 3bb7a7d). Preferred hatch #2: stay on GRUB but move to Type 1 (BLS) entries — add `blscfg` to BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI (nerves_defconfig:58, currently "boot linux fdt ext2 fat squash4 part_gpt part_msdos normal efi_gop loadenv echo test true search configfile regexp probe"). Also cheap; no new package. Hatch #3 (only if 1 and 2 fail) — vendored systemd-boot: (a) package/systemd-boot-efi/{Config.in,systemd-boot-efi.mk} as a BR2 generic-package vendoring a pinned arm64 systemd-bootaa64.efi, registered in Config.in + external.mk. Mechanism is valid Buildroot (no invented BR2_ symbol), and it sidesteps BR2_PACKAGE_SYSTEMD_BOOT entirely — correct, since that symbol is unreachable for us. (b) Grow BOOT_PART_COUNT (fwup.conf:62) — but note the claim's "shift every downstream offset" is FALSE: fwup.conf:64-71 already derives ROOTFS_A/B and APP offsets with define-eval, so one edit propagates. Size to 1048576 blocks (512 MiB) to match the proven system (q6a-ab.wks.in: `part esp ... --fixed-size 512M`), not 262144 (128 MiB) — two raw kernels plus headroom, and the proven system needed 512 MiB. (c) The real design cost the claim understates: moving kernels into the ESP breaks the atomic kernel+DTB+rootfs coupling documented at fwup.conf:5-9 and grub.cfg:10-13, and Type 1 entries have no `devicetree` directive, so we would inherit the Yocto assumption "rely on EFI provided one" (esp-qcom-image.bb:17-18 `KERNEL_DEVICETREE = ""`). Surface those two, not the watchdog. (d) DROP the claim's item (d). systemd-boot's Automatic Boot Assessment is filename-based on the ESP: an entry named `q6a-a+3.conf` is renamed by the boot loader itself to `q6a-a+2-1.conf` … `q6a-a+0-3.conf`, and a zero-tries-left entry is deprioritized/skipped. No EFI variables, no bootctl, no systemd. fwup can ship `q6a-a+3.conf` as a file-resource via fat_write, and a rootfs_overlay validate script can `mv` it back to `q6a-a.conf` on success. So bootloader-side boot-once/auto-fallback is NOT lost and no hardware watchdog is required.

**Uncertainty:** I did not verify that a Debian-built systemd-bootaa64.efi runs correctly against Radxa's EDK2, nor whether it needs the matching systemd version's loader features. Also unverified: whether the Q6A EDK2 exposes writable EFI variables at all (the Yocto system's bootctl calls imply yes, but nothing in the tree proves the writes succeed).

### [minor] No ESP FAT repair at boot — the proven system needed it after hard power cuts and watchdog resets  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** q6a-esp-fsck (added in 3fa9710, fixed in 04756e8) runs on every boot before the ESP is mounted: q6a-esp-fsck.service has DefaultDependencies=no, After=local-fs-pre.target, Before=local-fs.target boot.mount, ConditionPathExists=/dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP, TimeoutStartSec=60, and ExecStart=/bin/sh -c 'umount /boot 2>/dev/null; fsck.vfat -a /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP || true'. The recipe RDEPENDS on dosfstools and is SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE=enable. The recipe DESCRIPTION states the reason outright: "hard power cuts and watchdog resets leave the FAT dirty", with safeguards so a pathological filesystem never blocks boot.

**Ours today:** Confirmed absent. nerves_defconfig:59 has only BR2_PACKAGE_HOST_DOSFSTOOLS=y (host-side, used by the image build); BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS is unset, so no /sbin/fsck.vfat on target. Nothing in package/ (aic8800, qairt-runtime, qcom-dsp-firmware, qcom-dsp-shell, qcom-fastrpc) or rootfs_overlay/ (7 files) does FAT maintenance; qcom-coldplug.sh:21-25 goes straight to fwup reconcile/autovalidate. Correction to the exposure analysis: grub.cfg:56,60 save_env rewrites the preallocated 1 KiB grubenv block IN PLACE and does not mutate FAT allocation metadata, so it is not a per-boot FAT-dirtying operation. The metadata-mutating writers are fwup's fat_mkfs/fat_mkdir (fwup.conf:174-176, install only) and fat_write (fwup.conf:206-208,283,343; fwup-ops.conf:162,182,253,263,325,337), i.e. install and OTA/A-B ops only. Also note our GPT partition 0 is named "boot" (fwup.conf:132), not "ESP", so there is no /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP analogue; and udev is not running yet at the point where the repair would be inserted, so only erlinit's /dev/rootdisk* symlinks are available. The ESP is genuinely neve…

**Why it matters:** Our A/B rollback depends on GRUB reading and writing grubenv on that FAT. A dirty or corrupted FAT breaks slot selection and can make fwup's fat_write fail, which would silently break OTA slot switching (fwup-ops autovalidate is called with `|| true` in coldplug, so failures are swallowed). Because this board is watchdog-reset-prone during bring-up (that is literally how the UKI failure manifested on the Yocto side), the dirty-FAT scenario is not hypothetical.

**Action:** 1) nerves_defconfig: add BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS=y and BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS_FSCK_FAT=y (both verified in deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/dosfstools/Config.in; BR2_USE_WCHAR dependency is satisfied by our glibc toolchain). Keep BR2_PACKAGE_HOST_DOSFSTOOLS=y. dosfstools.mk installs /sbin/fsck.fat plus symlinks fsck.vfat/fsck.msdos/dosfsck, so /sbin/fsck.vfat is the correct path. Do NOT enable MKFS_FAT/FATLABEL — not needed. 2) busybox.fragment: add CONFIG_TIMEOUT=y. This is REQUIRED and was missing from the original proposal — nerves-common busybox.config:339 has "# CONFIG_TIMEOUT is not set", so `timeout` does not exist on target and the proposed command would exit 127, get swallowed by `|| true`, and silently never fsck. 3) rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh: insert BEFORE the existing block at lines 21-25 (correct placement — it precedes the udevd start at line 27, and precedes fwup's ESP writes), written so it still works if `timeout` is absent: # ESP (FAT) repair. Structural FAT damage from a power cut during an # fwup fat_write would break GRUB's grubenv A/B state and further # fat_writes. Auto-repair, hard time limit, failures never propagate. # (Mirrors Yocto q6a-esp-fsck.service; the ESP is never mounted here.) ESP_DEV=/dev/rootdisk0p1 if [ -b "$ESP_DEV" ] && [ -x /sbin/fsck.vfat ]; then if [ -x /usr/bin/timeout ] || [ -x /bin/timeout ]; then timeout 60 /sbin/fsck.vfat -a "$ESP_DEV" || true else /sbin/fsck.vfat -a "$ESP_DEV" || true fi fi 4) VERIFY ON TARGET (do not assume): that erlinit v1.15.1 actually creates /dev/rootdisk0p1. Only p2/p3/p4 are referenced anywhere in our tree (erlinit.config -m, fwup-ops.conf require-path-on-device). Because of the `[ -b ]` guard, a wrong device path degrades to a silent no-op — so add a boot-log check, or resolve the ESP by scanning for the partition whose offset is 2048 if p1 turns out not to be symlinked. 5) BRINGUP.md bench test: repeated hard power cuts (~20) with at least one during an OTA fwup fat_write, then confirm `fsck.vfat -n /dev/rootdisk0p1` reports clean and that A/B still switches and rolls back.

**Uncertainty:** Our exposure is genuinely lower than the Yocto system's because we never mount the ESP read-write from Linux, and GRUB's save_env rewrites a preallocated 1 KiB block in place without touching FAT allocation metadata. I did not measure how often a real dirty-FAT event would actually occur in our design.

### [minor] Kernel command line diverges from the proven one: no earlycon, no console=tty1, and permanent bring-up hacks  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Final, proven options line (q6a-a.conf:4): `root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0`. `earlycon` was added deliberately during bring-up (commit dfbf13c "Add earlycon to boot cmdline"). `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` were added as a display bring-up hack (commit 0e2cb2b: "Linux disables 'unused' MDSS clocks and the display power domain mid-boot, killing the EDK2 GOP pipeline efifb draws on") and then REMOVED once the display pipeline was built in (commit c84ddad: "Bring-up leftover... Vendor-equivalent 6.18 kernel needs none of these"), along with module_blacklist=msm,phy_qcom_qmp_combo. The shipped cmdline carries neither.

**Ours today:** grub.cfg:75 sets `console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused loglevel=4 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro`. So: no `earlycon`; no `console=tty1`; `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` permanently present with the comment at grub.cfg:72-74 calling them "bring-up safety nets ... revisit once the driver set is complete"; `loglevel=4` suppresses KERN_INFO; no `net.ifnames=0`. (Our QEMU variant test/grub.cfg.qemu:38 does have earlycon and loglevel=7, so the emulated path is better instrumented than the real one.)

**Why it matters:** Without `earlycon` there is no kernel output between GRUB's handoff and the GENI UART driver probing — which is precisely the window where a first-boot failure on unfamiliar hardware will land, leaving a black serial console and no way to distinguish "kernel never started" from "kernel died early". Without `console=tty1` there is no HDMI fallback if the serial console is not wired or EDK2 does not mirror ConOut to the UART. `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` mask real clock/power-domain bugs and were proven unnecessary on the same SoC with a 6.18 kernel; keeping them permanently means we will ship with the diagnosis suppressed. loglevel=4 hides the remoteproc/fastrpc probe messages the acc…

**Action:** Edit /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg: delete only lines 72-74 (the clk_ignore_unused/pd_ignore_unused "bring-up safety nets" comment), keep line 71, and set line 75 to: set nerves_cmdline="console=tty1 console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon loglevel=7 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro" Ordering is load-bearing and differs deliberately from Yocto: the LAST console= becomes /dev/console, and with BR2_INIT_NONE erlinit is PID 1 — its pre-`-c` output and its --warn-unused-tty behavior follow /dev/console, so ttyMSM0 must come last or early erlinit messages land on HDMI instead of the debug UART (erlinit.config:12 `-c ttyMSM0` only covers the IEx prompt). Comment to record: earlycon and console=tty1 match the proven cmdline in meta-q6a-nerveshub/.../q6a-a.conf:4 (earlycon added by git dfbf13c); clk_ignore_unused/pd_ignore_unused were tried on the proven system (0e2cb2b) and removed once the display pipeline was built in (9c9498f, c84ddad) — do not reintroduce without evidence. Keep loglevel=7 until on-board bring-up is complete. Do NOT justify loglevel=7 by the BRINGUP.md acceptance test: loglevel gates console printing only, and BRINGUP.md:50/:91 read `dmesg`, which is unaffected. Justify it by wanting KERN_WARNING/KERN_INFO on the serial console during first boot. net.ifnames=0: optional parity only. Predictable renaming comes from systemd-udevd's net_setup_link/net_id builtin, which Buildroot eudev (nerves_defconfig:26) does not ship, so eth0 is already the name README.md:118 and vintage_net_ethernet expect. Add it if you want byte-for-byte parity with the proven line; it changes nothing functionally. Optional follow-up before relying on console=tty1 for HDMI fallback: confirm the arm64 arch defconfig (nerves_defconfig:40) supplies CONFIG_VT_CONSOLE / CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE / CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM, since linux-dragon-q6a.fragment sets none of them and has DRM_MSM=m (line 219). If absent, tty1 is silently ignored (harmless, but no HDMI console).

**Uncertainty:** Whether the Q6A's `earlycon` (no arguments) resolves correctly depends on the firmware-provided DTB's stdout-path/SPCR — it should, since the proven system relies on exactly that, but it is only proven in combination with the firmware DTB (which reinforces the DTB finding). The net.ifnames question belongs to the networking domain; I did not verify eudev's rule set.

### [minor] Our QEMU test cannot exercise any of the real boot-chain risks (no measured boot, no DTB handoff)  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** test/qemu-boot-test.py has the SAME Secure-Boot/TPM blind spots but is a poorer analogy than claimed. Confirmed: lines 104-106 create a fresh varstore each run (`with open(vars_fd, "wb") as f: # fresh vars each run` / `f.truncate(64 * 1024 * 1024)`), so no PK/KEK/db is ever enrolled and Secure Boot is never active; edk2_code() (lines 32-43) picks the plain edk2-aarch64-code.fd / AAVMF_CODE.fd, not a secure-boot variant. But patch_loader_entry() (lines 65-77) only APPENDS to the shipped cmdline (`re.sub(r"(?m)^(options\s+.*)$", r"\1 console=ttyAMA0,115200", text)`) — the rest of the real `options` line is preserved, so Yocto's tested cmdline is much closer to shipped than ours. And Yocto has NO DTB-handoff code to test: meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image/q6a-a.conf is title/linux/initrd/options only, no `devicetree` line — the board's SPI-NOR EDK2 supplies the DTB. The DTB blind spot is therefore ours alone, not shared. Nothing in either tree documents the Secure-Boot/measured-boot gaps; the only "smoke test" wording is radxa-q6a-yocto/README.md:25.

**Ours today:** Partly already documented; the real residue is narrower. Confirmed: test/qemu-smoke.sh:126-127 makes a fresh 64 MiB `edk2-vars.fd` ("fresh each run so grubenv/boot state is clean"), and line 113 mcopies test/grub.cfg.qemu over ::/EFI/BOOT/grub.cfg. NEW and worth adding: test/qemu-rollback.sh:165 does the same mcopy, so NEITHER of our two automated tests ever executes grub.cfg:83/:88 (`devicetree ($nerves_disk,gptN)$nerves_dtb`). Emulation gap confirmed in the log, at corrected line numbers: qemu-run.trimmed.log:27-29 `Tpm2SubmitCommand - Tcg2 - Not Found` / `Tpm2GetCapabilityPcrs fail!`; line 40 `ACPI: RSDP 0x00000000BCB43018`; line 51 `ACPI: SPCR: console: pl011,mmio32,0x9000000,9600`; line 84 the QEMU cmdline. However the DTB caveat is NOT undocumented — it is stated in test/qemu-smoke.sh:11-12 ("We swap in a QEMU grub.cfg (ttyAMA0, no board DTB)"), test/grub.cfg.qemu:5-8, README.md:166, and README.md:174-176 already carries an explicit "What it proves … What it can't prove" paragraph; GOAL.md:37 flags "(Loaded by GRUB `devicetree` on HW.)" and GOAL.md:22-24 + 62-68 scope the headl…

**Why it matters:** Both of our top two blockers (LoadImage/MeasureBoot on a 39 MiB Image, and the DTB override) are structurally untestable in the current harness, yet GOAL.md:48-60 presents the QEMU pass as the headline success gate. That creates false confidence: 5/5 checks green tells us nothing about whether this board's EDK2 will load our kernel or which DTB the kernel ends up with.

**Action:** 1) Add the two genuinely undocumented caveats to the existing "What it can't prove" list at README.md:174-176 (which currently names only the Hexagon DSP) and to the header of test/qemu-smoke.sh: (a) Secure Boot is never active — test/qemu-smoke.sh:126-127 truncates a fresh empty `edk2-vars.fd` every run, so no PK/KEK/db is enrolled; (b) measured boot never runs — `-M virt` has no TPM (qemu-run.trimmed.log:27-29). Do NOT re-document the DTB substitution; it is already in test/qemu-smoke.sh:11-12, test/grub.cfg.qemu:5-8, README.md:166 and GOAL.md:37. Optionally add one line to GOAL.md next to line 48-52 pointing at README.md:174-176 rather than duplicating the list. 2) Note in test/grub.cfg.qemu that BOTH test/qemu-smoke.sh:113 and test/qemu-rollback.sh:165 install it, so the real grub.cfg is executed by zero automated tests. 3) Reduce cmdline drift, but accurately: grub.cfg:75 is `console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused loglevel=4 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro` while test/grub.cfg.qemu:38 is `console=ttyAMA0,115200 earlycon loglevel=7 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro` — four differences, not one. If test/grub.cfg.qemu is generated from grub.cfg by sed, the substitution must be explicit about all four (rewrite console=, keep or intentionally drop clk_ignore_unused/pd_ignore_unused, and decide on earlycon/loglevel) — a console-only sed silently changes two more things. 4) DTB coverage, corrected recipe: `-dtb` is wrong on `-M virt` (QEMU generates its own DTB and will not take the qcs6490 one). Use `-M virt,acpi=off` so EDK2 hands the OS QEMU's DT instead of ACPI; to exercise GRUB's `devicetree` command itself, first `-M virt,dumpdtb=virt.dtb`, mcopy virt.dtb into the image, and add a `devicetree` line to test/grub.cfg.qemu. CONFIG_VIRTIO_MMIO=y already exists (linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:126) so DT-mode virtio enumeration works. This is the one proposal with real value; it converts "GRUB can install a DTB at all" into a testable claim. 5) DROP the swtpm scenario, or demote it to a footnote. The Yocto commit that motivates the MeasureBoot worry (3bb7a7d, "UKI breaks EDK2 MeasureBoot") says the board's EDK2 failed to measure "the 47MB UKI **PE image**" — firmware PE measurement via LoadImage. Our chain LoadImages only BOOTAA64.EFI (831488 bytes, qemu-run.trimmed.log:15); the 39 MiB /boot/Image is loaded by GRUB's own arm64 `linux` loader and never passes through LoadImage/MeasureBoot. An upstream-EDK2 swtpm run would exercise neither Qualcomm's TrEE nor a code path we use. 6) BRINGUP.md: lines 78 already state the first two expected obser…

**Uncertainty:** Whether a swtpm-backed QEMU EDK2 would reproduce the Radxa EDK2's specific MeasureBoot failure is unknown — the bug is in the board's firmware build, so a negative result in QEMU would not clear the risk. It would still catch gross PE-measurement problems.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/test/qemu-boot-test.py:32-43,67-77,104-108 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/qemu-smoke.sh:118-128,145-159,199-207 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/grub.cfg.qemu:1-10,38 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/qemu-run.trimmed.log:24-26,37-40,84 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/GOAL.md:48-60</sub>


### [minor] Our 32 MiB ESP is fine today but blocks the systemd-boot fallback; Yocto needs 512 MiB because kernels live there  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** ESP is 512 MiB (q6a-ab.wks.in:5 `--fixed-size 512M`, mirrored in fwup/radxa-dragon-q6a.fwup:15-16 as ESP_PART_OFFSET=2048 / ESP_PART_COUNT=1048576, and esp-qcom-common.inc:14-17 pins IMAGE_ROOTFS_SIZE/MAXSIZE=524288). It must be that large because it holds both slots' boot payloads: /EFI/BOOT/bootaa64.efi, /EFI/Linux/q6a-a.Image + q6a-a.cpio.gz shipped in the factory image (esp-qcom-image.bbappend:27-29) and q6a-b.Image + q6a-b.cpio.gz written by OTA (fwup file lines 141-149,199-208 fat_write into EFI/Linux/), plus /loader/loader.conf and /loader/entries/q6a-{a,b}.conf. On upgrade the ESP is deliberately NOT rewritten ("ESP not rewritten on upgrade; per-slot kernel/initrd are fat_write'n instead").

**Ours today:** ESP is 32 MiB (fwup.conf:61-62 BOOT_PART_OFFSET=2048, BOOT_PART_COUNT=65536) and holds only /EFI/BOOT/{BOOTAA64.EFI, grub.cfg, grubenv} — 833,778 bytes total per the QEMU run's mdir listing, leaving ~31 MiB free. Kernel and DTB live inside each slot's squashfs, so they upgrade atomically with the rootfs and never touch the ESP.

**Why it matters:** Our design is strictly better for OTA atomicity (one raw_write per slot switches kernel+DTB+rootfs together; the Yocto side must fat_write two files into a shared FAT and can be left inconsistent if interrupted). But it hard-codes the assumption that the bootloader reads the kernel out of the rootfs — which only GRUB can do. If the GRUB LoadImage risk forces us to systemd-boot Type 1 entries, 32 MiB cannot hold two ~40 MB kernels and the entire partition table shifts, invalidating every offset in fwup.conf, fwup-ops.conf and install-to-disk.sh.

**Action:** Keep 32 MiB for now — do not grow it speculatively, since every offset change invalidates existing installed devices. Instead: (1) add a comment at fwup.conf:61-62 recording WHY 32 MiB is sufficient (GRUB reads the kernel from squashfs) and what would have to change if the loader ever changes (>=128 MiB, all downstream offsets shift, fwup-ops.conf must be updated in lockstep — the file already warns "It's critical that this is kept in sync with the main fwup.conf"); (2) note the same in the systemd-boot escape-hatch section of BRINGUP.md. If a bench test forces the switch, change BOOT_PART_COUNT to 262144 (128 MiB) in both fwup.conf and fwup-ops.conf together and re-derive ROOTFS_A_PART_OFFSET etc. from the define-eval chain (which already computes them, so only the one constant changes).

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/files/wic/q6a-ab.wks.in:5 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/fwup/radxa-dragon-q6a.fwup:14-24,137-149,195-208 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-common.inc:6-17 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image.bbappend:26-34 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf:41-71 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/qemu-run.trimmed.log:11-19</sub>


### [minor] ESP FAT sector size is hard-coded to 512 by fwup — our "boots from microSD, UFS or USB" claim is wrong for 4Kn media  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The sector size is an explicit, per-media machine variable: radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:52-53 `# SD card uses 512-byte sectors; override to 4096 for UFS targets` / `QCOM_VFAT_SECTOR_SIZE ?= "512"`, consumed at meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-common.inc:12 as `EXTRA_IMAGECMD:vfat += " -S ${QCOM_VFAT_SECTOR_SIZE}"` (mkfs.vfat -S). The shipped image is therefore SD-only, and the layer documents what to change for UFS.

**Ours today:** fwup.conf:175 `fat_mkfs(${BOOT_PART_OFFSET}, ${BOOT_PART_COUNT})` creates the FAT with fwup's built-in FatFs, which uses 512-byte logical sectors; there is no sector-size parameter. Meanwhile README.md:40-46 claims "The kernel boots with root=PARTUUID=... probed from whichever disk GRUB was loaded from, so one image works from microSD, UFS or USB", and grub.cfg:19-24 makes the same claim.

**Why it matters:** EDK2's FAT driver requires the BPB BytesPerSector to match the block device's logical block size. On a UFS or NVMe device that reports 4096-byte logical blocks, a 512-byte-sector FAT is unreadable by the firmware, so the ESP would not be found and the board would not boot from that medium at all — while the same image works perfectly from SD. Our documentation currently promises otherwise, which will cost a debugging session.

**Action:** 1) Correct README.md:40-46 and the grub.cfg:19-24 comment: the image is portable across media only where the logical block size is 512 bytes (microSD, USB mass storage, and 512e NVMe). Add: booting from a 4 KiB-logical-block UFS/NVMe requires an ESP formatted with 4096-byte sectors, which fwup's fat_mkfs cannot produce. 2) If UFS boot becomes a requirement, the options are (a) build the 32 MiB ESP image out-of-band with `mkfs.vfat -S 4096` in post-build.sh and ship it as a fwup file-resource raw_write'n at BOOT_PART_OFFSET (mirroring exactly what the Yocto side does with its rawcopy'd esp vfat), or (b) file/carry a fwup patch adding a sector-size argument to fat_mkfs. Record option (a) as the intended path since it needs no upstream change. 3) Verify the actual logical block size on the target's UFS before doing any of this (`cat /sys/block/sd*/queue/logical_block_size`) and record it in BRINGUP.md's observed-versions table.

**Uncertainty:** I did not confirm that the Q6A's onboard UFS reports 4096-byte logical blocks — the Yocto comment implies it can, but the shipped configuration is 512 (SD). Also unverified: whether Radxa's EDK2 FAT driver is strict about BytesPerSector matching the media block size (standard EDK2 FatPkg is).

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:52-53 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-common.inc:8-12 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf:175-177 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/README.md:40-46 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:19-24</sub>


### [minor] ESP partition naming and the legacy boot flag differ from the proven layout  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** q6a-ab.wks.in:5 gives the ESP `--label=ESP` with GPT part-type C12A7328-F81F-11D2-BA4B-00A0C93EC93B and no `--active` flag (note the canned efi-uki-bootdisk.wks.in:2 does use `--active`, but that file is not the one used). Slot devices are then addressed by partlabel throughout the runtime: /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP in q6a-esp-fsck.service:6,14; root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a|b in the loader entries; /dev/disk/by-partlabel/data in the fwup file. q6a-boot-select mounts the ESP as `${disk}p1` (line 45).

**Ours today:** fwup.conf:126-133 names p1 "boot" (not "ESP"), uses the same ESP type GUID c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b, and additionally sets `boot = true` (the legacy active/bootable flag in the protective MBR). Rootfs slots are named "rootfs.a"/"rootfs.b" and addressed by PARTUUID probed at boot (grub.cfg:81,86), not by partlabel. Partition GUIDs are fixed constants (fwup.conf:79-85) with a documented requirement that every medium in the same machine be burned with unique GUIDs.

**Why it matters:** Low risk, but worth knowing before bench debugging: (a) the proven system boots without the legacy active flag, so `boot = true` is not a requirement of this firmware — if we ever suspect the flag confuses EDK2's ESP discovery we know it can simply be dropped; (b) any script or note copied from the Yocto side that references /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP will not resolve on our image, because our label is "boot" — this matters for the fsck snippet in the ESP-repair finding, which is why I specified /dev/rootdisk0p1 there instead; (c) our PARTUUID-probing approach removes the need for partlabel lookups entirely, at the cost of requiring unique GUIDs per medium.

**Action:** No change required. Two documentation touch-ups: (1) note in fwup.conf near the partition 0 block that the proven Yocto image sets no legacy active flag and still boots, so `boot = true` is belt-and-braces rather than a firmware requirement; (2) when porting anything from the Yocto layer, translate /dev/disk/by-partlabel/{ESP,rootfs-a,rootfs-b,data} to our equivalents (/dev/rootdisk0p1..p4, or the "boot"/"rootfs.a"/"rootfs.b"/"app" labels) — the erlinit-provided /dev/rootdisk0pN symlinks are the stable choice since they follow whichever disk actually supplied the rootfs. Optionally rename p1 to "ESP" for parity, but only as part of a change that already shifts the layout, since renaming alone gains nothing.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/files/wic/q6a-ab.wks.in:5-9 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/oe-core/meta/files/wic/efi-uki-bootdisk.wks.in:2 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-esp-fsck/files/q6a-esp-fsck.service:6,14 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select:42-47 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf:73-85,126-155 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:64-69,81,86</sub>


### [minor] GRUB console output on this board depends entirely on EDK2 ConOut — no usable GRUB serial module exists for the GENI UART  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** systemd-boot's loader.conf sets `console-mode keep` (do not change the firmware's console mode), and the kernel cmdline carries both `console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon` and `console=tty1`, so the bring-up path had two independent output channels: the firmware's serial console and HDMI/efifb. Commits 433a8dd/0d62656/5eda71b show the team explicitly boot to a tty1 getty on HDMI during bring-up, i.e. they relied on the framebuffer console when serial was not enough.

**Ours today:** nerves_defconfig:58 builds GRUB with `boot linux fdt ext2 fat squash4 part_gpt part_msdos normal efi_gop loadenv echo test true search configfile regexp probe` — no `serial`/`terminal_output` modules, so GRUB's messages (including grub.cfg:57 "Slot was not validated..." and :82/:87 "Booting slot A/B") go only to the EFI Simple Text Output protocol. GRUB's own `serial` module would not help anyway: it drives ns16550-compatible UARTs, and the Q6A debug UART is a Qualcomm GENI SE (uart5 -> ttyMSM0), not 8250-compatible. Our kernel cmdline has neither earlycon nor console=tty1 (see the cmdline finding), and efi_gop is built in but nothing configures a GRUB graphical terminal.

**Why it matters:** If Radxa's EDK2 does not mirror ConOut to the debug UART, GRUB will run completely silently and a boot failure at the GRUB stage will be indistinguishable from the firmware never loading BOOTAA64.EFI. BRINGUP.md:10-12 assumes the XBL/UEFI log is visible on ttyMSM0, which implies ConOut does reach the UART — but that is an assumption, and it is the only diagnostic channel GRUB has.

**Action:** 1) Add to BRINGUP.md §0 an explicit first check: confirm that EDK2's own menu/ConOut text (not just the earlier XBL log) appears on ttyMSM0 — if only XBL output appears, GRUB will be silent and HDMI becomes the primary bring-up display. 2) Do NOT add GRUB's `serial` module to BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI; it cannot drive a GENI UART and would only add noise. 3) Ensure the HDMI fallback actually works by adding `console=tty1` to the kernel cmdline (already recommended in the cmdline finding) so that even if GRUB is silent, the kernel is not. 4) Consider adding `all_video` to the builtin module list alongside efi_gop so GRUB can put text on HDMI if the firmware's ConOut does not already include the GOP; keep `echo` (already present) since grub.cfg relies on it for the slot messages.

**Uncertainty:** I could not confirm whether Radxa's EDK2 ConOut includes the GENI serial terminal. The fact that the Yocto team debugged extensively over serial suggests it does, but their kernel had earlycon so their evidence may all be post-handoff.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/nerves_defconfig:57-58 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:57,82,87 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image/loader.conf:1-4 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image/q6a-a.conf:4 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto git shas 433a8dd, 0d62656 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/BRINGUP.md:8-12</sub>


### [info] There is no UEFI Secure Boot signature requirement — the proven system signs nothing  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Nothing in the boot chain is signed. oe-core/meta/classes-recipe/uki.bbclass:85-87 leaves UKI_SB_KEY/UKI_SB_CERT commented out, and lines 181-187 only add `--sign-kernel --secureboot-private-key`/`--secureboot-certificate` to the ukify command when those vars are set. A grep for UKI_SB / SECURE_BOOT / secureboot / sbsign / MOK / shim across meta-q6a-nerveshub, kas/ and README.md returns nothing at all. The bootloader is oe-core's stock systemd-boot installed unsigned at /EFI/BOOT/bootaa64.efi (EFI_PROVIDER ?= "systemd-boot" in meta-qcom/conf/machine/include/qcom-common.inc:65; EFIDIR ?= "/EFI/BOOT" and EFI_BOOT_IMAGE ?= "boot${EFI_ARCH}.efi" in oe-core/meta/conf/image-uefi.conf:2,26; esp-qcom-image.bb's setup_efi_folder moves /boot/EFI/* to /EFI). No shim, no MOK, no db/KEK/PK enrolment step exists. This unsigned image boots on real hardware. Signature enforcement on this SoC is strictly below UEFI: PBL/QFPROM verifies Qualcomm-signed XBL/ABL/TZ/HYP/UEFI in SPI NOR (cf. the ABL_SIGNATURE_VERSION handling at meta-qcom/classes-recipe/image_types_qcom.bbclass:187), and that whole layer …

**Ours today:** We also sign nothing: fwup.conf:97-99 ships `${NERVES_SYSTEM}/images/bootaa64.efi` (Buildroot's plain grub2 arm64-efi output, copied verbatim in post-build.sh:38) and fwup.conf:206 writes it to /EFI/BOOT/BOOTAA64.EFI. Our docs already assume Secure Boot is off but frame it as an unverified prerequisite: README.md:58-59 "Secure Boot must be off in the UEFI setup menu: GRUB disables its `devicetree` command under Secure Boot", BRINGUP.md:80-83, GOAL.md:110.

**Why it matters:** This closes the user's stated highest-risk unknown: there is no signature to satisfy, no key to enrol, no shim to chainload. It also means the "Secure Boot must be off" warning in our docs is about GRUB's own lockdown behaviour, not about the firmware rejecting our binary — and once we drop the `devicetree` command (see the DTB finding) even that concern evaporates. Knowing this prevents wasted effort building a signing pipeline.

**Action:** 1) Replace the speculative wording in README.md:58-59, BRINGUP.md:80-83 and GOAL.md:110 with the verified statement: the board's EDK2 executes an unsigned \EFI\BOOT\BOOTAA64.EFI from removable media (proven by the Yocto system's unsigned systemd-boot booting on hardware); UEFI Secure Boot is not enrolled/enforced. Note that Qualcomm secure boot applies only to the SPI-NOR stages, which we never touch. 2) Add the one residual guard to BRINGUP.md: if someone ever enables Secure Boot in the EDK2 setup menu, GRUB enters lockdown (grub-2.12 grub-core/kern/efi/init.c:123-126 calls grub_lockdown() when grub_efi_get_secureboot() == GRUB_EFI_SECUREBOOT_MODE_ENABLED) and lockdown-registered commands vanish — check `echo $lockdown` at the GRUB prompt. 3) No signing infrastructure is needed; do not add sbsign/shim packages.

**Uncertainty:** I could not read the Radxa SPI-NOR firmware payload itself (it lives under the excluded build/ tree), so I cannot state whether that EDK2 build even compiles in SecureBootEnable, nor whether the setup menu exposes a Secure Boot toggle. The negative evidence (an unsigned PE boots) is conclusive for our purposes regardless.

### [info] The Yocto WKS/ESP configuration is contradictory — the machine conf's UKI layout is dead, the image recipe's A/B layout wins  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Two conflicting declarations exist. meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:44-50 sets `WKS_FILE = "efi-uki-bootdisk.wks.in"` and `QCOM_ESP_IMAGE = ""`, with the comment "Use the canned UEFI UKI WKS — it creates the ESP inline via the bootimg_efi plugin (systemd-boot + UKI)... No need for the standalone esp-qcom-image recipe." That canned file (oe-core/meta/files/wic/efi-uki-bootdisk.wks.in) is a 2-partition layout: `part /boot --source bootimg_efi --sourceparams="loader=${EFI_PROVIDER}"` + `part / --source rootfs`. But the image recipe overrides it: q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:10 `WKS_FILE = "q6a-ab.wks.in"` — recipe scope beats machine conf, so the real layout is the 5-partition A/B GPT (files/wic/q6a-ab.wks.in): p1 ESP 512 MiB rawcopy of esp-qcom-image-${MACHINE}.rootfs.vfat with part-type C12A7328-F81F-11D2-BA4B-00A0C93EC93B and label ESP, p2/p3 rootfs-a/b 3 GiB ext4, p4 bootenv 1 MiB rawcopy of uboot-env-q6a.bin, p5 data. The recipe also re-adds the dependency the machine conf claimed was unnecessary (q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:141 `do_image_wic[depends] += "esp-qcom-image:…

**Ours today:** Not applicable — we have one authoritative layout in fwup.conf:41-56,123-156 (p1 ESP 32 MiB at LBA 2048 with type c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b and name "boot"; p2/p3 rootfs 2 GiB squashfs; p4 app ext4 expanding; u-boot-env KV block at LBA 64 outside any partition). But this matters for how we read the reference: several of the Yocto files that look authoritative are not.

**Why it matters:** If we mine the Yocto tree for the "real" boot design we can easily copy the wrong thing — the machine conf reads as if the system boots a UKI from a 2-partition disk, and QCOM_DTB_DEFAULT looks meaningful there. Neither is true of the shipped image. Getting this straight is what makes the DTB and MeasureBoot findings above trustworthy.

**Action:** No code change. Record the resolved facts wherever we document the reference system (e.g. a short "reference system: what actually ships" note in BRINGUP.md or GOAL.md): the proven boot chain is SPI-NOR PBL -> XBL -> EDK2 -> \EFI\BOOT\bootaa64.efi (unsigned systemd-boot) -> Type 1 entry -> raw Image + initramfs, with the DTB supplied by EDK2; the effective disk layout is q6a-ab.wks.in, not efi-uki-bootdisk.wks.in; QCOM_DTB_DEFAULT and the machine conf's UKI comments are inert. Cite the file:line pairs so a future session does not re-derive it.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:1-10,43-57 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-products/images/q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:6-10,141 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/files/wic/q6a-ab.wks.in:1-9 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/oe-core/meta/files/wic/efi-uki-bootdisk.wks.in:1-3 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom/classes/uki-esp-image.bbclass:12-19 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf:41-56,123-156</sub>


### [info] A/B slot selection needs no EFI variables in our design — the proven system's mechanism has no Buildroot analogue and we should not port it  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Slot selection is split across systemd units and EFI variables. q6a-boot-select (a systemd oneshot, files q6a-boot-select.service + q6a-fw-validate.service) mounts the ESP at /boot and calls `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default "q6a-${active}.conf"` and `set-oneshot` (lines 54-55, 60, 85), i.e. it writes the LoaderEntryDefault / LoaderEntryOneShot UEFI variables; loader.conf's `default q6a-a.conf` is only the fallback. Validation waits up to ~6 minutes for nerves_hub_agent.service to come up, sleeps 30 s, then sets nerves_fw_validated=1 and makes the slot the default (lines 71-87). The retry/revert logic (lines 49-70) uses a q6a_boot_attempts counter in the u-boot-format KV block on p4. The image also installs efibootmgr (q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:71). Commits eef6a55/eabd2b1/be5f6d8 show repeated fixes to resolve the boot disk under udev timing, and 47bf/ccb29e0/330a601 show fw_env.config offset churn.

**Ours today:** All of that collapses into grub.cfg:27,48-62 plus fwup: GRUB reads grubenv, applies boot-once semantics itself, and writes back with save_env; fwup's upgrade tasks flip grubenv last (fwup.conf:263-284, 323-344) and fwup-ops.conf implements validate/revert/reconcile/autovalidate (lines 246-344) called from rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh:21-25. No EFI variable is ever written, no ESP mount is required at runtime, no init system is involved, and the disk is resolved by erlinit's /dev/rootdisk0 symlink rather than by parsing findmnt/sysfs.

**Why it matters:** This is the one part of the boot domain where our design is clearly better suited to Nerves, and it is worth stating explicitly so nobody tries to port q6a-boot-select. It also removes an unknown the Yocto system depends on: whether Radxa's EDK2 offers reliable writable EFI variables (their bootctl calls assume it). Our only equivalent unknown is whether GRUB's save_env reaches the disk, which is tracked as its own finding.

**Action:** No port. Add a short "why we do not use EFI variables" note to README.md's A/B section (near lines 60-75) recording: the reference Yocto system selects slots via bootctl-written UEFI variables from a systemd unit, which requires systemd, a mounted ESP, efibootmgr/bootctl, and writable EFI variables on this firmware; our grubenv-on-FAT approach needs none of those and keeps bootloader-side boot-once semantics. Cross-reference the grubenv-write bench test as the one thing that must be proven for our approach to hold. Also note that the Yocto side needed several fixes for disk resolution under udev timing (git eef6a55, eabd2b1, be5f6d8) that we avoid entirely by using /dev/rootdisk0.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the Q6A EDK2's EFI variable writes actually succeed is unverified in either direction; the Yocto system's reliance on them is suggestive but I found no log or commit confirming a successful bootctl set-default on hardware.

## A/B partitioning, fwup, and boot-slot selection/rollback

Both systems use the same two-store design: a U-Boot-format env block as the Nerves KV store (nerves_fw_active/validated/…) plus a bootloader-side pointer for the actual slot decision — neither board runs U-Boot. They differ in where each store lives and, critically, in who arbitrates a failed boot. Yocto: 512 MiB FAT32 ESP with systemd-boot + per-slot raw kernel/initramfs (EFI/Linux/q6a-{a,b}.Image + .cpio.gz), ext4 slots selected by root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-{a,b}, the slot pointer in EFI variables (bootctl set-default/set-oneshot), and a 128 KiB env inside a real GPT partition p4 at ~6.8 GiB — which forced a fwup 1.13.2→1.16.0 bump because uboot_env block_offset was uint32_t. Rollback is entirely OS-side (q6a-boot-select converge/validate + q6a_boot_attempts), with the trial boot issued as a UEFI one-shot from the old, known-good slot, and validation gated on nerves_hub_agent.service actu…


### [BLOCKER] grub.cfg installs its own DTB; the proven system deliberately does not  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image/q6a-a.conf has FOUR directives (title/linux/initrd/options), not three: "title Radxa Q6A (slot A)" / "linux /EFI/Linux/q6a-a.Image" / "initrd /EFI/Linux/q6a-a.cpio.gz" / "options root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0"; q6a-b.conf is the same with q6a-b.Image / q6a-b.cpio.gz / root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-b (the claim quoted slot A's values as if shared). Otherwise correct, and stronger than stated: grep for dtb/devicetree across the entire meta-q6a-nerveshub layer returns zero hits, and upstream meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image.bb states the policy outright — "# Remove 'upstream' dtb, rely on EFI provided one" with KERNEL_DEVICETREE = "" and per-machine re-enables only for glymur-crd, kaanapali-mtp, sm8750-mtp (plus uno-q in meta-qcom-3rdparty, commented "until the proper edk2 firmware is available"). radxa-dragon-q6a is intentionally excluded. The machine conf still sets KERNEL_DEVICETREE = "qcom/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb" so the DTB is built/deployed but neve…

**Ours today:** Confirmed verbatim at every cited line, and the evidence list should also include the docs that encode the same wrong assumption and would re-introduce it: README.md:41-46 ("installs the board device tree with its `devicetree` command"), README.md:53 and :59, README.md:166, GOAL.md:35-37, GOAL.md:77-78, GOAL.md:108-110 (cites Armbian `grub-with-dtb` as validation), CHANGELOG.md:113, fwup.conf:51, fwup-ops.conf:65. Also worth stating: `fdt` IS present in BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI (nerves_defconfig:58), so the devicetree command really executes rather than erroring out; and BOTH tests (test/qemu-smoke.sh:113 and test/qemu-rollback.sh:165) mcopy test/grub.cfg.qemu over /EFI/BOOT/grub.cfg, so the shipped config is never executed by any test. Independent corroboration exists in project memory dragon-q6a-dtb-from-uefi.md, recorded as user-confirmed on 2026-07-25.

**Why it matters:** The firmware DTB carries boot-time state a statically built kernel DTB cannot: the memory map and reserved-memory carveouts that must agree with what XBL/TZ set up, DDR size/SKU fixups. A mismatch surfaces as SMMU faults or ADSP/CDSP remoteproc load failures, not a clean error — i.e. exactly the class of failure that makes a first-boot-on-hardware attempt look like an unrelated driver bug. It also means our A/B story is untested on the code path that will actually run on the board.

**Action:** 1) grub.cfg: delete line 77 (`set nerves_dtb=...`) and both `devicetree ($nerves_disk,gptN)$nerves_dtb` lines (83, 88), leaving each branch as probe --part-uuid + `linux ($nerves_disk,gptN)/boot/Image root=PARTUUID=$nerves_root_uuid $nerves_cmdline`. 2) Rewrite grub.cfg:11-24 and fwup.conf:6-9 (also fwup.conf:51 and :65 comments, fwup-ops.conf:65 comment) to state that the SPI-NOR EDK2 supplies the DTB via the EFI configuration table and that the EFI payload must not install one. Keep the note that Secure Boot state no longer matters for DTB purposes (GRUB's devicetree restriction becomes irrelevant). 3) post-build.sh: drop the `if [ -e $BINARIES_DIR/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb ]; then install -m 0644 ... $TARGET_DIR/boot/; fi` block (lines 49-54) and fix the comment at line 44-45. Keep BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_DTS_SUPPORT=y and BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_INTREE_DTS_NAME="qcom/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a" (nerves_defconfig:42-43) so the DTB stays in $BINARIES_DIR for QEMU/bring-up diffing against /sys/firmware/fdt (BRINGUP.md:51). 4) `fdt` may stay in BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI (nerves_defconfig:58) but is no longer required; if kept, drop it from the module list comment in grub.cfg:6 rationale. 5) ADDITIONAL, not in the original claim and required so the wrong assumption is not re-introduced: correct README.md:41-46, README.md:53 and :59, README.md:166, GOAL.md:35-37, GOAL.md:77-78, GOAL.md:108-110, and note the change in CHANGELOG.md. GOAL.md/README currently cite Armbian's `grub-with-dtb` as validating the devicetree call — replace that with the meta-qcom evidence (esp-qcom-image.bb "Remove 'upstream' dtb, rely on EFI provided one", per-machine overrides only for glymur-crd/kaanapali-mtp/sm8750-mtp/uno-q) plus the user-confirmed fact that the Q6A EDK2 supplies the DTB. 6) REPLACES the claim's step 5: do NOT plan to generate test/grub.cfg.qemu by sed-ing only the console token — after step 1 the two files still differ in earlycon, loglevel (4 vs 7) and the clk_ignore_unused/pd_ignore_unused bring-up flags, which should not be forced into QEMU. Either (a) keep two files but add a CI check that the A/B rollback logic block is byte-identical between grub.cfg and test/grub.cfg.qemu, or (b) factor the cmdline into a single `set nerves_cmdline=` line and have qemu-smoke.sh/qemu-rollback.sh sed only that one line out of the shipped grub.cfg, so the tested control flow is literally the shipped control flow.

**Uncertainty:** I did not independently verify that this board's EDK2 publishes a DTB via the EFI config table — the evidence is (a) the proven image has no devicetree directive and boots with working display/GPU/NPU, and (b) a prior user confirmation recorded in project memory. If it turns out the firmware DTB is unusable, the correct fix is a per-slot grub.cfg (see the OTA-of-grub.cfg finding) rather than resto…

### [MAJOR] Our ESP is FAT16; UEFI requires FAT32 on fixed media and the proven ESP is FAT32  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** meta-q6a-nerveshub/files/wic/q6a-ab.wks.in:5 is quoted verbatim correctly. Additional context the claim omits: the FAT32-ness is NOT a Q6A-specific fix, it is an inherited upstream meta-qcom default. meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-common.inc sets IMAGE_ROOTFS_SIZE = "524288" / IMAGE_ROOTFS_MAXSIZE = "524288" ("Align image size with the expected partition size (512MB)") plus EXTRA_IMAGECMD:vfat += " -S ${QCOM_VFAT_SECTOR_SIZE}" (512 for this board, meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:53). oe-core/meta/classes-recipe/image_types.bbclass:288 leaves EXTRA_IMAGECMD:vfat ?= "" so no -F flag is passed and mkfs.vfat auto-selects; at 512 MiB it picks FAT32 (verified: mkfs.vfat -S 512 -C esp512.vfat 524288 -> fstype string FAT32, 8 sectors/cluster). The 512 MiB size exists because that ESP holds systemd-boot plus per-slot raw kernels and initramfs (/EFI/Linux/q6a-a.Image, q6a-a.cpio.gz), not because the firmware demands FAT32. No commit in the Yocto history mentions FAT type: `git log -i --grep=fat --grep=vfat --grep=esp` yields only 04756e8/3fa9710 (q6a-esp-fsck) a…

**Ours today:** Confirmed exactly as claimed, re-measured independently. fwup.conf:61-62 and fwup-ops.conf:61-62 both `define(BOOT_PART_COUNT, 65536) # 32 MiB`; fwup.conf:126-133 partition 0 name="boot", type c12a7328-f81f-11d2-ba4b-00a0c93ec93b, boot=true; fwup.conf:175 fat_mkfs(${BOOT_PART_OFFSET}, ${BOOT_PART_COUNT}). Boot sector at LBA 2048 of test/work/rollback-disk.img: OEM "MSDOS5.0", bytes/sector 512, sec/clus 1, root entries 512, totsec32 65536, sectors/FAT 257, fstype string 'FAT16 '. No existing mitigation under any other name: grep -rni 'fat32|fat16|fat12|mkfs.vfat|fat_mkfs' across the tree hits only fwup.conf:175. Nothing in grub.cfg, rootfs_overlay/, post-build.sh, post-createfs.sh, fwup_include/ or test/*.sh depends on the FAT type or on the GPT partition name "boot".

**Why it matters:** UEFI 2.x §13.3.1 mandates FAT32 for the ESP on a fixed (non-removable) disk; FAT12/16 support is only required for removable media. QEMU's EDK2 FatPkg happily mounts FAT16, which is why our QEMU tests pass and hid this. A Qualcomm/Radxa EDK2 that follows the letter of the spec for its fixed eMMC/UFS/NVMe boot device would simply not see our ESP — the board would fall through to the next boot option and look dead, with no serial output past the firmware banner. This is the single most likely reason a first hardware boot of our image fails silently.

**Action:** Same edit, corrected comment and scope. In BOTH /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf:62 and /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup-ops.conf:62 replace `define(BOOT_PART_COUNT, 65536) # 32 MiB` with `define(BOOT_PART_COUNT, 262144) # 128 MiB, FAT32 (fwup fat_mkfs picks FAT16 at 32 MiB)`. Correct the rationale: the fwup 1.15.1 / FatFs crossover is just ABOVE 32 MiB, not 64 MiB — I measured 32 MiB -> FAT16 and 48/64/96/128/512 MiB -> FAT32, all at 1 sector/cluster (FatFs selects FAT32 once the cluster count exceeds 65525). Anything >= 48 MiB works; 128 MiB is a comfortable round choice. Also update the ASCII layout comments at fwup.conf:49 and fwup-ops.conf:50 which say "32 MiB". Verified nothing else needs editing: only these two files reference 65536/BOOT_PART_*, ROOTFS_A_PART_OFFSET moves 67584 -> 264192 via the existing define-eval, APP_PART_LAST_OFFSET lands at ~4.63 GiB inside the 6 GiB test images, and test/qemu-smoke.sh:110 / test/qemu-rollback.sh:35 hardcode only offset 2048 so both tests are unaffected. Re-run test/qemu-smoke.sh and test/qemu-rollback.sh and re-dump LBA 2048 to confirm the fstype string moved to offset 82 as 'FAT32 '. Drop the `name = "ESP"` rename from the recommendation, or mark it purely cosmetic: its stated justification ("so /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP works for the fsck below") does not apply — the ESP fsck is the Yocto systemd unit q6a-esp-fsck.service and has no analogue in our tree, and nothing in our repo consumes the GPT partition name. If ESP FAT robustness after hard power cuts is a real concern (it is on the Yocto side: commit 3fa9710 runs fsck.vfat -a every boot), that is a SEPARATE finding needing its own non-systemd design — an fsck.vfat invocation from rootfs_overlay/ before anything touches the ESP, which also requires adding dosfstools (BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS + BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS_FSCK_FAT) to nerves_defconfig.

**Uncertainty:** I could not test the board's actual EDK2 FAT driver. Most EDK2 builds use FatPkg, which does support FAT16, so FAT16 may well work — but the proven configuration is FAT32 and the change is nearly free, so there is no reason to gamble. Note also that our GPT sets attribute bit 2 (legacy BIOS bootable, from `boot = true`) which the wks does not; UEFI ignores it, so I would leave it alone.
