### [MAJOR] We validate a new slot before the application starts; the proven system validates only after the app is confirmed running  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select, `validate` branch (lines 71-87): exits immediately unless booted==active and validated==0, then polls `systemctl is-active --quiet nerves_hub_agent.service` up to 72 times at 5 s intervals (6 min). If the agent never becomes active it exits WITHOUT validating — so the next boot's converge branch reverts. Only after the agent is up does it `sleep 30` (settle window), then `fw_setenv nerves_fw_validated 1`, `fw_setenv q6a_boot_attempts 0`, and `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default q6a-${booted}.conf`. q6a-fw-validate.service is ordered `After=nerves_hub_agent.service network-online.target`. Commit dcbf15b widened the window from 2 to 6 min after dead tpm2.target/qteesupplicant device waits pushed slot-B's first boot past it and caused a spurious rollback.

**Ours today:** Confirmed as stated, plus two facts the claim missed that change the fix: 1. `rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh:21-25` and `etc/erlinit.config:53` are exactly as cited; `fwup.conf:191` is `uboot_setenv(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_autovalidate", "1")`; `fwup-ops.conf:316-344` autovalidate.a/.b gate only on `nerves_fw_autovalidate=1` + `nerves_fw_validated=0` + `nerves_fw_active`. Grep over the repo (excluding example/deps and build) finds `validate_firmware` only in comments (fwup-ops.conf:243,314; README.md:79) — no Elixir call site. `example/lib/example/application.ex:12` is literally `children = []`. Neither example/config/config.exs nor target.exs configures :nerves_runtime. So the absent-validation claim holds. 2. MISSED: `nerves_runtime` 0.13.13 (already vendored at example/deps/nerves_runtime, mix.exs `{:nerves_runtime, "~> 0.13"}`) already ships `Nerves.Runtime.StartupGuard` — the exact upstream analogue of q6a-boot-select validate. It is a `Task, restart: :transient` started by `nerves_runtime/lib/nerves_runtime/application.ex:24-25` when `startup_guard_enabled: true`. It re…

**Why it matters:** Our validation bar is "the kernel booted and erlinit ran". A firmware whose Elixir release fails to start, crash-loops, or cannot reach NervesHub is marked good and GRUB will boot it forever — the classic un-recoverable OTA. This is the one place where the proven system is strictly stronger, and it is the whole point of A/B. The proven design also demonstrates the trap: their validate window had to be widened to 6 min because slow device probes delayed the agent, so any window we pick needs slack.

**Action:** (a) Keep as proposed, with one caveat. `fwup.conf:191` -> `uboot_setenv(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_autovalidate", "0")`; update the comment at fwup.conf:185-189 and README.md:76-79. Keep autovalidate.a/.b/.skip in fwup-ops.conf as the opt-in escape hatch for headless images. Leave `fwup -q -t reconcile` in qcom-coldplug.sh:23 untouched — reconcile must precede the app and autovalidate.skip makes line 24 a no-op. (b) REPLACE the hand-rolled child. Do NOT write a custom waiter in example/lib/example/application.ex. Enable the module nerves_runtime already ships: - example/config/target.exs: add `config :nerves_runtime, startup_guard_enabled: true` - example/rel/vm.args.eex:42: add `-env HEART_INIT_TIMEOUT 900` alongside the existing `-heart -env HEART_BEAT_TIMEOUT 120 -env HEART_INIT_GRACE_TIME 120`. StartupGuard's `Nerves.Runtime.Heart.init_complete/0` issues a nerves_heart `init_handshake` (heart.ex:101-113, requires nerves_heart ~> 2.0 — we have v2.5.0); without HEART_INIT_TIMEOUT the handshake has no window. StartupGuard's own doc says 600; heart.ex:93 documents 900. Use 900 so it exceeds StartupGuard's own 15-min give-up, otherwise heart reboots before StartupGuard's timer fires. - No new Buildroot package and no BR2_ symbol change is needed. BR2_PACKAGE_NERVES_HEART is already selected transitively by BR2_PACKAGE_NERVES_CONFIG (nerves_defconfig:76). - Only if the project later needs a NervesHub-connectivity gate (the Yocto `systemctl is-active nerves_hub_agent.service` equivalent) should a custom Task replace StartupGuard — StartupGuard's own moduledoc lines 14-19 says exactly this and points at :alarm_handler/alarmist. Note the example app has no nerves_hub_link dep at all (example/mix.exs deps: nerves, shoehorn, ring_logger, toolshed, nerves_runtime), so there is nothing to gate on today. That is the honest scope of the parity gap. - Document in README.md that an integrator who neither enables startup_guard_enabled nor calls Nerves.Runtime.validate_firmware/0 will roll back on every update. (c) The test change is under-specified and will produce a false failure. Flipping the default to 0 breaks the EXISTING test/qemu-rollback.sh Phase 1, whose assertions at lines 182-183 are `grubenv validated=1 (autovalidate wrote the ESP)` and `uboot-env nerves_fw_validated=1` — Phase 1 must be rewritten to expect StartupGuard (not autovalidate) to do the validating. Also `boot_qemu` stops on `Interactive Elixir|iex\(` and then sleeps only 5 s (lines 121-129); StartupGuard runs concurrently with the release start and can take one 10 s retry_delay tick, so the settle s…

**Uncertainty:** Whether to keep autovalidate=1 for this system's own smoke tests is a product call: flipping the default to 0 will make test/qemu-rollback.sh Phase 1 fail until it either sets the var or the example app validates. I would have the example app validate, since that is what a real integrator must do.

### [MAJOR] Our upgrade tasks accept an upgrade on top of an unvalidated slot; the proven ones refuse  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** meta-q6a-nerveshub/fwup/radxa-dragon-q6a.fwup task upgrade.a lines 119-122 and upgrade.b lines 177-180 each carry FOUR requires, not two: require-uboot-variable(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_active", "b"/"a"), require-uboot-variable(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_validated", "1"), plus the platform and architecture checks. An upgrade is simply not offered while a trial slot is pending validation.

**Ours today:** fwup.conf task upgrade.a lines 235-239 requires only nerves_fw_active=b, b.nerves_fw_platform and b.nerves_fw_architecture; upgrade.b lines 296-300 is the mirror. nerves_fw_validated is never consulted.

**Why it matters:** If an update lands while the currently running slot is still an unvalidated trial, we overwrite the only known-good slot with a second unproven image. Both slots are then unvalidated and the GRUB boot-once fallback has nothing good to fall back to — a single bad deployment can brick the unit. With NervesHub-driven deployments and a device that reboots slowly, this window is real, not theoretical.

**Action:** Original action is valid as written; add two refinements. 1. As proposed, in /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf insert after line 235 (`require-uboot-variable(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_active", "b")` in `task upgrade.a`) and after line 296 (the `"a"` line in `task upgrade.b`): require-uboot-variable(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_validated", "1") 2. The diagnostic task must be placed after upgrade.b (line 351) and BEFORE `task upgrade.unexpected` (line 353). Because fwup's `require-uboot-variable` fails on a *missing* variable as well as a mismatched one, gate the diagnostic on the active slot rather than on `nerves_fw_validated == "0"`, so a wiped/legacy KV store does not fall through to upgrade.unexpected's misleading "neither A nor B is active" message: task upgrade.unvalidated.a { require-uboot-variable(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_active", "a") on-init { error("Refusing to upgrade: the running firmware is not validated (nerves_fw_validated != 1). Validate it (Nerves.Runtime.validate_firmware/0 or `fwup -t validate -d /dev/rootdisk0 /usr/share/fwup/ops.fw`), or reboot to let the GRUB boot-once rollback complete.") } } task upgrade.unvalidated.b { require-uboot-variable(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_active", "b") on-init { error("Refusing to upgrade: the running firmware is not validated (nerves_fw_validated != 1). Validate it, or reboot to let the GRUB boot-once rollback complete.") } } No BR2_ symbol, package, or systemd unit involved — this is pure fwup.conf. The prefix/first-match ordering assumption is correct and is already relied on elsewhere in this repo (fwup.conf:353-365, fwup-ops.conf:243-244). `error()` in `on-init` and `require-uboot-variable(..., "0")` are both already used in-tree (fwup.conf:357, fwup-ops.conf:318). The safety argument also checks out: fwup-ops.conf reconcile.a/b (lines 281-300) set nerves_fw_validated=1 after a GRUB fallback, validate.a/b (246-265) and autovalidate.a/b (316-339) set it on a good boot, and revert.a/b (146-184) set it too — all invoked from rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh:23-24 at every boot, so no reachable state is permanently blocked. Also worth adding to test/qemu-rollback.sh: assert that `fwup -t upgrade` exits non-zero while nerves_fw_validated=0.

### [minor] No ESP FAT repair on our side, yet our entire boot decision is a FAT file GRUB must write before booting  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** A dedicated recipe exists solely for this: recipes-core/q6a-esp-fsck/. q6a-esp-fsck.service runs `DefaultDependencies=no`, `After=local-fs-pre.target`, `Before=local-fs.target boot.mount`, `ConditionPathExists=/dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP`, `TimeoutStartSec=60`, `ExecStart=/bin/sh -c 'umount /boot 2>/dev/null; fsck.vfat -a /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP || true'`, RDEPENDS dosfstools. The recipe description states the rationale outright: "hard power cuts and watchdog resets leave the FAT dirty", with three deliberate safeguards — auto-repair so it never prompts, a 60 s hard timeout so a pathological fs cannot hold up boot, and `|| true` so a failed fsck never fails the boot.

**Ours today:** Correct that the gap exists, with two line-level fixes: BR2_PACKAGE_HOST_DOSFSTOOLS=y is nerves_defconfig line 59 (not 60); "# BR2_PACKAGE_E2FSPROGS_FSCK is not set" is line 54 as claimed. Also note our ESP partition is named "boot" (fwup.conf:131), so /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP does not exist on our side at all today. Everything else verified: no target fsck anywhere in the tree, ESP never mounted (erlinit.config mounts only /dev/rootdisk0p4), qcom-coldplug.sh has no fsck step, grub.cfg:48-62 does gate the whole trial-boot on `save_env booted_once` to a FAT file.

**Why it matters:** Our A/B state machine is more FAT-dependent than theirs, not less. grub.cfg's boot-once logic calls `save_env booted_once` BEFORE booting a trial slot; if that write fails because the FAT is dirty or the directory entry is unreadable, booted_once never becomes 1 and GRUB re-offers the same bad slot on every boot — an infinite retry loop with no rollback. The board has CONFIG_QCOM_WDT=y (linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:38) and the Qualcomm platform resets hard, which is precisely the condition the Yocto recipe was written for. On the plus side our exposure per update is 1 KiB (grubenv) versus their ~40 MB kernel+initrd fat_write, so corruption is rarer — but we have zero recovery when it happens.

**Action:** 1) nerves_defconfig — add next to BR2_PACKAGE_HOST_DOSFSTOOLS=y (line 59): BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS=y BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS_FSCK_FAT=y (verified in buildroot-2026.05/package/dosfstools/Config.in:1,17; dosfstools.mk:37-40 installs /sbin/fsck.fat + symlink /sbin/fsck.vfat. Do NOT enable MKFS_FAT/FATLABEL; not needed.) 2) busybox.fragment — add CONFIG_TIMEOUT=y (confirmed "# CONFIG_TIMEOUT is not set" at nerves-common/busybox.config:339 and absent from our fragment). 3) rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh — insert the fsck BEFORE the A/B bookkeeping block, i.e. between line 10 (`set -e`) and the `OPS_FW=` line at 21, NOT after it. autovalidate.a/b fat_write /EFI/BOOT/grubenv (fwup-ops.conf:325,337), so a dirty FAT must be repaired first: # 0. Repair the ESP FAT before anything writes to it. Hard power cuts and # watchdog resets (CONFIG_QCOM_WDT) leave the FAT dirty, and every A/B # decision lives in a 1 KiB grubenv on that FAT. /dev/rootdisk0p1 is # created by erlinit before --pre-run-exec (erlinit src/rootdisk.c). if [ -x /sbin/fsck.vfat ] && [ -e /dev/rootdisk0p1 ]; then timeout 60 /sbin/fsck.vfat -a /dev/rootdisk0p1 >/dev/null 2>&1 || true fi `|| true` is required because of `set -e` at line 10. 4) NEW, not in the original proposal: handle the case where fsck's repair deletes or truncates grubenv. GRUB's save_env cannot create the file, so a missing grubenv silently disables A/B rollback (load_env yields nothing, grub.cfg:80 falls through to slot A) until the next upgrade. Either add a `fwup -t <task>` / fat_write path in ops.fw that rewrites a pristine grubenv when absent, or check for it from Elixir at boot and log loudly. Decide this explicitly rather than leaving it implicit. 5) Do not rely on /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP: fwup.conf:131 names partition 0 "boot". /dev/rootdisk0p1 needs no udev and is correct as-is.

**Uncertainty:** I did not confirm busybox `timeout` is enabled in the nerves-common busybox.config; if not, either add CONFIG_TIMEOUT=y to busybox.fragment or drop the timeout wrapper (fsck.vfat -a on a 32-128 MiB volume is fast, and a hung fsck before the BEAM starts is the failure mode the Yocto recipe guarded against).

### [minor] No OTA path for BOOTAA64.EFI or grub.cfg — the kernel command line is frozen at provisioning time  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Accurate as written; exact lines: radxa-dragon-q6a.fwup:138 `info("ESP not rewritten on upgrade; per-slot kernel/initrd are fat_write'n instead")`, 142-144 fat_mkdir "EFI" / fat_mkdir "EFI/Linux" / fat_write "EFI/Linux/q6a-a.Image", 148 fat_write "EFI/Linux/q6a-a.cpio.gz" (196-206 mirror for B). esp-qcom-image.bbappend:20-34 installs loader/loader.conf and loader/entries/q6a-{a,b}.conf only in the factory image; the cmdline is the `options root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0` line of q6a-a.conf. One addition: the Yocto side does have a runtime ESP write path our system lacks — recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select:45,54-55,85 mounts ${disk}p1 rw on /boot and calls `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default/set-oneshot` — but it only changes the default entry, never the cmdline, and it is systemd/bootctl-dependent so it has no Nerves analogue.

**Ours today:** fwup.conf:97-102 declares bootaa64.efi and grub.cfg as file-resources, but only `task complete` writes them (206-207); upgrade.a (263-284) and upgrade.b (323-343) fat_write only /EFI/BOOT/grubenv, and fwup-ops.conf declares no grub.cfg/bootaa64 resource at all (only grubenv_a_valid/grubenv_b_valid). Confirmed by tree-wide grep: the only other writers of /EFI/BOOT/grub.cfg are the host-side test harnesses (test/qemu-smoke.sh:113, test/qemu-rollback.sh:165). So no single-step OTA can change grub.cfg:75's nerves_cmdline or the gpt2/gpt3 slot mapping. It is NOT, however, "frozen at provisioning time": /usr/share/fwup/ops.fw is regenerated from fwup-ops.conf into the rootfs on every build (post-build.sh:58) and rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh:22-25 already runs fwup tasks from it at every boot, and CONFIG_VFAT_FS=y (linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:113-115) means the ESP is writable on-target — so a new cmdline is deliverable in two steps (boot once on the old cmdline, then reboot). Kernel and DTB do upgrade atomically inside each slot's squashfs (grub.cfg:83-89, post-build.sh:52-54).

**Why it matters:** clk_ignore_unused and pd_ignore_unused are explicitly marked in grub.cfg as bring-up safety nets to revisit; loglevel, console and any future cmdline parameter (cma=, module_blacklist=, panic=) can never be changed on a fielded unit. A kernel that needs a new cmdline argument becomes undeliverable, which defeats the purpose of having OTA at all. Naively fat_write'ing grub.cfg during upgrade is not the answer: a power cut mid-write leaves an unparseable grub.cfg and GRUB drops to the `grub>` prompt on a headless board — unrecoverable in the field.

**Action:** Same restructure, plus the test-harness half the original proposal omitted: 1. /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg becomes a frozen shim: load_env, the boot-once/fallback block (lines 48-62 unchanged), the `regexp --set=nerves_disk` probe (66-69), then `if [ "$boot" = "1" ]; then set nerves_slot=gpt3; else set nerves_slot=gpt2; fi` and `configfile ($nerves_disk,$nerves_slot)/boot/grub.cfg`, with the current inline probe/devicetree/linux/boot lines kept after it as a last-resort fallback. configfile, regexp, probe, squash4, loadenv are already in BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI (nerves_defconfig:58) — no defconfig change. 2. The per-slot config owns nerves_cmdline, nerves_dtb, the PARTUUID probe and linux/boot. Install it from post-build.sh into $TARGET_DIR/boot/grub.cfg (next to the existing DTB install at line 53) so it lands in the squashfs; note post-build.sh:47's `rm -rf $TARGET_DIR/boot/grub` does not touch a plain /boot/grub.cfg. No fwup.conf change needed. 3. REQUIRED and missing from the original proposal: test/grub.cfg.qemu must NOT chain to the in-slot config — under -M virt the in-slot config's console=ttyMSM0 and `devicetree` of qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb are both wrong, and configfile would boot (or hang) before the shim's fallback lines could run. Either keep test/grub.cfg.qemu fully inline (no configfile) or have the shim export a flag (e.g. `set nerves_platform=qemu`) that the in-slot config tests before `devicetree`/cmdline selection. Re-run test/qemu-smoke.sh and test/qemu-rollback.sh after the change; both currently assume the entire boot policy is replaceable by mcopy'ing one ESP file. 4. Cheaper alternative that avoids touching the QEMU harness at all, and which should probably be done first: add an `update-esp` task to fwup-ops.conf with a grub.cfg file-resource and a `fat_write(${BOOT_PART_OFFSET}, "/EFI/BOOT/grub.cfg")`, invoked once from qcom-coldplug.sh (which already runs reconcile/autovalidate from ops.fw). ops.fw ships inside the rootfs, so each firmware carries its own new ESP config; the write happens after the new slot has already booted, so a bad grub.cfg cannot brick the unit before the boot-once fallback can act — but be explicit that this is a two-boot rollout and cannot deliver a cmdline the new kernel needs in order to boot at all. 5. Either way, document in README.md next to the A/B section that BOOTAA64.EFI is never updated post-provisioning (same posture as the reference system's systemd-boot) and that cmdline changes take effect one reboot late.

**Uncertainty:** I have not tested GRUB's `configfile` chainload out of a squashfs on this build; the squash4 module is present and the pattern is common, but it needs a QEMU smoke run. Also note the memory record that GRUB's squash4 cannot read zstd-compressed squashfs (our rootfs is gzip -b 131072) — a per-slot grub.cfg inherits that constraint.

### [minor] Runtime device-path discovery: theirs is defensive, ours delegates to erlinit — no change needed, but the failure mode differs  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** q6a-boot-select rederives the boot disk on EVERY boot rather than trusting anything baked in: `devno=$(findmnt -n -o MAJ:MIN / | tr -d "[:space:]")`, then `part=$(basename "$(readlink -f "/sys/dev/block/$devno")")` — deliberately using the kernel-provided /sys/dev/block path because it has no udev timing dependency — then sed patterns mapping mmcblkN p*, nvmeXnY p*, sdX to a disk. It then rewrites /etc/fw_env.config, and syncs nerves_fw_devpath and both {a,b}.nerves_fw_application_part0_devpath to ${disk}p5. This code was hardened across five commits (736bb51 by-path/by-partlabel, 37a9e03, d6ab42b nvme, be5f6d8 /dev/block/maj:min, eabd2b1 findmnt padding, eef6a55 basename) because SD enumerates as mmcblk1 when eMMC is present and mmcblk0 when it is not, and because findmnt SOURCE can return a by-partlabel symlink that does not exist yet when converge runs. The .fwup meanwhile bakes NERVES_FW_DEVPATH=/dev/disk/by-path/platform-8804000.mmc (the SD controller's stable by-path) and the data devpath as /dev/disk/by-partlabel/data.

**Ours today:** We do none of this and do not need to: erlinit creates /dev/rootdisk0 and /dev/rootdisk0pN by resolving whatever device the rootfs actually mounted from, before --pre-run-exec runs. fwup.conf bakes NERVES_FW_DEVPATH=/dev/rootdisk0 and NERVES_FW_APPLICATION_PART0_DEVPATH=/dev/rootdisk0p4; rootfs_overlay/etc/fw_env.config is a single static line `/dev/rootdisk0 0x8000 0x2000 0x200 16`; qcom-coldplug.sh guards on `[ -e /dev/rootdisk0 ]` before calling fwup. This is media-agnostic by construction, which is exactly the property they spent six commits reconstructing.

**Why it matters:** Ours is simpler and structurally better, so this is a note rather than a defect. The residual risk is different in kind: we depend on erlinit having created the rootdisk symlinks before the pre-run hook. That ordering is confirmed (erlinit.c creates them before pre-run-exec, and our QEMU rollback test passes 11/11, which requires fwup to have reached the KV store), but the guard in qcom-coldplug.sh silently skips ALL A/B bookkeeping if the symlink is missing — the same silent-skip shape as the v0.5.3 `command -v fwup` bug, which went undetected for a release because nothing complained. Also worth internalizing from their history: our fw_env.config must keep using /dev/rootdisk0, not a by-par…

**Action:** Make the skip loud instead of silent, in /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh: change the `if [ -e "$OPS_FW" ] && [ -e /dev/rootdisk0 ] && [ -x /usr/bin/fwup ]` block (lines 22-25) to add an `else` branch that writes a diagnostic to /dev/kmsg, e.g. `else echo "qcom-coldplug: A/B bookkeeping SKIPPED (ops.fw=$OPS_FW rootdisk=$( [ -e /dev/rootdisk0 ] && echo yes || echo no ))" > /dev/kmsg 2>/dev/null || true; fi`. That single line would have caught the v0.5.3 bug on its first boot. Optionally also add a positive-path assertion to test/qemu-rollback.sh: grep the serial log for absence of that message. No change to fw_env.config, fwup.conf devpaths, or erlinit.config is needed — and explicitly do NOT switch fw_env.config to a by-partlabel path.

### [minor] boardid serial-number source differs; ours is a superset  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** recipes-extended/boardid/files/radxa-dragon-q6a/boardid.config contains exactly one line: `-b macaddr`. The serial number is derived solely from the first Ethernet MAC. Their uboot.txt.env carries no nerves_serial_number key and their .fwup has no provisioning include. Commit 2851034 mentions fixing a "boardid getopt error", which is why the config is minimal.

**Ours today:** rootfs_overlay/etc/boardid.config tries the KV store first and falls back: `-b uboot_env -u nerves_serial_number`, then `-b uboot_env -u serial_number`, then `-b macaddr`. fwup_include/provisioning.conf supplies the value at burn time via `uboot_setenv(uboot-env, "nerves_serial_number", "\${NERVES_SERIAL_NUMBER}")`, escaped so it is resolved at burn time and no serial is stored in the .fw; the include is pulled in by task complete (fwup.conf line 181) and by task provision (line 371). erlinit.config uses it for the hostname (`-d /usr/bin/boardid`, `-n nerves-%-.4s`).

**Why it matters:** Not a gap — ours is strictly more capable and matches Nerves convention (per-unit provisioning at burn time, MAC as fallback). Recording it because it is the one A/B-adjacent place where we are ahead, and because the boardid uboot_env backend reads through /etc/fw_env.config, so it shares the KV-store dependency described above: if fw_env.config is ever pointed at a path U-Boot's fw_printenv cannot open, hostnames silently regress to the MAC-derived form rather than failing loudly.

**Action:** No change. When touching /etc/fw_env.config for any reason, remember boardid depends on it too (rootfs_overlay/etc/boardid.config lines 4-5), and verify with `boardid` on target that the KV backend still resolves rather than falling through to macaddr.

### [info] Two A/B state stores, two different arbiters — full side-by-side of the state machines  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Store 1 = 128 KiB U-Boot-format env block, mkenvimage -s 131072 from recipes-bsp/q6a-boot-env/files/uboot.txt.env, deployed as uboot-env-q6a.bin and rawcopy'd by wic into GPT partition p4 labelled "bootenv" (1 MiB, at ~6.8 GiB / sector 13633536). fwup accesses it as `uboot-environment uboot-env { block-offset = ${BOOTENV_PART_OFFSET}; block-count = 256 }`; userspace via /etc/fw_env.config, which q6a-boot-select REWRITES every boot as `printf '%s %s %s\n' "${disk}p4" 0x0 0x20000`. Keys: nerves_fw_active, nerves_fw_validated, q6a_boot_attempts, nerves_fw_devpath, {a,b}.nerves_fw_*. Store 2 = the actual slot pointer, held in EFI variables written by `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default|set-oneshot q6a-{a,b}.conf`, with /loader/loader.conf providing the initial `default q6a-a.conf` and `timeout 3`. Sequence after upgrade.b: fwup sets active=b validated=0 attempts=0 but does NOT touch systemd-boot -> reboot boots the OLD slot A -> converge sees booted!=active, validated=0, attempts<1 -> attempts=1, `bootctl set-oneshot q6a-b.conf`, reboot -> systemd-boot consumes the one-shot and boots B…

**Ours today:** Store 1 = 8 KiB U-Boot-format env at LBA 64-79, in the GPT gap (not a partition): `uboot-environment uboot-env { block-offset = 64; block-count = 16 }`; userspace via a STATIC rootfs_overlay/etc/fw_env.config line `/dev/rootdisk0 0x8000 0x2000 0x200 16`. Keys: nerves_fw_active, nerves_fw_validated, nerves_fw_autovalidate, nerves_fw_devpath=/dev/rootdisk0, {a,b}.nerves_fw_*, nerves_serial_number (fwup_include/provisioning.conf). Store 2 = /EFI/BOOT/grubenv on the ESP, a preallocated 1 KiB GRUB env block holding boot (0=A,1=B), validated, booted_once; four variants are built by post-build.sh with grub-editenv (grubenv_a, grubenv_b, grubenv_a_valid, grubenv_b_valid). Sequence after upgrade.b: fwup writes rootfs B first then grubenv_b last (boot=1 validated=0 booted_once=0) and sets active=b validated=0 -> reboot, GRUB sets booted_once=1, save_env, boots B once -> if B lives, autovalidate/validate writes grubenv_b_valid + validated=1; if B dies, next boot GRUB sees validated=0 booted_once=1, flips boot to 0, sets validated=1, save_env, boots A, and qcom-coldplug's `fwup -t reconcile` re-…

**Why it matters:** The two designs are equivalent in the property that matters — exactly one trial boot, with automatic return to the previously running slot — so our GRUB scheme is not inherently weaker and it avoids depending on a writable UEFI varstore and on systemd/bootctl entirely, which is the right call for a Nerves system with BR2_INIT_NONE. Two genuine differences are worth recording rather than fixing: (1) their trial boot is issued FROM the good slot, so the trial decision is made by working software, whereas ours is made by GRUB before any OS runs; (2) their q6a_boot_attempts gives one retry for the case where the bootloader picks a slot the env disagrees with (e.g. the operator changes the UEFI b…

**Action:** No change required. Do record the mapping in README.md so the two projects can be reasoned about together: nerves_fw_active <-> nerves_fw_active (same key, same meaning); grubenv boot=0/1 <-> EFI LoaderEntryDefault q6a-a.conf/q6a-b.conf; grubenv booted_once <-> EFI LoaderEntryOneShot + q6a_boot_attempts; grubenv validated <-> nerves_fw_validated. One concrete robustness note to add: our fw_env.config and fwup both address the KV store through /dev/rootdisk0, an erlinit-created symlink; Buildroot supplies U-Boot 2026.04's own fw_printenv (BR2_PACKAGE_UBOOT_TOOLS, selected by nerves-config), NOT libubootenv, so we do not inherit the Yocto bug where libubootenv's config parser rejected a /dev/disk/by-partlabel/... path (their commits 330a601, ccb29e0). Do not switch to a by-partlabel path in /etc/fw_env.config.

### [info] fwup version: their >4 GiB uboot-env bug does not apply to us, but the constraint must be documented before anyone moves the KV store  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** recipes-devtools/fwup/fwup_%.bbappend pins PV = "1.16.0", SRCREV = 8f6b3efc6fc2f9d1b34702cfc464f16a7d3a3812, with the root cause in the file itself: "uboot_env.block_offset is uint32_t in fwup <= 1.13.2: any uboot environment past 4GiB overflows to a wrong offset (our bootenv sits at ~6.8GiB, after the two 3GiB rootfs slots -> 'require-uboot-variable not met'). Fixed upstream by 95e88fc in v1.14.0." Commit 30a00aa adds the numbers: sector 13633536 (~6.8 GiB), reads landed at (offset mod 2^32) and returned all 0xff, so every require-uboot-variable failed. Commit be5f6d8 retracts the earlier misdiagnosis — the 0x1A0100000 offset had been correct all along (13633536*512), the failure was purely the uint32 overflow.

**Ours today:** Our KV store is at LBA 64 (32 KiB into the disk), so block_offset can never overflow. fwup.conf declares require-fwup-version="1.12.0" and fwup-ops.conf declares "1.0.0". On-target fwup is 1.16.0 (Buildroot 2026.05 package/fwup/fwup.mk FWUP_VERSION = 1.16.0, pulled in by nerves-config's `select BR2_PACKAGE_FWUP`) — the same version the Yocto side had to pin to. My host fwup is 1.15.1, which also has the fix.

**Why it matters:** Nothing is broken today, but the constraint is invisible in our tree and the trap is easy to walk into: if the KV store is ever moved into a real GPT partition after the slots (the Yocto layout), or if slot sizes grow past ~4 GiB with an env placed after them, every require-uboot-variable silently fails and A/B updates stop working with a misleading error. The 1.0.0 floor in fwup-ops.conf is also stale — that file uses require-path-on-device and relies on prefix task matching.

**Action:** 1) Add a comment next to `define(UBOOT_ENV_OFFSET, 64)` in both /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf (line 58) and fwup-ops.conf (line 58): "Keep the KV store below 4 GiB, or require fwup >= 1.14.0 — uboot_env block_offset was uint32_t through 1.13.2 (upstream fix 95e88fc)." 2) Raise fwup-ops.conf's `require-fwup-version` from "1.0.0" to match fwup.conf's "1.12.0" so a stale on-device fwup cannot half-execute ops.fw. 3) Note for the record that fwup has no redundant/two-copy U-Boot env support — I verified fwup 1.15.1 rejects both `redundant-offset` and `redundant` inside a `uboot-environment` block — so a power cut during uboot_setenv can corrupt the single 8 KiB copy in either system. Our exposure is milder than theirs because grubenv on the ESP independently holds the slot pointer, so the board still boots; only fwup upgrades break until the KV is rewritten. Worth one sentence in README.md rather than a code change.

### [info] On-disk layouts are mutually incompatible; decide now whether the two projects must interoperate  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** q6a-ab.wks.in, all on mmcblk0, align 1024: p1 esp 512 MiB label ESP type C12A7328...; p2 rootfs-a 3072 MiB ext4 label rootfs-a; p3 rootfs-b 3072 MiB ext4 label rootfs-b; p4 bootenv 1 MiB fstype=none label bootenv (rawcopy of uboot-env-q6a.bin); p5 data ext4 label data, --size 8 (grows). Offsets in the .fwup mirror it: ESP at 2048/1048576 sectors, rootfs A at 1050624, rootfs B at 7342080, bootenv at 13633536, app at 13635584. Slots are selected by root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a|rootfs-b, hardcoded in the loader entries. Their fwup `complete` task writes no GPT at all and is explicitly "NOT a bootable provisioning artifact" — real provisioning flashes .wic.gz with bmaptool. /data is shared across slots and holds SSH host keys (q6a-nerveshub-image.bb) so both slots present the same identity, growfs via `LABEL=data /data ext4 defaults,x-systemd.growfs 0 0`.

**Ours today:** Verified against the actual built image (test/work/rollback-disk.img GPT): p1 "boot" C12A7328... LBA 2048-67583 (32 MiB, attr 0x4); p2 "rootfs.a" 0FC63DAF... LBA 67584-4261887 (2048 MiB); p3 "rootfs.b" LBA 4261888-8456191 (2048 MiB); p4 "app" LBA 8456192-end (expand=true). KV store in the GPT gap at LBA 64. Slots selected by root=PARTUUID probed at boot with `probe --set=nerves_root_uuid --part-uuid ($nerves_disk,gptN)`, not by label, so the same image boots from microSD/UFS/NVMe/USB and coexisting media work (install-to-disk.sh runs `sgdisk -G` to randomize GUIDs per medium). Our `complete` task DOES write the GPT (gpt_write + fat_mkfs) and is a real provisioning artifact. App partition is p4 mounted at /root via erlinit `-m /dev/rootdisk0p4:/root:ext4:nodev:`, formatted on first boot because complete memsets its head to 0xff.

**Why it matters:** No partition offset, size, label or number matches between the two layouts, and the root= mechanism differs (PARTUUID vs PARTLABEL). A board flashed with the Yocto .wic cannot be updated with our .fw and vice versa; there is no migration path short of a full reflash. That is fine as long as it is a conscious decision — but if the plan is ever to move fielded Yocto units onto the Buildroot system over the air, it is not merely hard, it is impossible with the current designs, and that needs to be surfaced before hardware ships. Separately, our PARTUUID probing is the better mechanism (multi-medium safe, no label collisions) and our GPT-writing complete task is a better provisioning story than …

**Action:** Decide and document, don't silently diverge. (a) If the Buildroot system is the future and Yocto units get reflashed: add a short "Relationship to the Yocto reference" section to README.md stating that the layouts are intentionally incompatible and that migration is a full reflash via install-to-disk.sh or fwup -t complete. Keep PARTUUID probing. (b) Take the two cheap alignment wins regardless, since they cost nothing and reduce cross-project confusion: rename the ESP partition to `name = "ESP"` in fwup.conf (line 131) — it also gives us /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP for the fsck — and consider naming the slots "rootfs-a"/"rootfs-b" instead of "rootfs.a"/"rootfs.b" to match. Renames are safe because nothing in our boot path reads partition names (GRUB uses gptN indices and PARTUUID; erlinit uses /dev/rootdisk0pN). (c) Note the one identity gap worth copying eventually: they deliberately persist SSH host keys on the shared /data so A and B present the same identity. Our /root (p4) is shared across slots the same way, so the equivalent is available to the application layer, but nothing in this system arranges it. Mention it in README.md as an integrator responsibility.

**Uncertainty:** Whether Yocto-flashed boards exist in the field that would need migrating is a project fact I could not determine from the repositories.

### [info] Yocto-side systemd/U-Boot dependencies with no clean Nerves analogue — what we must NOT try to port  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Four load-bearing dependencies on infrastructure we do not have. (1) systemd unit ordering: q6a-boot-select.service uses DefaultDependencies=no, After=local-fs.target, Before=nerves_hub_agent.service, ConditionPathExists=/sys/firmware/efi/efivars; q6a-fw-validate.service uses After=nerves_hub_agent.service network-online.target, Wants=network-online.target; q6a-esp-fsck.service uses After=local-fs-pre.target, Before=local-fs.target boot.mount, TimeoutStartSec=60. (2) `systemctl is-active --quiet nerves_hub_agent.service` as the liveness oracle for validation. (3) `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default|set-oneshot` — systemd-boot plus a writable EFI variable store, with /boot mounted rw from ${disk}p1 by the script itself (mount_esp/umount_esp helpers). (4) The U-Boot env FORMAT (mkenvimage, fw_printenv/fw_setenv) used purely as a KV container, on a board with no U-Boot — plus u-boot-tools-native as a build dependency of q6a-boot-env. They also install efibootmgr in the image. (5) Their validate path depends on systemd's ability to mount and unmount the ESP at will.

**Ours today:** BR2_INIT_NONE (nerves_defconfig line 21) — no systemd, no units, no systemctl, no bootctl, no efibootmgr, no ordering graph. Our single hook is erlinit's `--pre-run-exec /usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh` (erlinit.config line 53), which runs once, synchronously, before the BEAM, with PATH=/usr/sbin:/usr/bin:/sbin:/bin. The ESP is never mounted; fwup writes it with fat_write and GRUB with save_env. Of their four dependencies we legitimately share exactly one — item (4), the U-Boot env format as a KV container, which is standard Nerves and needs no U-Boot.

**Why it matters:** This is the design boundary that must not be papered over. Items (1)-(3) and (5) are not portable and should not be emulated: there is no place in our boot to express "after the network is online, before the agent", no daemon supervision to query, and no reason to introduce EFI-variable writes when grubenv already holds the slot pointer. The consequence is concrete and already visible in the autovalidate finding: the pre-run hook is the WRONG place for anything that must observe the application, because by construction it runs before the application exists. Anything app-conditional must live in Elixir (supervision tree, NervesHub callbacks) — that is the analogue of their After=nerves_hub_ag…

**Action:** Codify the split so future porting work does not drift. Add a short section to /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/BRINGUP.md (or README.md next to the A/B section) with a table: (a) pre-BEAM, ordering-free, must be idempotent -> rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-beacon-coldplug.sh — ESP fsck, `fwup -t reconcile`, udev coldplug, device permissions, cdsprpcd; (b) needs the app running or the network up -> Elixir only — firmware validation, NervesHub check-in, anything with a timeout or a retry; (c) never port -> bootctl/EFI-variable slot selection, systemd unit ordering, mounting the ESP at runtime, efibootmgr. Then make the one substantive move this implies, already specified in the autovalidate finding: relocate validation from qcom-coldplug.sh into the application. Keep `fwup -t reconcile` in the hook — reconcile is pre-app by nature (it must run before anything reads Nerves.Runtime.KV) and is safe every boot, as fwup-ops.conf's reconcile.ok fallback documents.

**Uncertainty:** The exact filename in the (a) bullet is rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh; I mistyped it above as qcom-beacon-coldplug.sh — use the real path.

## kernel — source tree, version, DTB, and config symbols

The proven Yocto system does not use a Qualcomm or mainline kernel at all: kas pins PREFERRED_PROVIDER_virtual/kernel="linux-qcom" + PREFERRED_VERSION="6.18%", and then meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-qcom_%.bbappend *replaces* SRC_URI outright with git://github.com/radxa/kernel.git branch linux-6.18.2 @ 559f4f921a01e5358602153364c618fe2a3e431e — i.e. the Radxa vendor tree (stable 6.18.2 + ~100 Radxa board commits). Our Buildroot system uses Deka-Embedded-Linux/linux-dragon-q6a @ e05c4fa, which is mainline v6.18.0 plus only 31 commits — a rebased subset of an older Radxa branch (dragon-q6a-v6.17.8-wp). Both trees ship a byte-identical arch/arm64/configs/defconfig (verified: `diff` returns identical, 1825 lines), which is also the base the Yocto build starts from (KBUILD_DEFCONFIG="defconfig"), so switching trees costs us nothing config-wise and gains ~70 board patches we cu…

Keep BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_INTREE_DTS_NAME="qcom/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a" (present in the radxa tree at that SHA). ### [BLOCKER] SC_GPUCC_7280 and SC_DISPCC_7280 are =m for us; proven system requires =y (adreno SMMU probe -110)  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg sets CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y and CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y. Commit c1ecf57 ("Display: builtin gpucc/dispcc/msm like vendor — fixes adreno SMMU -110") states the root cause verbatim: "SC_GPUCC_7280=m meant the adreno SMMU had no clocks/power at its early probe (fail -110), dooming every later adreno bind (-19)." The Radxa vendor config arch/arm64/configs/qcom_module_defconfig agrees: CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y, CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y. Note the arm64 base defconfig has both as =m — the fragment is what flips them.

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:219-223 sets CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m, CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m under the comment at 216-218 ("Modules: not boot-critical, probed via udev coldplug"). Repo-wide grep confirms no override exists elsewhere. Correction to the cited evidence: CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y is at line 44 (CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y at line 45), not line 46. Additionally, our fragment sets no DRM base symbols whatsoever (no CONFIG_DRM=, DRM_KMS_HELPER, DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER, DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER, DRM_SIMPLEDRM, DRM_AUX_BRIDGE, DRM_MSM_DP, QCOM_LLCC, QCOM_OCMEM, PHY_QCOM_QMP), so it relies entirely on the arm64 default defconfig (nerves_defconfig:40 BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y) for those — which is why flipping only gpucc/dispcc would not be enough to make DRM_MSM builtin.

**Why it matters:** Our fragment sets CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y, so the adreno SMMU (adreno_smmu node) probes during early builtin driver init, long before any module can be loaded — worse for us than for the Yocto side, since we have no systemd and udev coldplug runs from rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh well after kernel init. gpu_cc as a module means the SMMU's clocks/power-domain are absent at that probe, it returns -110 (ETIMEDOUT) and is permanently dead, so every subsequent adreno/DRM_MSM bind fails -19. Net effect on hardware: no GPU, no HDMI, mesa freedreno and kmscube dead — all the BR2_PACKAGE_MESA3D_GALLIUM_DRIVER_FREEDRENO userspace we build is unusable.

**Action:** In /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment replace lines 216-223 (the comment block plus CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m, CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m) with the vendor-proven builtin block. All of it is needed: our fragment currently sets no DRM base symbols, so it inherits the arm64 defconfig where CONFIG_DRM and its helpers are modular, and Buildroot's post-merge `olddefconfig` will silently demote CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y back to =m if the helpers stay =m. # --- GPU / display: builtin like the vendor kernel. gpucc as a module # --- left the adreno SMMU (CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y, line 44) without # --- clocks/power at its early builtin probe -> -110, dooming every # --- later adreno bind (-19). Firmware from qcom-dsp-firmware. CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y CONFIG_DRM=y CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y # msm=y deps (tristate caps): must be builtin for MSM to stay builtin CONFIG_QCOM_LLCC=y CONFIG_QCOM_OCMEM=y Keep CONFIG_SC_LPASS_CORECC_7280=m and the audio/venus block that follows (line 224 onward) unchanged — the vendor fragment does not builtin those. Note the vendor block intentionally drops CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR entirely (q6a-display.cfg never sets it); dropping ours is consistent. After editing, verify the merged result actually kept =y by checking the built .config for CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y and CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y rather than assuming olddefconfig honored the fragment. Also fix the now-stale comment at /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/nerves_defconfig:94 which reads "Kernel side (CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m + a660 firmware) is already in the fragment".

### [BLOCKER] DTB: we override the firmware DTB; the proven system installs no devicetree at all  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** Correct as stated, and stronger: grep for "devicetree" or ".dtb" across the entire meta-q6a-nerveshub layer and kas/ returns zero hits. esp-qcom-image.bbappend deletes the class-built UKI (rm -f ${IMAGE_ROOTFS}/EFI/Linux/q6a.efi) and installs only q6a-a.Image, q6a-a.cpio.gz, loader/loader.conf and the two Type 1 entries — so no DTB arrives via a UKI .dtb section either. q6a-nerveshub-image.bb overrides the machine conf with WKS_FILE = "q6a-ab.wks.in", whose partitions are esp / rootfs-a / rootfs-b / bootenv / data with no dtb partition, so meta-qcom's linux-qcom-dtbbin.bbclass dtb-<name>-image.vfat is built but never flashed. The proven-on-hardware boot therefore runs 100% on the EDK2-provided DTB.

**Ours today:** grub.cfg:16-17 asserts the opposite ("The Qualcomm UEFI does not hand the OS a usable device tree - the EFI payload must install it"), sets nerves_dtb="/boot/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb" (grub.cfg:77) and calls `devicetree ($nerves_disk,gpt2)$nerves_dtb` / `($nerves_disk,gpt3)` before `linux` (grub.cfg:83,88). post-build.sh:50-53 installs the built DTB into $TARGET_DIR/boot/. nerves_defconfig:42-43 builds it via BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_DTS_SUPPORT=y + BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_INTREE_DTS_NAME="qcom/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a".

**Why it matters:** GRUB's `devicetree` replaces the firmware DTB wholesale. The EDK2 DTB carries boot-time state a statically built kernel DTB cannot: the memory map and reserved-memory carveouts that must agree with what XBL/TZ already set up, DDR size/SKU fixups, and the display graph the vendor OS is proven against. A mismatch does not fail cleanly — it surfaces as SMMU faults or ADSP/CDSP remoteproc load failures, i.e. exactly the FastRPC/NPU bring-up we care most about. Additionally our DTB comes from the Deka tree, whose top commits are "dts: qcom: Added dts for deka's dragon board" and "dts: qcom: deka-board double shared dma buf size" — a different board's DTS lineage layered on the Q6A one. Confirmed …

**Action:** Same as proposed, with one fix: do NOT delete the post-build.sh DTB install if you want the on-bench diff reference. Concretely — (a) grub.cfg: delete lines 83 and 88 (`devicetree ($nerves_disk,gptN)$nerves_dtb`), delete line 77 (`set nerves_dtb=...`), rewrite the comment block at lines 11-18 and 24 to state that the SPI-NOR EDK2 firmware hands the kernel its DTB via the EFI config table and the payload must not override it (drop the now-irrelevant Secure Boot note about `devicetree`); (b) post-build.sh: keep the install but move it off the boot path and retitle the comment, e.g. `install -d $TARGET_DIR/usr/share/dtb && install -m 0644 $BINARIES_DIR/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb $TARGET_DIR/usr/share/dtb/` so BRINGUP.md's `dtc -I fs /sys/firmware/devicetree/base` vs our .dtb comparison is possible on target; (c) fix the stale text in fwup.conf:6-9 (and the "kernel + DTB at /boot" notes at :51 and :65), README.md:41-46 and :59, GOAL.md:37 and :77-78 and :106-110, CHANGELOG.md:113. Leave BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_DTS_SUPPORT=y / BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_INTREE_DTS_NAME and the `fdt` entry in BR2_TARGET_GRUB2_BUILTIN_MODULES_EFI untouched.

**Uncertainty:** This is a boot/DTB design decision that overlaps another domain; the "must use the firmware DTB" statement is user-confirmed per project memory (dragon-q6a-dtb-from-uefi), not something I re-derived from a boot log. I have not seen a dump of the actual EDK2 DTB, so I cannot state which nodes it has that our built DTB lacks.

### [MAJOR] Whole DRM/display pipeline must be builtin (=y); we leave DRM=m from the base defconfig and are missing DRM_SIMPLEDRM entirely  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** q6a-display.cfg sets, all =y: CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280, CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP, CONFIG_DRM, CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER, CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE, CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE, CONFIG_DRM_MSM, CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP, CONFIG_QCOM_LLCC, CONFIG_QCOM_OCMEM. The last two carry the comment "msm=y deps (tristate caps): must be builtin for MSM to stay builtin". Vendor qcom_module_defconfig matches and additionally has CONFIG_DRM=y (1684), CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=y (1718), CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y (1719), CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y, CONFIG_FB_EFI=y, and disables all DRM_MSM_DSI_*_PHY plus DRM_MSM_HDMI. Commit 89c038c documents why SIMPLEDRM matters: "CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y — keeps the EFI GOP framebuffer for early console (was: not set, instant black at kernel takeover)" and "CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y — binds the ra620 DP->HDMI bridge so the msm DP chain completes".

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:219-223 has CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m, CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m — the last two are the real headline gap (hardware-proven -110/-19 failure per radxa-q6a-yocto c1ecf57). CONFIG_DRM is never set by us so it stays =m from the base defconfig of our pinned kernel (Deka-Embedded-Linux/linux-dragon-q6a@e05c4fa arch/arm64/configs/defconfig:899); DRM_MSM=m (930), DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m (947), DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m (958). CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM, DRM_AUX_BRIDGE, DRM_MSM_DP, DRM_KMS_HELPER, DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER, DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER are indeed absent from our fragment, and DRM_SIMPLEDRM is absent from the base defconfig. CORRECTION 1: we are NOT without a firmware framebuffer. The base defconfig sets CONFIG_FB=y (991) and CONFIG_FB_EFI=y (992); FB_EFI selects SYSFB (drivers/video/fbdev/Kconfig:436-442), so sysfb registers an "efi-framebuffer" device and efifb drives the EFI GOP today. The "instant black screen at kernel takeover" failure mode does not apply to our baseline, and CONFIG_FB_EFI=y in the p…

**Why it matters:** Three distinct failures. (1) No DRM_SIMPLEDRM means the moment the kernel takes over from EDK2 the screen goes black and stays black — no on-screen evidence of anything, which is also why the Yocto bring-up was blind for several commits. The board's UEFI DTB contains a simple-framebuffer node (confirmed in commit 89c038c), which is what simpledrm binds to. (2) DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE as a module means the radxa,ra620 DP->HDMI bridge never binds early, so the msm DP chain never completes — the DTS graph is hdmi_bridge_in <- usb_1_qmpphy_out_dp and hdmi_bridge_out -> hdmi_connector_in, both endpoints must be bound. (3) DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR as a module leaves the hdmi-connector node unbound. Vendor …

**Action:** Rewrite the GPU/display block in /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment. Replace ONLY lines 219-223 (keep/adjust the comment at 216-218 — it also documents the video/audio module lines at 224-238) with: # --- GPU / display: BUILTIN, mirroring the proven vendor config # (radxa/kernel qcom_module_defconfig) and radxa-q6a-yocto q6a-display.cfg. # gpucc/dispcc MUST be =y: ARM_SMMU is builtin (line 44) and the adreno SMMU # needs gpu_cc clocks + power-domain at its own early probe. With gpucc=m # that probe returns -110 and every later adreno bind fails -19 (proven on # HW, radxa-q6a-yocto commit c1ecf57). CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y CONFIG_DRM=y CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y # Firmware framebuffer for early on-screen console. SYSFB_SIMPLEFB is what # makes sysfb register a "simple-framebuffer" device for simpledrm instead of # an "efi-framebuffer" for efifb; vendor sets it (qcom_module_defconfig:776) # and our base defconfig does not. Without it simpledrm binds nothing, # because our grub.cfg loads the in-tree DTB, which has no # simple-framebuffer node (that node only exists in the EDK2-supplied DTB). CONFIG_SYSFB_SIMPLEFB=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y # radxa,ra620 DP->HDMI bridge + hdmi-connector + qmpphy aux bridge: all three # endpoints of the DTS display graph (qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dts:78-127, # 1319-1320) must bind for msm DP to come up. CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=y CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y # msm=y deps (tristate caps): must be builtin for MSM to stay builtin. # Already =y at fragment:31-32 — keep them there or here, not both. CONFIG_QCOM_LLCC=y CONFIG_QCOM_OCMEM=y # No DSI panel and no native HDMI TX on this board (DP -> ra620), same as # vendor (qcom_module_defconfig:1697-1702). # CONFIG_DRM_MSM_HDMI is not set # CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DSI is not set Drop CONFIG_FB_EFI=y from the proposal: the base defconfig already has CONFIG_FB=y (991) and CONFIG_FB_EFI=y (992), so it changes nothing. Do NOT add CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y again — already at fragment:104, and PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y at :165 matches vendor. Keep CONFIG_SC_LPASS_CORECC_7280=m and the Venus/audio =m lines unchanged. Separately (different domain, but it is what makes SIMPLEDRM moot): decide whether grub.cfg should keep the `devicetree` override at all — per the project's own note the EDK2 DTB is the one the vendor OS proves, and it is the only DTB with the simple-framebuffer node. Verification after build: grep -E 'CONFIG_(D…

**Uncertainty:** CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE is not in the vendor qcom_module_defconfig (it is likely auto-selected by PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO); the Yocto fragment pins it =y, so I recommend pinning it too. I could not confirm whether kconfig will accept DRM_KMS_HELPER/DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER as literal fragment lines on 6.18 (they may be select-only, in which case merge_config warns and olddefconfig resolves them from DRM_MSM=y)…

### [MAJOR] CONFIG_QCOMTEE missing — no /dev/tee0  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Confirmed verbatim. /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-platform.cfg is 162 bytes, 3 lines: # QTEE (Qualcomm TEE): creates /dev/tee0 so qteesupplicant and the TEE # trusted apps can run. Without it the tee0 device wait always times out. CONFIG_QCOMTEE=y Commit 7b95684 "Enable CONFIG_QCOMTEE (tee0 device) + q6a-platform.cfg" adds exactly that file (3 insertions). Commit c7fd105 changes linux-qcom_%.bbappend from `SRC_URI += "file://configs/q6a-display.cfg"` to `SRC_URI += "file://configs/q6a-display.cfg file://configs/q6a-platform.cfg"`. CONFIG_QCOMTEE appears in exactly one place in the whole Yocto tree (that .cfg). Two pieces of history the claim omits, both strengthening it: - dcbf15b (before): "dev-tpm0/tpmrm0/tee0 never appear (QTEE/TPM drivers don't create them); the sequential 90s waits pushed slot-B first boot past the 2min validate window and triggered a spurious rollback." - 3c045e5 (after, the current HEAD state): "Drop inert qtee-off override (pn- name wrong; service runs fine with tee0)" — i.e. once CONFIG_Q…

**Ours today:** Absence is CONFIRMED but the reasoning is wrong. `grep -rni qcomtee` over /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a returns rc=1 (zero hits anywhere: fragment, nerves_defconfig, package/, rootfs_overlay/, patches/, docs). So we genuinely lack the QTEE backend. But the claim's clause "it is not in the arm64 base defconfig either, so we build no TEE driver at all" is FALSE. arch/arm64/configs/defconfig at Deka e05c4fa (fetched, 1825 lines) contains: line 1707: CONFIG_TEE=y line 1708: CONFIG_OPTEE=y line 269: CONFIG_QCOM_TZMEM_MODE_SHMBRIDGE=y We therefore already build the TEE subsystem and the OP-TEE backend; what is missing is specifically the QTEE (Qualcomm) backend. Since QCS6490 firmware is QTEE, not OP-TEE, OPTEE never probes and no /dev/tee0 appears — so the end-state conclusion (no tee0) is right, but "no TEE driver at all" is not.

**Why it matters:** No /dev/tee0 means no Qualcomm TEE access: no qteesupplicant equivalent, no TEE trusted apps, and any future secure-storage / attestation / wrapped-key work is blocked. It is also a prerequisite for the QSEECOM/UEFISECAPP path (efivars on this SoC go through a TZ app). The driver exists in our tree (drivers/tee/qcomtee/Kconfig confirmed present at Deka e05c4fa), so this is purely a missing config line.

**Action:** Valid as proposed — append to /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment: # --- Qualcomm TEE (QTEE): creates /dev/tee0. Base arm64 defconfig ships # CONFIG_TEE=y + CONFIG_OPTEE=y, but QCS6490 runs QTEE, not OP-TEE, so no # tee0 appears without the QCOMTEE backend. # (radxa-q6a-yocto meta-q6a-nerveshub/.../configs/q6a-platform.cfg, sha 7b95684) CONFIG_QCOMTEE=y Validity checks that pass: - Symbol exists in OUR exact kernel (Deka-Embedded-Linux/linux-dragon-q6a@e05c4fa, drivers/tee/qcomtee/Kconfig): `tristate "Qualcomm TEE Support"`, `depends on ARCH_QCOM || COMPILE_TEST`, `depends on !CPU_BIG_ENDIAN`, `select QCOM_SCM`, `select QCOM_TZMEM_MODE_SHMBRIDGE`. - It lives inside `if TEE` in drivers/tee/Kconfig, so it only sticks because CONFIG_TEE=y is already in arch/arm64/configs/defconfig (line 1707) — the base used via BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG (nerves_defconfig:40). merge_config.sh would silently drop the line if TEE were off; it is not, so this works. - No devicetree node needed (important, given the DTB comes from UEFI on this board): drivers/tee/qcomtee/call.c has no of_device_id, only `static const struct platform_device_id qcomtee_ids[] = { { "qcomtee", 0 }, {} };` and drivers/firmware/qcom/qcom_scm.c:2208 `qcom_scm_qtee_init()` does `platform_device_register_data(scm->dev, "qcomtee", ...)` after probing smcinvoke support. CONFIG_QCOM_SCM=y is already at linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:16. - No BR2_ symbol, no systemd dependency. MISSING half of the action, which the claim understates: the kernel line only creates the device node. The Yocto side's consumer is the meta-qcom package `minkipc-qteesupplicant` (systemd service). We have no Buildroot equivalent and none exists upstream, so /dev/tee0 on our system will be inert (no TA loading). If TEE is ever actually needed, the real work item is packaging a supplicant under package/qteesupplicant/ from Qualcomm's minkipc source and launching it from rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh — not the config line.

**Uncertainty:** I did not confirm whether anything in our Nerves userspace currently wants /dev/tee0 — for us this is enablement, not a regression fix.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-platform.cfg · radxa-q6a-yocto git sha 7b95684 and c7fd105 (full diffs read) · radxa/kernel@559f4f92:arch/arm64/configs/qcom_module_defconfig:2301 · Deka-Embedded-Linux/linux-dragon-q6a@e05c4fa:drivers/tee/qcomtee/Kconfig (HTTP 200)</sub>


### [minor] Debug kernel cmdline we still carry was explicitly removed from the proven system  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Verified accurate, with the full sequence being: 9c9498f changed the options line from `... net.ifnames=0 module_blacklist=msm clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` to `... net.ifnames=0` (blob bfa4bc6 -> d1b1c77) as part of the builtin-display commit; the params were then re-added (with the combo PHY appended) and c84ddad removed them a second time (blob f098006 -> d1b1c77), diff line exactly: `-options root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0 clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused module_blacklist=msm,phy_qcom_qmp_combo` / `+options root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0`. Same change in q6a-b.conf. HEAD state of both q6a-a.conf and q6a-b.conf confirms the final proven cmdline as quoted. Also relevant: the third-slot UKI path carries the same string — meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image.bbappend:10 `UKI_CMDLINE = "rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0"` — so there is no other cmdline source in the Yocto tree still passing…

**Ours today:** Correct in substance, two precisions. (a) The cited line is grub.cfg:75, not :74 (lines 71-74 are the comment block; :72-74 is the "bring-up safety nets ... revisit once the driver set is complete" comment). (b) "We have no earlycon" is true only for the hardware boot path: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/grub.cfg.qemu:38 already reads `set nerves_cmdline="console=ttyAMA0,115200 earlycon loglevel=7 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro"`, and BRINGUP.md:84 already documents "If the kernel hangs early: add `earlycon` to the cmdline in `grub.cfg`." So the pattern exists and is already deliberate for QEMU; the gap is that the hardware grub.cfg was never given it. Grep for clk_ignore_unused/pd_ignore_unused across the whole tree returns only grub.cfg:72 and :75 — no other cmdline source, so a single-line edit is the complete fix. Also worth noting for the sequencing argument: our fragment has CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m, CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m (lines 219-223), while CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y (line 165) is already builti…

**Why it matters:** clk_ignore_unused/pd_ignore_unused keep every clock and power domain the bootloader left on, permanently. That masks exactly the class of bug the display work chased (a device whose clock parent is a module that has not loaded) so a broken =m configuration can look half-working, and it prevents any real power management. The proven system runs without them once the display domain is builtin — keeping them means we would never detect that our builtin conversion is incomplete. Missing `earlycon` also means no kernel output before the GENI UART driver registers, which is where a bad DTB/SMMU failure would appear.

**Action:** The action is mechanically valid (a grub.cfg string edit, no BR2_ symbol, no systemd dependency) and `earlycon` will actually work on our kernel: linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:50 sets CONFIG_SERIAL_QCOM_GENI_CONSOLE=y, which selects SERIAL_EARLYCON, and qcom_geni_serial declares OF_EARLYCON_DECLARE for "qcom,geni-debug-uart"; bare `earlycon` then resolves via DT /chosen stdout-path, which our own notes confirm exists (BRINGUP.md:12 and GOAL.md:111: `stdout-path = "serial0:115200n8"`, serial0 = &uart5). Two amendments: (1) add `console=tty1` as a second console to match the proven cmdline, so kernel output also reaches the display once the display block is builtin — this is what makes the display change observable without serial; (2) the note "net.ifnames=0 is handled by vintage_net/erlinit" is the wrong reason — the actual reason we do not need it is that Buildroot's eudev 3.2.14 (BR2_ROOTFS_DEVICE_CREATION_DYNAMIC_EUDEV=y, nerves_defconfig:26) is built with --disable-rule-generator and ships no predictable-naming NAME= rule, so interfaces keep kernel names (eth0) regardless. Resulting line for grub.cfg:75, with the comment at :72-74 deleted in the same commit: set nerves_cmdline="console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 console=tty1 earlycon loglevel=7 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro" Keep the claim's sequencing advice: land it as a separate commit after the =m -> =y display/clock-controller conversion, and be prepared to revert just this one, since these flags are exactly the kind of crutch whose removal can expose a firmware-left-on clock that no builtin driver claims.

**Uncertainty:** Removing pd_ignore_unused on a not-yet-proven driver set could plausibly gate a domain we do need; that is why I recommend it as a separate commit rather than bundled.

<sub>evidence: radxa-q6a-yocto git sha c84ddad (full diff) · radxa-q6a-yocto git sha 9c9498f (commit message) · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:70-74</sub>


### [minor] We enable direct QSPI/SPI-NOR access, but on this board QSPI is TrustZone-managed  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The radxa tree carries "arm64: dts: qcom: qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a: Remove QSPI to make it managed by TrustZone" together with "mtd: devices: Add Qualcomm SCM storage driver" and "firmware: qcom: scm: Add SCM storage interface support"; the vendor config enables CONFIG_MTD_QCOM_SCM_STORAGE=m, CONFIG_MTD=y, CONFIG_MTD_SPI_NOR=y, CONFIG_MTD_BLOCK=m. SPI-NOR content (XBL/UEFI/TZ/HYP/DEVCFG/AOP/CDT) is flashed out-of-band via Radxa's edl-ng (machine conf lines 26-33 blank out every QCOM_BOOT_FIRMWARE variable; meta-q6a-nerveshub ships recipes-bsp/q6a-spi-firmware and edl-ng for on-device flashing).

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:174-181 enables CONFIG_SPI_QCOM_QSPI=y and, under the comment "QSPI NOR flash (winbond w25q256jw)", CONFIG_MTD=y, CONFIG_MTD_SPI_NOR=y, CONFIG_MTD_BLOCK=y.

**Why it matters:** If the firmware-supplied DTB has the QSPI controller removed (as the Radxa DTS does), our spi-qcom-qspi + mtd-spi-nor drivers have nothing to bind to — the config is inert and the comment about w25q256jw is misleading. Any future "read/write the SPI NOR from Linux" plan needs CONFIG_MTD_QCOM_SCM_STORAGE, whose driver is not in the Deka tree at all (it is one of the radxa-only commits). Not boot-blocking, but it is a wrong mental model baked into the fragment.

**Action:** Leave the symbols in place for now (harmless) but correct the comment at linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:178 to say the QSPI controller is TrustZone-managed on this board and the node may be absent from the firmware DTB. If SPI-NOR access from Linux is ever required, it depends on finding 1 (switch to the radxa tree) and then adding: CONFIG_MTD_QCOM_SCM_STORAGE=y On the bench, confirm which way it is by checking for the qspi node in /sys/firmware/devicetree/base/soc@0/ and for /dev/mtd*.

**Uncertainty:** I did not confirm whether the QSPI node is actually absent from the *firmware* DTB (the DTS I read, radxa's in-tree qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dts, is not necessarily the one EDK2 ships).

### [minor] bsp-additions.cfg netfilter/qdisc/bridge/TCP-congestion block is entirely absent from our config  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-qcom-6.18/configs/bsp-additions.cfg is merged into every linux-qcom build and adds ~280 symbols we have none of: the full nftables/iptables/ebtables/conntrack module set (CONFIG_NF_TABLES=m, CONFIG_NF_CONNTRACK=m, CONFIG_NF_NAT=m, CONFIG_NETFILTER_XT_* =m, CONFIG_BRIDGE=m, CONFIG_VETH=m, CONFIG_BRIDGE_VLAN_FILTERING=y, ...), all NET_SCH_* qdiscs =m, CONFIG_TCP_CONG_ADVANCED=y with all TCP_CONG_* =m, CONFIG_PACKET_DIAG=y, CONFIG_NETLINK_DIAG=y, CONFIG_INET_UDP_DIAG=m. It also adds board-relevant lines we already cover (CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y, CONFIG_SCSI_UFS_QCOM=y, CONFIG_BLK_DEV_NVME=y, CONFIG_DMABUF_HEAPS*=y) and three lines for entirely different SoCs (CONFIG_SM_TCSRCC_8750=y, CONFIG_CLK_KAANAPALI_TCSRCC=y, CONFIG_MSM_MMCC_8996=y).

**Ours today:** None of the netfilter/qdisc/bridge/TCP-congestion symbols appear in linux-dragon-q6a.fragment, and the arm64 base defconfig does not set them either. We do independently cover the storage/dmabuf/QMP lines (fragment:83-113).

**Why it matters:** Mostly a non-issue for a single-app headless Nerves device — vintage_net needs wpa_supplicant/iw and DHCP, not nftables. It becomes a real gap only if the application ever wants an on-device firewall, traffic shaping (NET_SCH_*), BBR congestion control, or container/bridge networking. Conversely: the three foreign-SoC clock symbols the Yocto side builds in (SM_TCSRCC_8750, CLK_KAANAPALI_TCSRCC, MSM_MMCC_8996) are pure bloat for sc7280 and must NOT be copied.

**Action:** Do not bulk-copy bsp-additions.cfg. If/when needed, add a small explicit block to /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment rather than the whole thing, e.g. for BBR + basic nftables: CONFIG_TCP_CONG_ADVANCED=y CONFIG_TCP_CONG_BBR=m CONFIG_NF_CONNTRACK=m CONFIG_NF_TABLES=m CONFIG_NF_TABLES_INET=y CONFIG_NFT_CT=m CONFIG_NFT_NAT=m CONFIG_NF_NAT=m CONFIG_NF_NAT_MASQUERADE=y and pair it with the matching userspace (BR2_PACKAGE_NFTABLES) in nerves_defconfig. Explicitly do NOT add CONFIG_SM_TCSRCC_8750, CONFIG_CLK_KAANAPALI_TCSRCC or CONFIG_MSM_MMCC_8996.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the target application needs any of these is a product decision I cannot make.

### [minor] Audio machine driver and TEE/QSEECOM symbols: our choices are right, but the QSEECOM allowlist patch is tree-gated  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The board DTS sets the sound card compatible to "qcom,qcs6490-rb3gen2-sndcard" with model "QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A" and DAI links onto &q6apmbedai / &mdss_dp — i.e. the AudioReach (q6apm) path via the sc8280xp machine driver. Vendor qcom_module_defconfig builds both CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC8280XP=m and CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC7280=m, CONFIG_QCOM_APR=y, CONFIG_SOUNDWIRE_QCOM=m, CONFIG_PINCTRL_SC7280_LPASS_LPI=m, CONFIG_SC_LPASS_CORECC_7280=m. The base defconfig already has CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM=y and CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM_UEFISECAPP=y (lines 270-271), and the radxa tree additionally carries "firmware: qcom: scm: Allow QSEECOM for Radxa Dragon Q6A" to add the board to the SCM allowlist.

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:224-236 sets CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC8280XP=m, CONFIG_QCOM_APR=m, CONFIG_SOUNDWIRE_QCOM=m, CONFIG_SND_SOC_LPASS_{RX,TX,VA}_MACRO=m, CONFIG_SND_SOC_WCD938X{,_SDW}=m, CONFIG_PINCTRL_SC7280_LPASS_LPI=m, CONFIG_SC_LPASS_CORECC_7280=m. We do not set CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC7280 and we do not set QSEECOM symbols.

**Why it matters:** Confirming rather than fixing: SND_SOC_SC8280XP is the correct driver (sound/soc/qcom/sc8280xp.c matches qcom,qcs6490-rb3gen2-sndcard) and it `select SND_SOC_QDSP6` which selects SND_SOC_QDSP6_APM — so our QCOM_APR=m chain does reach AudioReach and CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC7280 is genuinely unnecessary. QSEECOM/UEFISECAPP come free from the base defconfig, but without the radxa allowlist commit the SCM layer will refuse QSEECOM on compatible "radxa,dragon-q6a" / "qcom,qcm6490", so EFI variable access (efivarfs) will not work on our current tree. That matters only if we ever want efivars-based boot control — our A/B design uses grubenv, so today it is informational.

**Action:** No audio config change required. Do not add CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC7280. If efivars access is ever wanted, it is another argument for finding 1 (switch to the radxa tree, which carries the allowlist patch); no fragment line can substitute for it. Optionally pin what the base already gives us so it cannot drift, in /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment: CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM=y CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM_UEFISECAPP=y CONFIG_EFIVAR_FS=y

**Uncertainty:** I did not confirm on hardware that QSEECOM currently fails on our tree; the conclusion is from the existence of the radxa allowlist commit plus mainline's per-machine QSEECOM allowlist design.

### [info] The proven base config is the mainline arm64 defconfig, not the vendor qcom_module_defconfig — and the base is identical in both trees  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-qcom_6.18.bb:43 sets KBUILD_DEFCONFIG ?= "defconfig" and do_configure:prepend (lines 53-57) copies ${S}/arch/arm64/configs/defconfig to .config then runs scripts/kconfig/merge_config.sh -m over KBUILD_CONFIG_EXTRA plus the recipe's .cfg fragments. Our layer's bbappend (lines 11-13) clears KBUILD_CONFIG_EXTRA and :remove's the inherited prune.config / qcom.config appends because those files do not exist in the radxa tree (confirmed: prune.config -> HTTP 404). So the proven final config = arch/arm64/configs/defconfig + bsp-additions.cfg + q6a-display.cfg + q6a-platform.cfg. Radxa's own qcom_module_defconfig (2618 lines) is NOT used by the Yocto build — it is only the source the =y decisions were copied from (commit c1ecf57 cites "vendor's config-6.18.2-4-qcom").

**Ours today:** nerves_defconfig:40 BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y + nerves_defconfig:41 BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_CONFIG_FRAGMENT_FILES pointing at linux-dragon-q6a.fragment — structurally the same scheme (arch defconfig + fragment merge).

**Why it matters:** This is the fact that makes the tree switch safe and makes symbol-by-symbol comparison meaningful: I fetched arch/arm64/configs/defconfig from both radxa@559f4f92 and Deka@e05c4fa and they are byte-identical (1825 lines, `diff` clean). So our fragment layers on exactly the same base the proven system layers on, and every difference in the final config is a difference in the fragments — no hidden base drift to worry about when repinning the tree.

**Action:** No change needed; keep BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y. Do NOT switch to the vendor qcom_module_defconfig via BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_CUSTOM_CONFIG — the proven system does not use it, and it enables a large amount of irrelevant hardware (all Amlogic/other-SoC LPASS pinctrl variants, WCD934X/WCD9335 codecs, NVMe target stack, WSA88x speaker amps). Record in nerves_defconfig's kernel comment block that the base is arch/arm64/configs/defconfig and that it is identical across the two candidate trees.

### [info] realtek-eth-8169.cfg is dead code in the proven build — ethernet runs as a module there; our R8169=y is a deliberate improvement  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** meta-qcom-3rdparty/recipes-kernel/linux/radxa-dragon-q6a/realtek-eth-8169.cfg sets CONFIG_NET_SELFTESTS=y, CONFIG_R8169=y, CONFIG_MDIO_BUS=y, CONFIG_PHYLIB=y, CONFIG_FIXED_PHY=y, CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y, # CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY_HWMON is not set, CONFIG_FWNODE_MDIO=y, CONFIG_OF_MDIO=y, CONFIG_ACPI_MDIO=y. BUT it is wired in only by meta-qcom-3rdparty/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-qcom-next_git.bbappend (SRC_URI:append:radxa-dragon-q6a), and kas overrides PREFERRED_PROVIDER_virtual/kernel to linux-qcom — so that bbappend never applies. There is no linux-qcom_%.bbappend in meta-qcom-3rdparty/recipes-kernel (directory listing verified). The proven build therefore ships CONFIG_R8169=m (base defconfig:379) with CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y (base defconfig:403), loaded by systemd/udev.

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:153 CONFIG_R8169=y, with the comment "(arch defconfig has =m)". We do not pin REALTEK_PHY/PHYLIB/OF_MDIO, but the base defconfig already has CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y.

**Why it matters:** Our =y is the right call for a Nerves system (no early module loading; NervesHub needs the network up before anything user-space-driven runs) and is strictly stronger than what the proven system does. Worth recording so nobody "fixes" it back to =m to match Yocto. The one caveat is that the r8169 ASPM workaround commit ("r8169: Remove the check of a register to enable ASPM on Radxa Dragon Q6A") lives only in the radxa tree.

**Action:** Keep CONFIG_R8169=y. For robustness against future base-defconfig drift, pin the PHY plumbing explicitly next to it in /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment: CONFIG_R8169=y CONFIG_PHYLIB=y CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y CONFIG_MDIO_BUS=y CONFIG_FWNODE_MDIO=y CONFIG_OF_MDIO=y # CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY_HWMON is not set

**Uncertainty:** I inferred the bbappend does not apply from the recipe-name mismatch (linux-qcom-next_git.bbappend vs PN linux-qcom) and the directory listing; I did not read a bitbake -e dump to confirm.

### [info] CONFIG_CMA_SIZE_MBYTES=512 and CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y are unproven deviations from the reference  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Neither the base defconfig nor any fragment changes CMA sizing: CONFIG_CMA_SIZE_MBYTES stays at 32 (base defconfig; vendor qcom_module_defconfig:2571 also 32, with CONFIG_DMA_CMA=y). bsp-additions.cfg enables the dmabuf heaps (DMABUF_HEAPS/SYSTEM/CMA =y) but not a larger CMA pool. No fragment sets CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM; the vendor config sets only CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y, CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_V3=y and CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM_DEBUG=y.

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:80-88 sets CONFIG_DMA_CMA=y, CONFIG_CMA=y and CONFIG_CMA_SIZE_MBYTES=512 with a reasoned comment about the mainline sc7280 fastrpc dma_heap model. fragment:45-46 sets CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y and CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y.

**Why it matters:** CMA_SIZE_MBYTES=512 is a defensible, documented deviation (FastRPC/QNN buffers come from the CMA dma_heap) but it is NOT what the proven system runs, so if we hit early-boot memory problems it is one of the first things to bisect. It also permanently reserves 512 MB. CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM is a def_bool symbol (def_bool y when ARM_SMMU && ARCH_QCOM), not user-selectable, so the fragment line is a no-op that merge_config may warn about — harmless but misleading.

**Action:** Keep CONFIG_CMA_SIZE_MBYTES=512 for now but note it as a deviation in the fragment comment and prefer tuning it at runtime via the `cma=` cmdline arg in grub.cfg during bench bring-up rather than rebuilding. Optionally drop the no-op line CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y from linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:46 (or leave it with a comment that it is def_bool). Consider adding, to match the vendor's UFS inline-crypto posture once on the radxa tree: CONFIG_SCSI_UFS_CRYPTO=y

**Uncertainty:** CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM being non-prompting is from my knowledge of drivers/iommu/arm/arm-smmu/Kconfig, not from reading that file in these trees.
