---

## display (DSI/HDMI/DP), GPU (Adreno 635/643 + a660 firmware), graphics userspace

The proven Yocto system and ours agree on the hardware picture (HDMI comes out of MDSS DP → QMP combo PHY → a `radxa,ra620` simple-bridge → `hdmi-connector`; no DSI panel; GPU is `qcom,adreno-635.0` with a660-family firmware), and our firmware blob set is byte-identical in content and path layout to the linux-firmware packages Yocto installs. The difference that matters is module-vs-builtin. Yocto's hard-won fix (commit c1ecf57) makes gpucc, dispcc, DRM, DRM_MSM, the simple-bridge and PHY_QCOM_QMP builtin because the *builtin* adreno SMMU needs five gpucc clocks plus GPU_CC_CX_GDSC at its early probe; with gpucc=m it times out at -110 and every later adreno bind returns -19. Our fragment has exactly the broken shape (`SC_GPUCC_7280=m`, `SC_DISPCC_7280=m`, `DRM_MSM=m`, bridges =m, and the arch defconfig contributes `CONFIG_DRM=m`), and our situation is strictly worse than Yocto's: Nerves …


### [BLOCKER] BLOCKER: SC_GPUCC_7280 / SC_DISPCC_7280 are =m — this is the adreno SMMU -110 failure Yocto fixed  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg pins `CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y` and `CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y`. Commit c1ecf57 ("Display: builtin gpucc/dispcc/msm like vendor — fixes adreno SMMU -110") states the root cause verbatim: "SC_GPUCC_7280=m meant the adreno SMMU had no clocks/power at its early probe (fail -110), dooming every later adreno bind (-19). Vendor's config-6.18.2-4-qcom builtins SC_GPUCC_7280, SC_DISPCC_7280, DRM_MSM, SIMPLE_BRIDGE, PHY_QCOM_QMP." The mechanism is visible in the DT: sc7280.dtsi `adreno_smmu` (compatible "qcom,sc7280-smmu-500","qcom,adreno-smmu") takes 5 clocks from &gpucc (GPU_CC_AHB_CLK, GPU_CC_HLOS1_VOTE_GPU_SMMU_CLK, GPU_CC_CX_GMU_CLK, GPU_CC_HUB_CX_INT_CLK, GPU_CC_HUB_AON_CLK) plus 2 from &gcc, and `power-domains = <&gpucc GPU_CC_CX_GDSC>`. ARM_SMMU is bool (always builtin), so a modular gpucc provider can never satisfy it in time: driver_deferred_probe_check_state() returns -ETIMEDOUT (=-110) after the deferred-probe timeout, the SMMU is dropped from the deferred list, and the gpu node (`iommus = <&adreno_smmu 0 0x400>`) can the…

**Ours today:** /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:222-223 has `CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m` and `CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m`; a repo-wide grep (excluding deps/, example/deps/, .git/) finds them nowhere else, patches/ is empty, and nerves_defconfig:41 declares a single fragment file, so nothing overrides them. CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y and CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y are at fragment:44-45. The claim's assertion that the arm64 arch defconfig also has these =m could NOT be verified (the kernel tarball for Deka-Embedded-Linux/linux-dragon-q6a @e05c4fa is not unpacked on disk) and is not load-bearing: BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_CONFIG_FRAGMENT_FILES is merged after the arch defconfig, so our explicit =m demotes the symbol regardless of the defconfig value. The gap is BROADER than claimed: lines 219-221 also leave `CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m`, `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m`, `CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m`, and the fragment contains NO occurrence of CONFIG_DRM=, DRM_KMS_HELPER, DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER, DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER, DRM_SIMPLEDRM, DRM_AUX_BRIDGE or DRM_MSM_DP — all of which the proven Yocto con…

**Why it matters:** Identical dead-GPU/black-screen failure as Yocto pre-c1ecf57, and worse for us: our modules are only loaded when udevd starts from erlinit `--pre-run-exec /usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh`, i.e. after the whole BEAM boot. The kernel's deferred_probe_timeout (10 s when CONFIG_MODULES=y) will always have expired by then, so no amount of later modprobing revives the adreno SMMU. GPU is dead (no /dev/dri render node usable by Mesa) and MDSS/DP bring-up is compromised.

**Action:** Edit /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment: DELETE lines 219-223 (CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m, CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m) and replace with the full vendor-equivalent builtin block — mirroring the proven q6a-display.cfg, not just the two clock controllers: # --- Display/GPU: builtin, mirroring the proven Yocto system # (meta-q6a-nerveshub/.../q6a-display.cfg, commits 89c038c + c1ecf57) and # the Radxa vendor kernel config-6.18.2-4-qcom. # Root cause this fixes: the adreno SMMU (ARM_SMMU=y, builtin, line 44) # takes 5 gpucc clocks + GPU_CC_CX_GDSC at its early probe. With gpucc as a # module it can only arrive when qcom-coldplug.sh starts udevd -- after the # whole BEAM boot, long past deferred_probe_timeout -- so the SMMU latches # -ETIMEDOUT, is dropped from the deferred list and never retried, and every # adreno bind then fails -ENODEV. Same reasoning applies to msm and the ra620 # DP->HDMI simple-bridge: as modules they miss the bring-up window entirely. CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y CONFIG_DRM=y CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y Already correct elsewhere in the fragment, do not duplicate: CONFIG_QCOM_LLCC=y / CONFIG_QCOM_OCMEM=y (lines 31-32, the tristate dep caps that would otherwise force DRM_MSM back to =m), CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y (104), CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y (165), CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y / CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y (44-45). CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR: Yocto pins DRM_AUX_BRIDGE instead; keep DISPLAY_CONNECTOR only if the EDK2-supplied DTB actually has a display-connector node, otherwise drop it rather than leaving it =m. Verify after building that the final .config really has these as y (run `grep -E 'SC_(GPU|DISP)CC_7280|DRM_MSM|SIMPLE_BRIDGE' build/linux-*/.config`): olddefconfig silently demotes =y to =m when a dependency is tristate, which is the trap already documented at fragment lines 11-13 for QRTR. Optional, not a substitute: append `deferred_probe_timeout=30` to `set nerves_cmdline=` at grub.cfg:75 as a safety net for the late-udev design. Separately consider dropping `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` from that same line — Yocto removed those debug params in 9c9498f once the display pipeline was builtin.

**Uncertainty:** The exact -110 site (of_iommu vs genpd attach) was not read in the driver-core source; the numeric evidence (-110 then -19) and the DT dependency chain are consistent and Yocto's fix is empirical.

Note commit 89c038c's body says "QMP-combo PHY + aux-bridge stay modules exactly like vendor", which the current cfg contradicts (PHY_QCOM_Q…

**Ours today:** Confirmed and extended. linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:219-221 = CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m / CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m / CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m; those are the ONLY CONFIG_DRM* lines in the entire repo, and nerves_defconfig:41 loads exactly one fragment, so CONFIG_DRM is never set by us. Deka tree @e05c4fa arch/arm64/configs/defconfig: CONFIG_DRM=m (899), DRM_MSM=m (930), DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m (947), DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m (958); DRM_MSM_DP, DRM_AUX_BRIDGE, DRM_SIMPLEDRM absent. QCOM_LLCC=y/QCOM_OCMEM=y already at fragment:31-32 (defconfig has them =m at 1515-1516) — correct, and QCOM_AOSS_QMP=y (33) + QCOM_COMMAND_DB=y (16) satisfy the other two tristate caps in msm/Kconfig:10-13. ALSO MISSING, same domain, not mentioned by the claim: (a) fragment:222-223 CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m and CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m — the exact symbols the Yocto file blames for dead HDMI + adreno -19/-110; the Deka defconfig has them =m too (1408, 1417). (b) No CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM / SYSFB / FB_* symbol anywhere in our fragment, so nothing takes over the UEFI GOP framebuffer that grub.cfg boots through. (c) fragment:…

**Why it matters:** Two independent breakages. (1) With DRM_MSM=y (which we need for a deterministic display) and the bridge as a module, msm_dp's drm_of_find_panel_or_bridge returns -EPROBE_DEFER forever and hits the same -110 timeout — no HDMI. (2) Leaving everything =m means display only exists after erlinit starts udevd, and CONFIG_DRM=m also caps other symbols (see the QMP-combo finding). Firmware timing is NOT an argument against builtin: msm_drv.c's msm_open() calls load_gpu() with the comment "For now, load gpu on open.. to avoid the requirement of having firmware in the initrd", so a660_sqe.fw/a660_gmu.bin are only requested on the first /dev/dri open, long after the squashfs root is mounted.

**Action:** Replace linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:219-223 (note: 223, not 221 — the two clock controllers must move too) with the block below. Dropped the 9 non-prompted symbols from the original proposal (DRM_KMS_HELPER, DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER, DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER, DRM_BRIDGE, DRM_BRIDGE_CONNECTOR, DRM_PANEL_BRIDGE, DRM_AUX_BRIDGE, DRM_MSM_KMS, DRM_MSM_MDSS): they have no Kconfig prompt at this sha, so olddefconfig recomputes them from selects and merge_config.sh only emits "value requested ... not in final .config" noise. Added the two clock controllers and SIMPLEDRM, which the original omitted. # Display/GPU builtin, mirroring the Yocto/vendor kernel. Two proven # root causes on hardware: gpucc/dispcc as modules starve the adreno # SMMU at early probe (-110, every later adreno bind fails), and no # SIMPLEDRM means the UEFI GOP handover goes black. CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y CONFIG_DRM=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DPU=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y # no DSI panel and no direct-HDMI block on this board # CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DSI is not set # CONFIG_DRM_MSM_HDMI is not set # CONFIG_DRM_MSM_MDP4 is not set # CONFIG_DRM_MSM_MDP5 is not set Keep QCOM_LLCC=y / QCOM_OCMEM=y (fragment:31-32) and QCOM_AOSS_QMP=y (33) / QCOM_COMMAND_DB=y (16) — all four are `depends on X || X=n` caps in msm/Kconfig:10-13 and any of them at =m silently drops DRM_MSM back to =m. CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y (fragment:165) only becomes real once CONFIG_DRM=y, and it is what selects DRM_AUX_BRIDGE, so no explicit AUX_BRIDGE line is needed. Also: CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y is only useful with a console attached — check that CONFIG_VT/CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE end up =y in the resolved config (neither appears in our fragment today) and that grub.cfg's `linux` line does not need a console= addition; Yocto's counterpart change added console=tty1 to its loader entries in the same commit (89c038c). Verification stays as proposed but must include the clock controllers, which the original grep listed while the action never changed them: grep -E 'CONFIG_(DRM|DRM_SIMPLEDRM|DRM_MSM|DRM_MSM_DP|DRM_MSM_DPU|DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE|DRM_AUX_BRIDGE|PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO|SC_GPUCC_7280|SC_DISPCC_7280|QCOM_LLCC|QCOM_OCMEM)=' <buildroot-output>/build/linux-*/.config

**Uncertainty:** DRM_MSM_KMS is `def_bool n` and only becomes y via DRM_MSM_DPU's select — writing CONFIG_DRM_MSM_KMS=y in a fragment is harmless but not what sets it; keep DRM_MSM_DPU=y as the real switch.

### [BLOCKER] MAJOR (design decision): no compositor and no systemd — and Yocto's 'working display' is a text console, not a validated GL path  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Accurate as written, but incomplete on the crucial point. Yocto's weston disable is explicitly DEBUG bring-up scaffolding (`weston-off: SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE:pn-weston-init = "disable"`, comment "DEBUG (bring-up)"; image adds getty@tty1 for the same reason) — temporary, not architectural. The real on-hardware display history is in the git log: 89c038c fixed a black HDMI screen by making CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y and CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y (RA620 DP->HDMI bridge) with DRM/KMS/MSM/LLCC/OCMEM builtin, and c1ecf57 (2026-07-24, HEAD-adjacent) fixed the Adreno itself: "SC_GPUCC_7280=m meant the adreno SMMU had no clocks/power at its early probe (fail -110), dooming every later adreno bind (-19)". The resulting known-good set is meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg: SC_GPUCC_7280=y, SC_DISPCC_7280=y, PHY_QCOM_QMP=y, DRM=y, DRM_KMS_HELPER=y, DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y, DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y, DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y, DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y, DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y, DRM_MSM=y, DRM_MSM_DP=y, QCOM_LLCC=y, QCOM_OCMEM=y. Also note both mesa-vulkan-drivers (turnip) and libopencl-mesa/clinfo/vulk…

**Ours today:** nerves_defconfig:93-104 is confirmed exactly as claimed (libdrm + tests, mesa3d freedreno gallium, EGL, GLES, GBM, kmscube; no wayland/X11/compositor/Vulkan/OpenCL, BR2_INIT_NONE). Nothing equivalent to glmark2/mesa3d-demos/eglinfo exists anywhere in the tree. Two additions the claim missed: (a) linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:219-223 currently reproduces Yocto's proven-broken display config — CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m, CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m, no CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM/DRM_AUX_BRIDGE/DRM_MSM_DP (QCOM_LLCC=y and QCOM_OCMEM=y at lines 31-32 are already right). So the GL path is expected to fail with adreno SMMU -110 / bind -19 on real hardware, not merely to be untested. (b) Vulkan and OpenCL are not just unselected but unavailable: Buildroot 2026.05 mesa3d offers no freedreno Vulkan driver, and GALLIUM_DRIVER_FREEDRENO does not select MESA3D_RUSTICL_SUPPORTED_DRIVER, so BR2_PACKAGE_MESA3D_OPENCL cannot be enabled.

**Why it matters:** Two things to surface rather than paper over. (1) weston/wayland has no clean Nerves analogue (systemd unit + seat management + systemd-notify module); the correct Nerves design is direct GBM/KMS + EGL/GLES2 from the app (scenic_driver_local), which is what we already build — so this is a deliberate divergence, not a gap to close. (2) Because weston autostart is disabled on the proven Yocto image, its on-hardware evidence covers KMS modeset + fbcon on HDMI only. Nobody has demonstrated EGL/GLES rendering through freedreno on this board in either system, so our GL stack remains unvalidated even after we copy Yocto's kernel config.

**Action:** Keep the compositor-free design (record it in GOAL.md as a decision — weston/wayland has no clean Nerves analogue: weston.service + weston-start.sh --modules=systemd-notify.so + logind seat management all assume systemd). Sequence the rest: 1. PREREQUISITE (file as its own blocker finding, in the kernel-config domain): port meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg into linux-dragon-q6a.fragment, replacing lines 219-223 — CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y, CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y, CONFIG_DRM=y, CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y, CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y, CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y, CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y, CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y (keep QCOM_LLCC=y/QCOM_OCMEM=y), and delete the "Modules: not boot-critical, probed via udev coldplug" comment for the display block — Yocto disproved it. Testing GL before this lands is wasted effort. 2. Then close the test gap in nerves_defconfig: BR2_PACKAGE_GLMARK2=y BR2_PACKAGE_MESA3D_DEMOS=y Both symbols verified present in deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05, and both selectable with our config (HAS_LIBEGL/HAS_LIBGLES/HAS_LIBGBM from mesa3d, HAS_UDEV from BR2_ROOTFS_DEVICE_CREATION_DYNAMIC_EUDEV=y at nerves_defconfig:26, BR2_INSTALL_LIBSTDCPP from BR2_TOOLCHAIN_EXTERNAL_CXX=y). glmark2 auto-selects only the drm-glesv2 flavour (no X11/Wayland deps pulled in); note it also installs ~15-20 MB of texture/model data under /usr/share/glmark2, which matters for squashfs size. 3. Bench acceptance (corrected commands): `modetest -M msm -c` must list a connected HDMI connector; `kmscube -D /dev/dri/card0` must draw (NOT `/dev/dri/msm` — that path does not exist; kmscube -D takes a DRM node); `glmark2-es2-drm` must score; `es2_info` / `eglinfo` (from mesa3d-demos, the only source — Buildroot 2026.05 has no standalone eglinfo package) must report the freedreno renderer string. 4. Surface as an explicit non-goal, not silently: Vulkan (turnip) and OpenCL/rusticl are not reachable in Buildroot 2026.05 for freedreno, so Yocto's mesa-vulkan-drivers / libopencl-mesa / clinfo / vulkan-tools cannot be matched without a custom package. Confirm nothing in the Elixir app needs Vulkan before accepting that.

### [minor] MAJOR: CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y in our fragment silently resolves to =m (DRM/TYPEC tristate caps)  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Confirmed as stated, with one correction: q6a-display.cfg pins only `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y` and its header says "Vendor builtins the whole display domain; QMP combo/USB phys stay modules (vendor-proven)." 89c038c created the file with `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y` (while its own commit message already said combo stays a module); c1ecf57 removed it. c84ddad separately removed `module_blacklist=msm,phy_qcom_qmp_combo` from q6a-a.conf/q6a-b.conf. NOTE: Yocto's own `CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y` in that .cfg is also ineffective — DRM_AUX_BRIDGE is a promptless tristate (drivers/gpu/drm/bridge/Kconfig:15-23) whose value comes only from selects, and with PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO capped at m by TYPEC=m the select gives m. So Yocto's resolved aux bridge is =m, not =y.

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:165 does set `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y`, and it does resolve to =m: pinned tree drivers/phy/qualcomm/Kconfig:59-67 has `depends on TYPEC || TYPEC=n` and `depends on DRM || DRM=n`, while arch/arm64/configs/defconfig (our base, nerves_defconfig:40) has CONFIG_TYPEC=m (1173) and CONFIG_DRM=m (899). Two corrections: (a) it is not fully silent — buildroot-2026.05/package/pkg-kconfig.mk:67 runs `merge_config.sh -m`, which prints "Value of CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO is redefined by fragment" because the defconfig-generated base .config already has =m; what is silent is the post-`olddefconfig` revert. (b) the -110 scenario does not apply to us: dwc3-qcom with a modular PHY gets -EPROBE_DEFER and is retried when qcom-coldplug.sh loads phy-qcom-qmp-combo, and our DRM_MSM is =m (fragment:219) so there is no builtin mdss_dp consumer. Net functional effect of this line today: none — our resolved value already equals the hardware-proven Yocto/vendor value.

**Why it matters:** We believe we have a builtin USB3+DP combo PHY and we do not. It is not fatal for the display (the vendor ships it as a module and msm DP simply defers until it loads), but it is fatal to reasoning: the same silent demotion pattern is what bit us with QRTR, and with our late-udev design a builtin consumer of the combo PHY (usb_1 dwc3, mdss_dp when DRM_MSM=y) can latch -110 before coldplug.

**Action:** Documentation/hygiene fix only. In /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment replace line 165 with: # QMP combo PHY (USB3 + DP) is a MODULE, like the vendor and Yocto kernels. # Kconfig: depends on TYPEC || TYPEC=n and DRM || DRM=n; the arm64 defconfig # has TYPEC=m and DRM=m, so =y is impossible without also forcing both to =y. # dwc3-qcom probe-defers until qcom-coldplug.sh loads phy-qcom-qmp-combo. CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=m Do NOT adopt this finding's "alternative" (DRM=y + TYPEC=y) on its own merits — a builtin combo PHY is not what the proven board runs. If CONFIG_DRM=y is adopted from the separate display finding, adding CONFIG_TYPEC=y purely to make the combo PHY builtin is a deviation from the vendor-proven set and should be rejected. Also drop the claim that DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y follows from `select DRM_AUX_BRIDGE if DRM_BRIDGE`; it does not, and copying Yocto's `CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y` line into our fragment would be a no-op. Separately worth adding to the build checklist: grep the resolved output/build/linux-*/.config for every symbol our fragment forces to =y and diff against the fragment, since merge_config's redefinition warnings are the only signal and they scroll past.

**Uncertainty:** Whether msm DP with a modular combo PHY survives our very late module load has not been demonstrated on hardware — Yocto proved it with systemd-udev starting within ~2 s of boot, we start udevd after the BEAM. If HDMI is still dead after making gpucc/dispcc/DRM builtin, making TYPEC=y + COMBO=y is the next experiment.

### [minor] MAJOR: no on-screen output at all — no simpledrm, no fbcon, no console=tty1  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** q6a-display.cfg:13 (not :14) `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y`; commit 89c038c justifies it verbatim as "keeps the EFI GOP framebuffer for early console (was: not set, instant black at kernel takeover)". Crucially the same commit states the EDK2-supplied DTB contains a `simple-framebuffer` node (Yocto ships no DTB and never overrides it), so on the Yocto side simpledrm binds a DT node. console=tty1 is in esp-qcom-image.bbappend:10 `UKI_CMDLINE = "rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0"` (the claim omitted `earlycon` here) and in q6a-a.conf:4 / q6a-b.conf:4 as quoted; q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:112-116 symlinks getty@tty1.service, explicitly marked "DEBUG (bring-up)".

**Ours today:** We already have a firmware-framebuffer console path. Pinned kernel Deka-Embedded-Linux/linux-dragon-q6a @ e05c4fa is Linux 6.18.0; arch/arm64/configs/defconfig:991-996 sets CONFIG_FB=y, CONFIG_FB_EFI=y (built-in efifb, and FB_EFI `select SYSFB`), CONFIG_FB_MODE_HELPERS=y, CONFIG_LOGO=y. Because CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m in our fragment pulls in DRM_CLIENT_SELECTION=m (drivers/gpu/drm/msm/Kconfig:66), CONFIG_DRM_FBDEV_EMULATION defaults y (`default FB`) and CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE defaults y (`default DRM_FBDEV_EMULATION`) after olddefconfig — their absence from the minimised defconfig does not mean they are off. What we genuinely lack: (a) `console=tty1` anywhere (grub.cfg:75 is serial-only; whole-repo grep for tty1 returns nothing outside BRINGUP.md/test/grub.cfg.qemu earlycon mentions), (b) loglevel=4 hides KERN_INFO probe output, (c) any getty at all (BR2_INIT_NONE=y, nerves_defconfig:21), and (d) DRM itself is =m so no DRM-based early console (simpledrm/efidrm) can be built in. Also note grub.cfg:82/87 override the EDK2 DTB with the mainline in-tree DTB, which has no `simple-framebuffe…

**Why it matters:** Display bring-up on the bench becomes unobservable: with the EFI GOP dropped at kernel takeover and no fbcon, a *successful* msm/DP modeset and a *failed* one look identical (black screen). Yocto's entire display debugging loop depended on tty1 + persistent journal. We have no journal either, so serial + on-screen console is all we get.

**Action:** 1) grub.cfg:75 — the real, valid fix (bring-up only): set nerves_cmdline="console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused loglevel=7 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro" (keep ttyMSM0 first; erlinit already pins IEx with `-c ttyMSM0`. Do not drop clk_ignore_unused/pd_ignore_unused as the claim's rewrite silently did.) 2) linux-dragon-q6a.fragment — pin only what is real, and do NOT add SYSFB_SIMPLEFB: # On-screen console. FB_EFI=y and FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE/DRM_FBDEV_EMULATION are # already on via arm64 defconfig + Kconfig defaults; pinned here so an # upstream default flip cannot silently take HDMI text output away. CONFIG_FB=y CONFIG_FB_EFI=y CONFIG_DRM_FBDEV_EMULATION=y CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE=y # Do NOT set CONFIG_SYSFB_SIMPLEFB=y: it makes sysfb publish a # "simple-framebuffer" device that built-in efifb cannot claim # (drivers/firmware/Kconfig SYSFB_SIMPLEFB help), and DRM_SIMPLEDRM would # only be =m while CONFIG_DRM=m -> net loss of the EFI GOP console. 3) If a DRM-based (not fbdev) firmware console is actually wanted, it is gated on making DRM built-in, which is the separate display finding anyway. Only then: CONFIG_DRM=y CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_EFIDRM=y # 6.18 successor to efifb for EFI GOP; requires !SYSFB_SIMPLEFB (CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y is pointless for us: it needs a DT `simple-framebuffer` node, and grub.cfg replaces the EDK2 DTB with the mainline DTB that has none. Either way, with CONFIG_DRM=m today both symbols are demoted to =m by merge_config.sh + olddefconfig.) 4) Design decision to surface, not paper over: Yocto's HDMI *login prompt* comes from systemd getty@tty1. We have BR2_INIT_NONE and no getty. To get a shell on HDMI we must either spawn `/sbin/getty 0 tty1` from /usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh (erlinit --pre-run-exec) with BR2_PACKAGE_BUSYBOX getty enabled in busybox.fragment, or point erlinit `-c tty1` and lose IEx on serial. Kernel messages on tty1 (step 1) are free; an interactive console is not.

**Uncertainty:** On arm64 DT boot, simpledrm binds either the EDK2 DTB's `simple-framebuffer` node (which Yocto commit 89c038c says the firmware DTB has) or a sysfb-synthesized device from the EFI stub's screen_info. Our own DTB has no simple-framebuffer node, so with our current `devicetree` override the SYSFB_SIMPLEFB path is the only one — untested here.

### [minor] MAJOR: /dev/dri card enumeration is ambiguous once simpledrm and msm coexist  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** meta-qcom ships a generic, family-wide (`:qcom` override) weston helper — `meta-qcom/recipes-graphics/wayland/weston-init/weston-start.sh` — that walks `/dev/dri/by-path/platform-*-card`, keeps only cards with connectors under `/sys/class/drm/${card_name}-*`, sorts by platform address, and passes every card AFTER the first to weston as `--additional-devices=`. Its own comments state the primary is chosen by weston, not by the script ("First card in sorted list is primary (Weston will auto-select it)"), so it is a multi-KMS-device aggregator, not a simpledrm-vs-msm disambiguator. It is wired in only through systemd: `weston-init/additional-devices.conf` is a `weston.service.d` drop-in that clears `ExecStart=` and re-points it at `weston-start.sh`. `weston-init.bbappend` sets `DEFAULTBACKEND:qcom ?= "drm"` (weak default). Crucially it is NOT active on the proven-working Q6A image: `kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml:74-76` sets `SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE:pn-weston-init = "disable"` and `q6a-nerveshub-image.bb` symlinks `getty@tty1.service` into `getty.target.wants` — the reference board's proven di…

**Ours today:** Correct that we have no DRM/DRI device selection anywhere: `rootfs_overlay/` contains only `etc/udev/rules.d/99-qcom-npu.rules` (fastrpc/dma_heap MODE=0666) and `usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh` (fwup A/B bookkeeping, udevd + coldplug, chmod of DSP nodes, cdsprpcd/adsprpcd); nothing in `post-build.sh`, `erlinit.config` or `test/qemu-smoke.sh` mentions dri/drm. Two corrections: (1) `BR2_PACKAGE_KMSCUBE=y` (nerves_defconfig:104) is an installed binary, not a smoke test — `BRINGUP.md` never mentions kmscube, modetest, /dev/dri or HDMI, so there is currently NO display bring-up procedure at all (that is the more useful finding). (2) The ambiguity does not exist in our tree today: `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment` has no `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM` anywhere (lines 219-221 are `CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m`, `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m`, `CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m`), so msm is the only DRM card and card0 is deterministic. This is a pre-emptive hardening item contingent on adopting the separate simpledrm proposal, not a present defect.

**Why it matters:** If we add simpledrm (previous finding), card0 may be the GOP framebuffer while the real MDSS/DP device is card1 (minor numbers are also reused after simpledrm is unbound by aperture takeover), so kmscube/modetest and any Elixir GBM app can silently target the wrong device and appear to fail. Yocto needed a whole script for this on the same board.

**Action:** Do not port the weston-start.sh logic (systemd-only, weston-only, disabled on the reference, and dependent on `/dev/dri/by-path/` which I could not confirm eudev provides in Buildroot). 1. Cheapest correct fix — select by DRIVER NAME, not by node number, in bring-up docs. `modetest -M msm` already works with no symlink: libdrm's `-M` is the DRM driver name passed to `drmOpen()`, which scans `/dev/dri/card0..15` and matches `version->name`. Add to `BRINGUP.md` (which today has no display section at all): - `modetest -M msm -c` to list connectors/modes - `modetest -M msm -s <conn_id>@<crtc_id>:1920x1080` for a test pattern - For an Elixir/GBM app, enumerate with `drmGetDevices2()` / open the node whose `drmGetVersion()->name == "msm"` rather than hardcoding card0. 2. Only if the separate simpledrm proposal is adopted (`CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y`), add a stable name via udev — an event-driven rule, NOT a one-shot loop in `qcom-coldplug.sh` (which runs right after `udevadm settle`, before the `CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m` module has finished probing MDSS/DP and creating connectors, so it would usually find nothing). New file `rootfs_overlay/etc/udev/rules.d/99-qcom-drm.rules`: SUBSYSTEM=="drm", KERNEL=="card[0-9]*", SUBSYSTEMS=="platform", DRIVERS=="msm_dpu", SYMLINK+="dri/msm" SUBSYSTEM=="drm", KERNEL=="card[0-9]*", ATTR{dev}=="?*", ENV{ID_PATH}!="*simple-framebuffer*", TAG+="uaccess" (verify the parent driver name on hardware with `udevadm info -a /dev/dri/card0`; on sc7280 the DRM master is the `msm_dpu`/`mdss` platform device.) Then use `kmscube -D /dev/dri/msm` — kmscube has no driver-name option, so it is the one tool that needs the symlink. 3. If a shell fallback is still wanted in `qcom-coldplug.sh`, it must retry, iterate `card[0-9]*` (not `card[0-9]`), and not fail the boot: i=0; while [ $i -lt 20 ]; do for c in /sys/class/drm/card[0-9]*; do [ -d "$c" ] || continue for conn in "$c"-HDMI-A-* "$c"-DP-* "$c"-eDP-*; do [ -d "$conn" ] || continue ln -sf "/dev/dri/$(basename "$c")" /dev/dri/msm 2>/dev/null || true break 3 done done i=$((i+1)); sleep 0.25 done 4. Design decision to surface: the reference board's only PROVEN on-screen output is a kernel fbcon getty on tty1 with `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y`. We have no getty and no systemd, so if we enable simpledrm for an early console we get the GOP framebuffer plus fbcon (needs `CONFIG_FRAMEBUFFER_CONSOLE` and `console=tty1` on the GRUB linux line) purely as a bring-up aid — that is a separate decision from GBM app output, and it is exactly what creates the two-card situation this finding is about.

**Uncertainty:** Whether Buildroot's eudev ships the systemd 60-drm.rules that creates /dev/dri/by-path/* was not verified, which is why the snippet above uses /sys/class/drm instead of by-path.

### [minor] MINOR (cross-domain, display-relevant): our grub.cfg overrides the firmware DTB the Yocto display path depends on  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** The proven boot path installs no devicetree: q6a-a.conf/q6a-b.conf are `title/linux/initrd/options` only. Commit 89c038c states the reasoning explicitly — "Boot chain uses the EDK2-provided DTB (esp recipe ships no dtb by design; loader entries have no devicetree line). Vendor OS proves that DTB has the full display graph (mdss, dp-controller, qmp combo phy, /hdmi-bridge radxa,ra620, /hdmi-connector, simple-framebuffer)".

**Ours today:** grub.cfg:77 sets `nerves_dtb="/boot/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb"` and :83/:88 run `devicetree ($nerves_disk,gptN)$nerves_dtb` before `linux`; post-build.sh:52-54 installs that DTB into $TARGET_DIR/boot (all verified). The DTB we build from the pinned Deka tree (nerves_defconfig:39, e05c4fa) DOES carry the full display graph — &gpu status=okay (DTS:638), &gpu_zap_shader firmware-name="qcom/qcs6490/a660_zap.mbn" (642), &mdss okay (702), &mdss_dp okay with pinctrl-0=<&dp_hot_plug_det> (706-711), &mdss_dp_out data-lanes=<0 1> -> usb_dp_qmpphy_dp_in (713), bridge{compatible="radxa,ra620"} (77), hdmi-connector (120), &usb_1_qmpphy (1312) — and contains NO simple-framebuffer node (zero grep hits) and no framebuffer entry under `chosen` (102, stdout-path only). Critically, the missing simple-framebuffer node is not the binding constraint on our side: linux-dragon-q6a.fragment sets neither CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM nor CONFIG_SYSFB_SIMPLEFB, so nothing could bind such a node however it arrived; and DRM_MSM/DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE are =m (:219,:220) where Yocto's q6a-display.cfg proved =y is required (t…

**Why it matters:** Display-specific consequences of the DTB choice: (a) with our own DTB there is no simple-framebuffer node, so the EFI GOP early console depends entirely on CONFIG_SYSFB_SIMPLEFB synthesizing the device from the EFI stub's screen_info — an untested path here; (b) any EDK2-side display fixups (GOP mode, reserved memory agreed with XBL/TZ, including the gpu_zap_mem carveout the zap shader loads into) are replaced by our static assumptions, and a mismatch there surfaces as SMMU faults rather than a clean error. The boot-domain agent owns the fix; this domain needs to know which DTB wins before interpreting any bench result.

**Action:** Treat this as a note owned by the boot/DTB finding, not a display action item. Boot domain: delete grub.cfg:77 (`set nerves_dtb=...`) and grub.cfg:83 and :88 (`devicetree ...`), delete the post-build.sh:52-54 `install -m 0644 $BINARIES_DIR/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dtb $TARGET_DIR/boot/` block, and also correct the stale comments that assert the opposite (grub.cfg:11-24, fwup.conf:6-9, README.md:41-46 and :59, GOAL.md:35-37, :77-78, :108-110). Keep BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_DTS_SUPPORT=y / BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_INTREE_DTS_NAME="qcom/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a" (nerves_defconfig:42-43) so the DTB stays available in $BINARIES_DIR as a fallback artifact. The display-domain work is independent and must happen regardless of which DTB wins: append to linux-dragon-q6a.fragment the proven set from meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg — CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y, CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y, CONFIG_DRM=y, CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y, CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y, CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y, CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y, CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y, CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y, CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y (QCOM_LLCC=y/QCOM_OCMEM=y already at :31-32, PHY_QCOM_QMP=y at :104) — plus CONFIG_SYSFB_SIMPLEFB=y if the early GOP console is wanted with a DTB that has no simple-framebuffer node. Bench comparison of `dmesg | grep -Ei 'mdss|msm_dp|simple-bridge|adreno|smmu'` across the two DTB paths is only meaningful after those symbols are =y.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the EDK2 DTB's display graph is identical to mainline's (e.g. same data-lanes, same zap firmware-name) could not be checked — no dump of the firmware DTB is available in this repo. Grab `/sys/firmware/fdt` on the bench and diff it against our built DTB.

### [minor] MINOR: no Vulkan (turnip) and no OpenCL — Buildroot has no freedreno option for either  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** DISTRO_FEATURES (poky defaults opengl/wayland/vulkan/x11 + qcom-base.inc adding opencl/glvnd/x11) drive mesa.bb's PACKAGECONFIG, and oe-core mesa.inc does `VULKAN_DRIVERS .= contains('PACKAGECONFIG','freedreno',',freedreno')`, so meta-qcom's `PACKAGECONFIG:append:qcom = "freedreno tools"` yields turnip in mesa-vulkan-drivers (installed explicitly by the image) plus rusticl OpenCL via libopencl-mesa and vulkan-tools/clinfo for validation.

**Ours today:** Buildroot 2026.05's package/mesa3d/Config.in has Vulkan driver symbols only for AMD, BROADCOM, IMAGINATION, INTEL, PANFROST, SWRAST and VIRTIO — there is no BR2_PACKAGE_MESA3D_VULKAN_DRIVER_FREEDRENO. BR2_PACKAGE_MESA3D_OPENCL additionally `depends on` r600/panfrost/radeonsi/iris/amd/intel, so freedreno cannot enable it either.

**Why it matters:** Any future workload wanting Vulkan (e.g. a Vulkan-based renderer, or Vulkan-backed ML) or OpenCL on the GPU is not reachable from stock Buildroot on this system. Not needed for GLES2 fullscreen rendering, and the NPU path (QNN/HTP) does not use OpenCL, so this is only a ceiling to know about.

**Action:** Accept for now; document in GOAL.md. If Vulkan becomes a requirement, the route is a local package override in /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/package/ that reuses Buildroot's mesa3d.mk with an extra meson arg — concretely a `MESA3D_CONF_OPTS += -Dvulkan-drivers=freedreno` plus a Config.in symbol, or a patch under patches/mesa3d/ adding BR2_PACKAGE_MESA3D_VULKAN_DRIVER_FREEDRENO. Do not enable BR2_PACKAGE_MESA3D_OPENCL (it will not accept freedreno).

### [minor] MINOR: clk_ignore_unused / pd_ignore_unused still on our cmdline — Yocto removed them once display went builtin  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Commit 0e2cb2b added `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` as an explicit DEBUG measure ("Linux disables 'unused' MDSS clocks and the display power domain mid-boot, killing the EDK2 GOP pipeline efifb draws on"). Commits 9c9498f and c84ddad removed them again together with `module_blacklist=msm,phy_qcom_qmp_combo` once the builtin display pipeline landed; the proven loader entries are `options root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0` — no clk/pd overrides.

**Ours today:** grub.cfg:75 still carries `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused`, described in the comment as "bring-up safety nets on Qualcomm platforms ... revisit once the driver set is complete".

**Why it matters:** They mask exactly the class of bug we are trying to see (a display driver that never claims its clocks/GDSCs) and keep GDSCs powered, which can leave MDSS/GPU rails in a state the driver does not expect at first modeset. They are also not why our GPU is dead — the -110 is a deferred-probe timeout, not clock gating — so keeping them buys nothing once gpucc/dispcc are builtin.

**Action:** After the builtin display block lands, drop them from /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:75 (final line as proposed in the console finding): set nerves_cmdline="console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 loglevel=7 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro" If HDMI then dies at the exact moment the kernel takes over from the EFI GOP, re-add `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` temporarily — that is the signature Yocto saw before its display driver was builtin.

### [minor] MINOR: DP/HDMI audio codec becomes a module under a builtin DRM_MSM  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The board DTS gives the DP block an audio identity (`&mdss_dp { sound-name-prefix = "Display Port0"; }`), and DRM_MSM in the pinned tree does `select SND_SOC_HDMI_CODEC if SND_SOC`. Yocto's vendor-derived config has the whole AudioReach/ASoC stack from the Radxa defconfig, and the image installs alsa-utils incl. alsaucm.

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:229 has CONFIG_SND_SOC_QCOM=m (with the AudioReach/WCD938x stack all =m). With DRM_MSM=y and SND_SOC=m, the `select SND_SOC_HDMI_CODEC if SND_SOC` is capped to =m by its dependency, so HDMI/DP audio needs a module load at coldplug. nerves_defconfig has alsa-lib/alsa-utils but no UCM profiles (Buildroot 2026.05 has no alsa-ucm-conf).

**Why it matters:** HDMI audio will not work until qcom-coldplug.sh's udev pass loads snd-soc-hdmi-codec and the qcom card drivers. That is acceptable (audio is not boot-critical and not deferred-probe fragile the way iommu/clk consumers are), but it should not surprise anyone testing sound over HDMI right after the display comes up.

**Action:** No change needed for video. If HDMI/DP audio is required, either accept module loading (verify `aplay -l` lists the Display Port0 PCM after coldplug) or pin the codec builtin alongside the ASoC core in linux-dragon-q6a.fragment: CONFIG_SND_SOC=y CONFIG_SND_SOC_HDMI_CODEC=y (promoting SND_SOC to =y pulls the whole ASoC core builtin — weigh the image-size cost).

### [info] GPU firmware set is already correct and complete — do not touch it  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** meta-qcom-3rdparty/recipes-bsp/packagegroups/packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a.bb pulls `linux-firmware-qcom-adreno-a660` and `linux-firmware-qcom-qcm6490-adreno` into ${PN}-firmware, gated on `bb.utils.contains_any('DISTRO_FEATURES', 'opencl opengl vulkan', ...)`; the machine conf adds packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a-firmware via MACHINE_ESSENTIAL_EXTRA_RRECOMMENDS. oe-core linux-firmware_20260622.bb defines exactly `FILES:${PN}-qcom-adreno-a660 = "${firmwaredir}/qcom/a660*.*"` (a660_sqe.fw + a660_gmu.bin) and `FILES:${PN}-qcom-qcm6490-adreno = "${firmwaredir}/qcom/qc[ms]6490/a660_zap.mbn*"`, with `RPROVIDES:${PN}-qcom-qcm6490-adreno = "${PN}-qcom-qcs6490-adreno"`.

**Ours today:** blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware/qcom/ carries a660_sqe.fw (43292 B) and a660_gmu.bin (55252 B) as real files, qcm6490/a660_zap.mbn (1054648 B) as a real file, and qcs6490/a660_zap.mbn as a relative symlink to ../qcm6490/a660_zap.mbn — the same upstream layout Yocto installs. package/qcom-dsp-firmware/qcom-dsp-firmware.mk installs with `cp -a $(@D)/lib $(TARGET_DIR)/` (preserves the symlinks) and the nerves-common skeleton has a real /lib directory, so files land at /lib/firmware/qcom/... The DTS asks for exactly this path: `&gpu_zap_shader { firmware-name = "qcom/qcs6490/a660_zap.mbn"; }`, and adreno_gpu.c reads that property and calls request_firmware_direct() with the string verbatim (no "qcom/" prefix added), so the path matches byte for byte. QCOM_SCM=y and (via DRM_MSM's `select QCOM_MDT_LOADER if ARCH_QCOM`) the MDT/PAS loader are present; sc7280.dtsi supplies the `gpu_zap_mem` reserved-memory region the zap-shader node references.

**Why it matters:** Rules out the most commonly suspected cause of a dead Adreno (missing/mispathed zap shader or GMU firmware). The zap blob is the TZ-signed image that takes the GPU out of secure mode; a path or signature mismatch shows as a GPU that never initializes. Ours is the same Qualcomm-signed file the vendor and Yocto use, so no signing/TZ interaction problem is expected.

**Action:** No change required. Two hardening items: (1) the install is guarded by `if find $(@D)/lib/firmware -type f \( -name '*.mbn' -o -name '*.mdt' \) | grep -q .` — keep that guard but make the "blobs missing" printf a hard failure for release builds so a stripped checkout cannot silently ship a GPU-less image; (2) after a build, assert presence in the squashfs: `unsquashfs -l ... | grep -E 'firmware/qcom/(a660_(sqe.fw|gmu.bin)|qcs6490/a660_zap.mbn)'`.

## WiFi, Bluetooth, and wired networking (AIC8800 USB WiFi/BT, r8169 GbE on PCIe, credential plumbing, USB/PCIe/QMP PHY enablement)

Both systems agree on the hardware picture: the Dragon Q6A's WiFi/BT is an Aicsemi AIC8800D80 USB module (the board DTS deletes the WCN6750 `&wifi` and `&remoteproc_wpss` nodes outright), and wired GbE is a Realtek RTL8111 that simply appears on `pcie0` with no DT node. Kernel-config-wise the proven Yocto build is actually *thinner* than ours: its kernel is Radxa's linux-6.18.2 tree whose `arch/arm64/configs/defconfig` is byte-identical to the defconfig in our pinned Deka tree, and the only fragments it merges are `q6a-display.cfg` + `q6a-platform.cfg` — so on the proven board `R8169`, `PHY_QCOM_USB_SNPS_FEMTO_V2` and `PHY_QCOM_QMP` are all `=m` and get module-loaded from an initramfs by systemd/udev, whereas we build them `=y`. The `realtek-eth-8169.cfg` fragment in meta-qcom-3rdparty is dead code in the proven build (it bbappends `linux-qcom-next`, but kas switched `virtual/kernel` to …


### [BLOCKER] CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB is still =m in our kernel — stock btusb can claim the AIC BT interface  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** Accurate as written. Exact lines from radxa/kernel@559f4f92 arch/arm64/configs/defconfig: 189 `CONFIG_BT=m`, 194 `CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB=m`, 195 `CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB_MTK=y`. The bbappend that selects this tree and strips prune.config/qcom.config is meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-qcom_%.bbappend; the only added fragments are q6a-display.cfg (DRM/gpucc/dispcc only) and q6a-platform.cfg (`CONFIG_QCOMTEE=y` only). q6a-nerveshub-image.bb installs aic8800-usb and no BlueZ, and no recipe or kas layer blacklists btusb — so the Yocto image has the identical latent conflict and simply never exercised BT.

**Ours today:** Correct, with one citation fix: BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y is at nerves_defconfig line 40 (not 43); the fragment file is wired on line 41. Everything else verified: fragment lines 176-184 hold only the comment plus CONFIG_CFG80211=m / CONFIG_RFKILL=y / CONFIG_BT=m / CONFIG_BT_RFCOMM=m; no modprobe.d anywhere in rootfs_overlay; no .ko pruning in post-build.sh or post-createfs.sh; patches/ is empty except .gitkeep; the Deka e05c4fa defconfig carries CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB=m at line 194 and is byte-identical to radxa 559f4f92's.

**Why it matters:** `aic_btusb`'s USB id table (aic_btusb.c:3704, `MODULE_DEVICE_TABLE(usb, btusb_table)`) competes with stock `btusb`, which matches the generic Bluetooth interface class. Whichever driver udev modprobes first wins the interface; if `btusb` binds, the AIC BT firmware is never downloaded and BT is dead with no obvious error. Non-deterministic and hard to debug on a read-only rootfs.

**Action:** Unchanged in substance. Append to /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment immediately after line 184 (CONFIG_BT_RFCOMM=m): # Stock btusb matches the generic Bluetooth interface class (btusb.c:73, # USB_DEVICE_INFO(0xe0,0x01,0x01)) and so matches the AIC8800D80 BT # interface (A69C:8D81 e0/01/01) exactly like aic_btusb does. The arch # defconfig has it =m (line 194), so it must be turned off explicitly. # CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB is not set (BT_HCIBTUSB_MTK needs no line: it is a bool under BT_HCIBTUSB and olddefconfig drops it.) Belt-and-braces, new file /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/rootfs_overlay/etc/modprobe.d/aic8800.conf containing `blacklist btusb` — honoured because eudev's kmod builtin (BR2_PACKAGE_EUDEV_MODULE_LOADING -> BR2_PACKAGE_KMOD) applies modprobe.d blacklists to modalias auto-load, which is how modules get loaded from rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh. Also fix the now-true-only-after-this-change README wording at /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/README.md:127-128, and mirror the note in package/aic8800/Config.in:15-16 so the help text points at the actual fragment line rather than an aspiration.

**Uncertainty:** I did not read `btusb`'s id table in the 6.18 tree to prove a class-level overlap with the AIC BT interface descriptor; the risk is inferred from the vendor's own Makefile shipping `echo aic_btusb >> /etc/modules-load.d/aic_bt.conf` (i.e. vendor forces its module in) and from standard practice with AIC/Realtek USB BT combos.

### [MAJOR] `net.ifnames=0` missing from our kernel cmdline — eudev will rename eth0/wlan0  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** Both boot entries carry it explicitly: `meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/images/esp-qcom-image/q6a-a.conf` and `q6a-b.conf`: `options root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a rw rootwait console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 earlycon console=tty1 net.ifnames=0`. It was present from the first commit and survived the debug-cmdline cleanup (c84ddad), so it is intentional, not leftover.

**Ours today:** Substantively correct. One citation nit: `BR2_ROOTFS_DEVICE_CREATION_DYNAMIC_EUDEV=y` is at nerves_defconfig **line 26**, not 27. `BR2_PACKAGE_EUDEV` itself is not listed in nerves_defconfig but is auto-selected — `deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/system/Config.in:289-294` has `config BR2_ROOTFS_DEVICE_CREATION_DYNAMIC_EUDEV ... select BR2_PACKAGE_EUDEV`. Extra evidence the claim omits, which makes it stronger: - `80-net-name-slot.rules` is NOT unconditionally installed by eudev — `rules/Makefile.am` installs it only when the rule generator is disabled. We leave `BR2_PACKAGE_EUDEV_RULES_GEN` unset (absent from nerves_defconfig), so `eudev.mk:40-44` passes `--disable-rule-generator` and the rule IS installed. The claim's conclusion survives, but for a reason it did not state. - We actually run udevd AND replay add events, which is what makes the rename fire on a Nerves system that would otherwise never run udevd: `rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh:28-34` does `udevd --daemon` then `udevadm trigger --type=devices --action=add` then `udevadm settle`. The rule's first line …

**Why it matters:** eudev 3.2.14's `80-net-name-slot.rules` renames using `ID_NET_NAME_ONBOARD/SLOT/PATH` from the `net_id` builtin unless `net.ifnames=0` is on the cmdline (the rule literally does `IMPORT{cmdline}="net.ifnames"` / `ENV{net.ifnames}=="0", GOTO="net_name_slot_end"`). The RTL8111 on `pcie0` will come up as something like `enp1s0`, not `eth0`, and the USB WiFi as a path/MAC-derived name instead of `wlan0`. Every VintageNet config keyed on `"eth0"`/`"wlan0"` — which is the Nerves norm and what README.md:118-126 promises — silently matches nothing and the device has no network.

**Action:** Add `net.ifnames=0` to BOTH GRUB cmdlines (real hardware + QEMU test, so the smoke/rollback tests exercise the same naming): 1. `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:75` ``` set nerves_cmdline="console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused loglevel=4 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro net.ifnames=0" ``` 2. `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/grub.cfg.qemu:38` (currently `set nerves_cmdline="console=ttyAMA0,115200 earlycon loglevel=7 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro"`) — append `net.ifnames=0` as well, otherwise the QEMU virtio-net NIC gets renamed (e.g. `enp0s1`) and the tests silently diverge from hardware. No Buildroot symbol change is needed or available: there is no `BR2_*` knob for interface naming, and `BR2_PACKAGE_EUDEV_RULES_GEN=y` would be the wrong lever (it drops `80-net-name-slot.rules` but installs the persistent-net rule generator instead, which writes MAC-pinned `NAME=` rules into a writable `/etc/udev/rules.d` we do not have — rootfs is squashfs `ro`). Optional belt-and-braces (not instead of the cmdline): ship a zero-byte `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/rootfs_overlay/etc/udev/rules.d/80-net-name-slot.rules`; eudev reads `/etc/udev/rules.d` with priority over `/lib/udev/rules.d` for the same basename, so an empty file masks it. `BR2_ROOTFS_OVERLAY` already includes `${NERVES_DEFCONFIG_DIR}/rootfs_overlay` (nerves_defconfig line 29), and we already ship `rootfs_overlay/etc/udev/rules.d/99-qcom-npu.rules`, so the path is proven.

**Uncertainty:** For a USB WiFi interface eudev may produce no `ID_NET_NAME_*` at all (in which case the kernel's `wlan0` survives); the PCIe ethernet rename is the certain part.

### [MAJOR] Our aic8800 SRCREV is one vendor release behind the hardware-proven one (missing fix-usb-suspend-reboot-hang)  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** `meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/aic8800-usb/aic8800-usb_git.bb`: `SRCREV = "6e076049b719ac2ff7ce5c92786a680407b11cdb"` = tag `5.0+git20260123.5f7be68d-7`, authored 2026-07-16. (The recipe's DESCRIPTION claiming "4.0+git20250410.b99ca8b6-5" is stale and wrong.) Its `debian/patches/series` has 25 entries, including `fix-usb-suspend-reboot-hang.patch`.

**Ours today:** `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/package/aic8800/aic8800.mk:11`: `AIC8800_VERSION = bd11969265809a0fc948f1107c8256bbb2c1aa60` = tag `5.0+git20260123.5f7be68d-6`, authored 2026-06-26. Its series has 24 entries — `fix-usb-suspend-reboot-hang.patch` is absent.

**Why it matters:** The delta is a real functional fix: `fix-usb-suspend-reboot-hang.patch` removes a `down(&aicwf_deinit_sem)` wait in `aicwf_usb_rx_complete()` that blocks on the disconnect callback when a URB errors — i.e. a reboot/suspend hang path in the WiFi datapath. We should be on the exact revision that is proven on this board, not a nearby one.

**Action:** In `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/package/aic8800/aic8800.mk` set: ``` AIC8800_VERSION = 6e076049b719ac2ff7ce5c92786a680407b11cdb ``` and update the header comment to `# Pinned commit = release 5.0+git20260123.5f7be68d-7`. I verified by clone + sequential `patch -g0 -p1 -E --no-backup-if-mismatch` that the full 25-patch series applies cleanly at that rev after our CRLF normalization, so no other change is needed. Remember to refresh the hash if you add a `.hash` file (there is none today — Buildroot will download unverified).

### [MAJOR] No Bluetooth userspace on either system — BT is unproven end-to-end  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** The `aic_btusb` half is right; the "no BlueZ userspace" half is wrong on every point. 1. `bluetooth` IS in `DISTRO_FEATURES`. `qcom-base.inc:16` is `DISTRO_FEATURES:append =`, an addition on top of the defaults — not the full set. `oe-core/meta/conf/distro/include/default-distrovars.inc:28-32`: ``` DISTRO_FEATURES_DEFAULTS ?= " \ acl alsa bluetooth debuginfod ext2 ipv4 ipv6 \ wifi xattr nfs zeroconf pci 3g nfc x11 vfat seccomp pulseaudio \ ... ``` folded in by `oe-core/meta/conf/bitbake.conf:902`: `DISTRO_FEATURES:append = " ${@oe.utils.filter_default_features('DISTRO_FEATURES', d)}"`. Nothing puts `bluetooth` in `DISTRO_FEATURES_OPTED_OUT` (kas local_conf_header only removes `virtualization kvm`). (The claim's quoted feature list also silently drops `minidebuginfo`.) 2. `bluetooth` is ALSO in `MACHINE_FEATURES`: `meta-qcom/conf/machine/include/qcom-common.inc:7` — `MACHINE_FEATURES = "alsa bluetooth usbgadget usbhost wifi"`. So `COMBINED_FEATURES` contains bluetooth, and `packagegroup-base.bb:61` pulls `packagegroup-base-bluetooth` (RDEPENDS `bluez5`, line 188-190) into `packagegrou…

**Ours today:** Confirmed accurate as stated. `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:183-184` is exactly `CONFIG_BT=m` / `CONFIG_BT_RFCOMM=m` and a grep for `CONFIG_BT` in that file returns only those two lines. `nerves_defconfig` has no `BR2_PACKAGE_BLUEZ5*`, no `BR2_PACKAGE_DBUS`; a tree-wide grep for bluetooth/bluez/hci hits only comments (Config.in:19, package/aic8800/Config.in:12-16, aic8800.mk:5/19/35/38, fragment:178-180, README.md:115-129) — no package. README.md:126-127 does say "registers a BlueZ HCI (usable from Elixir with BlueHeron)". Two additions the original missed: - `CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB=m` is inherited from the arm64 defconfig (line 192) and never disabled by our fragment, directly contradicting fragment:179-180's stated intent. Stock `btusb` will be built. - The BT_LE line number is 189, not 191 (191 is `# CONFIG_BT_DEBUGFS is not set`).

**Why it matters:** "BT works on the Yocto board" cannot be true in any tested sense — there is no tool on that image that can open an HCI device. So we get no proven reference for BT and must validate it ourselves. Also, BlueHeron's transports are UART and libusb-based; neither speaks to a kernel-registered HCI device (`AF_BLUETOOTH`/`BTPROTO_HCI`), so the README's plan may not work as written. And `aic_btusb`'s own firmware reader only handles `PRODUCT_ID_AIC8800DC` and `PRODUCT_ID_AIC8800D80N` chip subdirs — for a D80 it prints "unknown chipid" and loads nothing; the D80's BT patch/adid comes from `aic_load_fw` instead (which does handle `aic8800D80`).

**Action:** Drop the "decide (a) or (b)" framing — the reference board answers it: it runs the full BlueZ stack with `bluetoothd` auto-started. Match it, and fix a real kernel-config leak the original finding missed. 1. `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment` — the fragment's own comment ("Do NOT enable BT_HCIBTUSB") is not enforced. We build with `BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y` (nerves_defconfig:40) = arm64 defconfig, and that defconfig has `CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB=m` at line 192. Nothing in our fragment turns it off (only `CONFIG_BT=m` / `CONFIG_BT_RFCOMM=m` at 183-184), so stock `btusb.ko` IS built and can bind the AIC BT interface first — exactly the failure the comment warns about. Append: ``` # CONFIG_BT_HCIBTUSB is not set CONFIG_BT_LE=y CONFIG_BT_HCIUART=m CONFIG_BT_HCIUART_QCA=y ``` (`CONFIG_BT_LE=y` is genuinely needed — `# CONFIG_BT_LE is not set` is at arm64 defconfig line **189**, not 191; `net/bluetooth/Kconfig:72-75` shows it is `bool ... default y`, so the defconfig explicitly overrides the default off and our fragment inherits that. `BT_HCIUART*` mirrors meta-qcom/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-yocto-6.18/bsp/qcom-armv8a/qcom.cfg:18-19.) 2. `nerves_defconfig` — add BlueZ. All symbols verified present in deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/bluez5_utils/Config.in: ``` BR2_PACKAGE_BLUEZ5_UTILS=y BR2_PACKAGE_BLUEZ5_UTILS_CLIENT=y BR2_PACKAGE_BLUEZ5_UTILS_MONITOR=y BR2_PACKAGE_BLUEZ5_UTILS_TOOLS=y BR2_PACKAGE_BLUEZ5_UTILS_DEPRECATED=y ``` `BR2_PACKAGE_DBUS=y` is NOT needed as an explicit line — bluez5_utils.mk's Config.in already has `select BR2_PACKAGE_DBUS` and `select BR2_PACKAGE_LIBGLIB2` (Config.in:9-10). Adding it is harmless but redundant. `_DEPRECATED` is mandatory if you want the proposed bring-up command `hciconfig -a` to exist at all (Config.in:51-59: hciattach, hciconfig, hcitool, hcidump, rfcomm, sdptool, ciptool live behind it, and it `depends on _CLIENT || _TOOLS`). `_MONITOR` gives `btmon`, the actually-useful HCI trace tool. 3. Daemon start — the "no systemd" concern is real but Buildroot already ships an init script: `deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/bluez5_utils/S40bluetoothd`, which runs `start-stop-daemon --start --background --exec /usr/libexec/bluetooth/bluetoothd -- -n`. With `BR2_INIT_NONE` that S-script is never executed, so port its two lines into `rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh` (or an erlinit pre-run script): launch `dbus-daemon --system` first (needs `/var/run/dbus` and `/etc/dbus-1` present), then `/usr/libexec/bluetooth/bluetoothd -n &`. 4. Only if you skip BlueZ: verify the Elixir library can …

**Uncertainty:** Whether BlueHeron (or whatever Elixir BT library is intended) can drive a kernel HCI device — I did not check its transport list.

### [minor] WiFi credentials: the Yocto NetworkManager-profile approach has no Buildroot analogue and must not be ported  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** `meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-products/images/q6a-nerveshub-image.bb` `q6a_image_tweaks()` writes a 0600 `${sysconfdir}/NetworkManager/system-connections/${WIFI_SSID}.nmconnection` (`[wifi-security] key-mgmt=wpa-psk`, `psk=...`, `[ipv4] method=auto`) at image build time. Creds come from `kas/wifi.yml` (gitignored; `kas/wifi.yml.example` sets `WIFI_SSID`/`WIFI_PSK` as bitbake vars). Two iterations were needed: ae4362c ($VAR vs ${VAR} expansion), then ec7dfea (kas-container drops host env, so use a kas include instead of BB_ENV_PASSTHROUGH). The network manager is **NetworkManager**, pulled in by `packagegroup-qcom-utilities-network-utils` (via `qcom-console-image.bb`), with `wifi` in `DISTRO_FEATURES` (`meta-qcom-distro/conf/distro/include/qcom-base.inc`) and `INIT_MANAGER = "systemd"`.

**Ours today:** No NetworkManager, no systemd-networkd, no /etc/wpa_supplicant.conf, nothing under rootfs_overlay/etc/NetworkManager (rootfs_overlay contains only etc/erlinit.config, etc/fw_env.config, etc/boardid.config, boot/.empty, etc/profile.d/qcom-npu-env.sh, usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh, etc/udev/rules.d/99-qcom-npu.rules). We ship the full VintageNetWiFi station-mode set: BR2_PACKAGE_AIC8800 (nerves_defconfig:80), BR2_PACKAGE_WPA_SUPPLICANT + NL80211/AP_SUPPORT/WPA3/EAP/WPS/CLI/PASSPHRASE (82-89), BR2_PACKAGE_WIRELESS_REGDB (90), BR2_PACKAGE_IW (91), BR2_PACKAGE_LIBNL (50), BR2_PACKAGE_LIBMNL (49-ish), plus busybox udhcpc/udhcpd/ip from deps/nerves_system_br/board/nerves-common/busybox.config (CONFIG_UDHCPC=y:1011, CONFIG_UDHCPD=y:1004, CONFIG_IP=y:921). Credentials are the application's problem; GOAL.md/BRINGUP.md/README.md currently say nothing about credential provisioning, so the boundary is undocumented rather than technically missing.

**Why it matters:** NetworkManager+systemd is the whole delivery mechanism on the Yocto side; on Nerves the equivalent is VintageNet/VintageNetWiFi in Elixir, which writes its own wpa_supplicant config into `/tmp` at runtime and manages DHCP itself. Baking creds into the system image would also put secrets in a squashfs slot that ships over OTA to every device — worse than the Yocto build-time hack. This needs to be an explicit documented boundary, not silently absent.

**Action:** 1. Keep the system credential-free. Document in README.md (Connectivity section, the block at lines 115-132) that WiFi creds are supplied by the application via VintageNetWiFi config (config :vintage_net, config: [{"wlan0", %{type: VintageNetWiFi, vintage_net_wifi: %{networks: [%{key_mgmt: :wpa_psk, ssid: ..., psk: ...}]}, ipv4: %{method: :dhcp}}}]), and that runtime provisioning must persist under /root — the ext4 app partition mounted by erlinit (rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config: `-m /dev/rootdisk0p4:/root:ext4:nodev:`, which is also vintage_net's default persistence_dir /root/vintage_net) — never the read-only squashfs rootfs, since the rootfs slot ships over OTA to every device. 2. If AP/hotspot provisioning is wanted, add BR2_PACKAGE_DNSMASQ=y to nerves_defconfig. Rationale corrected: a DHCP server binary already exists (busybox udhcpd, CONFIG_UDHCPD=y in nerves-common busybox.config); dnsmasq is required because VintageNet's `dhcpd:` interface option is implemented on top of dnsmasq, not udhcpd. Symbol verified at deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/dnsmasq/Config.in:1. 3. Optional parity with the Yocto network-utils set: BR2_PACKAGE_ETHTOOL=y (verified at buildroot-2026.05/package/ethtool/Config.in:1). iproute2/tcpdump/hostapd are deliberate omissions — busybox `ip` covers vintage_net, and wpa_supplicant's AP_SUPPORT replaces hostapd. 4. Drop the dependency on a "net.ifnames=0 fix": grub.cfg's nerves_cmdline has no net.ifnames and nothing in either tree shows Buildroot eudev doing predictable renaming here. Verify eth0/wlan0 empirically on hardware before treating it as a prerequisite of this item.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the product needs AP-mode provisioning at all — that decides the dnsmasq item.

### [minor] rtl_nic firmware for the RTL8111 is shipped by neither system  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a.bb` `RRECOMMENDS:${PN}-firmware` lists only adreno, camx, lt9611uxc, q6a audio/compute, qupv3fw and vpu firmware — no `linux-firmware-rtl8168`/`rtl_nic`. So the proven board runs r8169 without its firmware patch and works.

**Ours today:** We enable no `BR2_PACKAGE_LINUX_FIRMWARE` at all (`nerves_defconfig` has only our vendored `BR2_PACKAGE_QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE`), so `/lib/firmware/rtl_nic/` is absent.

**Why it matters:** r8169 calls `request_firmware()` for a chip-specific `rtl_nic/rtl8168*.fw` PHY patch; missing it is non-fatal (a dmesg warning) but can cost link stability or throughput on some steppings. Since the proven board is fine without it, this is optional — but it's a cheap thing to have ready if bench testing shows link flaps.

**Action:** Only if dmesg on hardware shows `r8169 ...: unable to load firmware patch rtl_nic/rtl8168X-Y.fw`, add to `nerves_defconfig`: ``` BR2_PACKAGE_LINUX_FIRMWARE=y BR2_PACKAGE_LINUX_FIRMWARE_RTL_8169=y ``` That option (Buildroot 2026.05) installs `rtl_nic/rtl8168{d,e,f,fp,g,h}-*.fw`, `rtl8125*`, `rtl8402/8411*` — 24 files. Note the exact filename r8169 asks for is printed in dmesg; match it against that list.

**Uncertainty:** Which RTL8111 stepping is on the board, hence which .fw name it requests — only readable from dmesg on hardware.

### [minor] Module autoload: no systemd, but modalias autoload works — worth an explicit fallback in the coldplug script  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** No `KERNEL_MODULE_AUTOLOAD`, no `modules-load.d`, no udev rule in `aic8800-usb_git.bb` — it relies purely on depmod-generated modalias + systemd-udevd. RadxaOS does the same (`debian/aic8800-usb-dkms.dkms` has `AUTOINSTALL=yes`, `DEST_MODULE_LOCATION=/updates/dkms`, no blacklist/modprobe.d file). The vendor Makefile's `install` target would write `/etc/modules-load.d/aic_bt.conf` (a systemd-modules-load file) but DKMS never runs it — so that systemd dependency is not actually in play.

**Ours today:** `BR2_ROOTFS_DEVICE_CREATION_DYNAMIC_EUDEV=y`; `BR2_PACKAGE_EUDEV_MODULE_LOADING` defaults to `y` and selects `BR2_PACKAGE_KMOD`, so eudev has libkmod. nerves-common's busybox.config has `CONFIG_MODPROBE=y`, `CONFIG_LSMOD=y`, `CONFIG_MODINFO=y` (lines 570-576). Buildroot's kernel-module infra passes `INSTALL_MOD_PATH=$(TARGET_DIR)` and `DEPMOD=$(HOST_DIR)/sbin/depmod` (linux.mk:159-167) and `LINUX_RUN_DEPMOD` runs `depmod -a -b $(TARGET_DIR)` at target-finalize (linux.mk:615-621), so `modules.alias` will contain the AIC USB ids. `rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh` starts udevd and does `udevadm trigger --type=devices --action=add` + `settle`.

**Why it matters:** Both `aic_load_fw` (`MODULE_DEVICE_TABLE(usb, aicwf_usb_id_table)`, VID 0xA69C / 0x368B) and `aic_btusb` (`MODULE_DEVICE_TABLE(usb, btusb_table)`) carry modalias entries, so the coldplug trigger should load them. But our udev is started by hand from a shell script rather than by an init system, and the AIC chip *re-enumerates* after `aic_load_fw` downloads firmware — the second enumeration needs udevd still running and responsive to bind `aic8800_fdrv`. `udevadm settle --timeout=10` may return before the re-enumeration completes, which is fine as long as udevd stays up (it does, it's daemonized), but a failure here would look like "no wlan interface" with no error.

**Action:** Add a cheap idempotent fallback to `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh`, after the udev block (step 1) and before the DSP permissions block: ```sh # 2b. AIC8800D80 USB WiFi/BT. udev normally autoloads these via modalias; # load explicitly in case coldplug raced the USB enumeration. aic_load_fw # downloads firmware and the chip re-enumerates, then aic8800_fdrv binds. for m in aic_load_fw aic8800_fdrv aic_btusb; do modprobe "$m" 2>/dev/null || true done ``` Also verify after a build that `$TARGET_DIR/lib/modules/<ver>/modules.alias` contains `usb:v A69C*` entries and that the three `.ko` files landed under `lib/modules/<ver>/extra/`.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the post-firmware re-enumeration reliably completes within our coldplug window — only observable on hardware.

### [minor] Our aic8800 package: unconditional full-series patch apply is fragile (but verified OK at both revs)  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The recipe converged, over six commits, on: apply only patches matching `fix-linux-[0-9]|fix-usb|fix-aic_btusb-use-bluez|fix-vmalloc|fix-Lower`, each behind a `patch -l -p1 --dry-run -f` gate that *skips and records* failures into `${S}/.skipped-patches` instead of aborting. Stated root cause (41cb375): "debian/patches in main drifted from main's own source (packaging tracks a different ref)". Also uses `patch -l` for CRLF (71d0d28) and drops non-driver patches touching `tools/aicrf_test` (0d971f3, 9a2b869, 354eec3).

**Ours today:** `package/aic8800/aic8800.mk:14-33` normalizes CRLF over `src/USB/driver_fw/drivers/**/*.{c,h},Makefile,Kconfig` and then applies **every** entry of `debian/patches/series` with `patch -g0 -p1 -E --no-backup-if-mismatch ... || exit 1`. I verified by clone + sequential apply that this succeeds cleanly at our pinned `bd119692` (24 patches) and at the proven `6e076049` (25 patches), so it is not a build blocker today.

**Why it matters:** The CRLF normalization is exactly why our stricter approach works where the Yocto one needed a skip-gate — but it also means the next SRCREV bump can fail the whole build with `|| exit 1`, and the sdio/pcie/allwinner entries we apply touch files we never compile (`src/SDIO/**`, `src/PCIE/**`) so their success is incidental. Note the CRLF normalization is load-bearing in the other direction too: it is what makes `fix-usb-firmware-path.patch` apply, which is what makes our `/lib/firmware/aic8800_fw/USB` install path correct.

**Action:** Keep the current behaviour (fail-loud is right for a system we control), but harden two things in `package/aic8800/aic8800.mk`: 1. Widen the CRLF normalization so a future patch touching a file outside `src/USB/driver_fw/drivers` doesn't fail on line endings — change the `find` root from `src/USB/driver_fw/drivers` to `src` (still excluding `debian/`). 2. Fix the license metadata (line 13): the repo is GPL-3.0-only for the driver with proprietary firmware blobs. Use ``` AIC8800_LICENSE = GPL-3.0-only (driver), PROPRIETARY (firmware blobs) AIC8800_LICENSE_FILES = LICENSE AIC8800_REDISTRIBUTE = NO ``` (matches the Yocto recipe's `LICENSE = "GPL-3.0-only"` / `LIC_FILES_CHKSUM = file://LICENSE;md5=1ebbd3e34237af26da5dc08a4e440464`.) 3. Add a `package/aic8800/aic8800.hash` with the tarball sha256 — right now the download is unverified.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/package/aic8800/aic8800.mk:11-49 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/aic8800-usb/aic8800-usb_git.bb (do_apply_vendor_patches) · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto (git show 41cb375, 71d0d28, 0d971f3, 354eec3, 9a2b869) · local verification: sequential full-series apply OK at bd119692 (24 patches) and 6e076049 (25 patches)</sub>


### [minor] Regulatory domain / country code is world-default on both systems  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Nothing sets a regulatory domain. `wireless-regdb` comes in via `DISTRO_FEATURES += wifi` / NetworkManager, and the AIC driver's own `CONFIG_COUNTRY_CODE = "00"` (aic8800_fdrv/Makefile) plus `aic_powerlimit_8800d80.txt` govern TX limits.

**Ours today:** `BR2_PACKAGE_WIRELESS_REGDB=y` (nerves_defconfig:90) installs `regulatory.db`/`regulatory.db.p7s` into `/lib/firmware`; the vendor driver builds with the same `CONFIG_COUNTRY_CODE = "00"`. No `iw reg set` anywhere, and there is no systemd/NM to apply one.

**Why it matters:** With country "00" the driver advertises only the world-roaming channel set — 5 GHz channels are typically passive-scan/no-IR, which shows up as "can't see or associate to my 5 GHz AP". On the Yocto side NetworkManager would not fix this either, so it's a shared latent issue rather than a regression; but we have no place for it to happen automatically.

**Action:** After bring-up, if 5 GHz association fails, set the domain explicitly. Options: (a) one line in `rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh` after the modprobe block, e.g. `iw reg set AR 2>/dev/null || true` (we already ship `BR2_PACKAGE_IW=y`); (b) better for a product, do it from Elixir so it's per-deployment configurable rather than baked into the system image. Do not hardcode a country into the vendor Makefile.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the AIC fullmac driver consults the kernel regdb at all (it has its own `regdb.c`), so `iw reg set` may or may not take effect — needs a hardware test.

### [info] Firmware install path: ours is right, the Yocto recipe's is wrong-but-accidentally-consistent — do not copy it  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `aic8800-usb_git.bb` do_install does `cp -r ${S}/src/USB/driver_fw/fw/. ${D}${nonarch_base_libdir}/firmware/`, i.e. `/usr/lib/firmware/aic8800D80/...`. That only works because `fix-usb-firmware-path.patch` (which would repoint the driver at `/lib/firmware/aic8800_fw/USB`) is *skipped* by the recipe's dry-run gate: I reproduced it — the patch fails with `Hunk #1 FAILED at 144 (different line endings)` against the CRLF `aicbluetooth.c`, even with `patch -l`. So the driver keeps its unpatched default `/lib/firmware` and the layout matches by accident. Root cause is documented in commit 41cb375 ("debian/patches in main drifted from main's own source") and 71d0d28 ("aicbluetooth.c hunk has CRLF endings").

**Ours today:** `package/aic8800/aic8800.mk:46-49` installs `src/USB/driver_fw/fw/.` into `$(TARGET_DIR)/lib/firmware/aic8800_fw/USB/`, and `AIC8800_APPLY_DEBIAN_PATCHES` normalizes CRLF *before* patching, so `fix-usb-firmware-path.patch` DOES apply and the driver default becomes `/lib/firmware/aic8800_fw/USB`. Self-consistent, and it matches what RadxaOS itself ships.

**Why it matters:** This is the one place where copying the "proven" system would break us. `aic_load_fw` reads firmware with `filp_open` (not `request_firmware` — `CONFIG_USE_FW_REQUEST = n` in both Makefiles) and builds `<default>/aic8800D80/<name>`, so a one-directory mismatch means zero WiFi with only a `filp_open` failure in dmesg. Our path is confirmed correct by the vendor's own dpkg manifest.

**Action:** No change — keep `/lib/firmware/aic8800_fw/USB/`. Add a comment in `package/aic8800/aic8800.mk` recording *why* it differs from the Yocto recipe (CRLF-skipped patch there vs. normalized-and-applied here) so nobody "fixes" it toward the Yocto layout. If you ever drop the CRLF normalization, you must simultaneously change the install dir to `/lib/firmware/`.

### [info] realtek-eth-8169.cfg is dead code in the proven build; our r8169 setup is already a superset  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `meta-qcom-3rdparty/recipes-kernel/linux/radxa-dragon-q6a/realtek-eth-8169.cfg` sets `CONFIG_NET_SELFTESTS=y CONFIG_R8169=y CONFIG_MDIO_BUS=y CONFIG_PHYLIB=y CONFIG_FIXED_PHY=y CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y # CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY_HWMON is not set CONFIG_FWNODE_MDIO=y CONFIG_OF_MDIO=y CONFIG_ACPI_MDIO=y`. But it is added only by `meta-qcom-3rdparty/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-qcom-next_git.bbappend` (`SRC_URI:append:radxa-dragon-q6a`), and `kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml` sets `PREFERRED_PROVIDER_virtual/kernel = "linux-qcom"` / `PREFERRED_VERSION_linux-qcom = "6.18%"`. Our local layer's `linux-qcom_%.bbappend` adds only `q6a-display.cfg` + `q6a-platform.cfg`. So the proven kernel ships the Radxa defconfig values: `CONFIG_R8169=m`, `CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y`, no `NET_SELFTESTS`, and the module is loaded from the initramfs (`INITRAMFS_IMAGE = "initramfs-rootfs-image"`).

**Ours today:** `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:149,153` — `CONFIG_PCIE_QCOM=y`, `CONFIG_R8169=y` (built-in, no initramfs), plus `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y` / `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_PCIE=y` in the storage section. `CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y` comes from the arch defconfig (line 403), and `R8169`'s Kconfig `select`s PHYLIB/REALTEK_PHY/MII/FW_LOADER anyway. No DT node is required — the RTL8111 is enumerated on `pcie0`, which the board DTS enables (`&pcie0 { status = "okay"; }` with `perst-gpios = <&tlmm 87 ...>`, `wake-gpios = <&tlmm 89 ...>`).

**Why it matters:** Avoids a false errand: copying `realtek-eth-8169.cfg` into our fragment would add nothing that is actually proven, and it would give a misleading impression that those MDIO symbols were needed to make the NIC work.

**Action:** No required change. Optional, cheap and harmless if you want the exact symbol set spelled out for future readers, append to `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment` near line 153: ``` CONFIG_REALTEK_PHY=y CONFIG_PHYLIB=y CONFIG_MDIO_BUS=y CONFIG_FWNODE_MDIO=y CONFIG_OF_MDIO=y ``` Do NOT add `CONFIG_NET_SELFTESTS=y` (test-only bloat) and do not add `CONFIG_ACPI_MDIO` (we boot DT, not ACPI).

### [info] USB/PCIe/QMP PHY enablement: ours is builtin where the proven build uses modules — no gap, but note the display interaction  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Defconfig values (unchanged by the two q6a fragments except as noted): `CONFIG_USB_DWC3=y`, `CONFIG_USB_XHCI_HCD=y`, `CONFIG_PCIE_QCOM=y`, `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=m`, `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_USB_SNPS_FEMTO_V2=m`. `q6a-display.cfg` promotes `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y` and builtins the whole display domain; its comment states "QMP combo/USB phys stay modules (vendor-proven)". Commit c1ecf57 found the display root cause (`SC_GPUCC_7280=m` starving the adreno SMMU), and c84ddad removed a debug `module_blacklist=msm,phy_qcom_qmp_combo` that had disabled the combo PHY on the running system.

**Ours today:** `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:160-172`: `CONFIG_USB=y`, `CONFIG_USB_XHCI_HCD=y`, `CONFIG_USB_DWC3=y`, `CONFIG_USB_DWC3_QCOM=y`, `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_USB_SNPS_FEMTO_V2=y`, `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO=y`, plus `CONFIG_PCIE_QCOM=y` / `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_PCIE=y` / `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y`. All builtin because we boot with no initramfs.

**Why it matters:** For the AIC8800 specifically, only `usb_2` matters and it is HS-only: the board DTS enables `&usb_2 { status = "okay"; dr_mode = "host"; }` with `&usb_2_hsphy` (the SNPS femto V2), no SS PHY. Our `=y` for that PHY plus DWC3-QCOM is exactly what's needed and is strictly stronger than the proven `=m`+initramfs arrangement. `usb_1` is the one with the QMP combo PHY (USB3 + DP out to the HDMI bridge) — so `PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO` is shared between USB3 and display, which is why blacklisting it killed HDMI on the Yocto board. There is no DT node for the AIC module (it's a plain USB device) and no GPIO/regulator handshake in the DTS to reproduce.

**Action:** No change required. Do NOT demote `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP_COMBO` to `=m` to "match vendor" — with no initramfs, module-only PHYs are a boot-order risk for us, and the combo PHY is also the DP path. When debugging on hardware, do not use `module_blacklist=phy_qcom_qmp_combo` (Yocto commit c84ddad shows it breaks display).

**Uncertainty:** I could not confirm from the DTS that the AIC module is physically on usb_2 rather than a hub off usb_1 — there is no DT node either way. `lsusb -t` on hardware settles it.

### [info] Confirmation that the board's WiFi is AIC8800 USB and not WCN6750  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The layer ships an out-of-tree `aic8800-usb` recipe and adds `aic8800-usb` to `CORE_IMAGE_BASE_INSTALL` (commit ec49e3b message: "mainline-next 7.1 has no AIC8800 WiFi path; 6.18.y matches the vendor kernel"). It ships no ath11k/WCN6750 firmware in `packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a-firmware`, and the WiFi profile is a plain NM wifi connection with no device pinning.

**Ours today:** Same conclusion, reached from the DTS: `qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dts` lines 24-36 do `/delete-node/ &wifi;`, `/delete-node/ &remoteproc_wpss;`, `/delete-node/ &wpss_mem;`, `/delete-node/ &wlan_ce_mem;`, `/delete-node/ &wlan_fw_mem;`. `nerves_defconfig:79-80` enables `BR2_PACKAGE_AIC8800`. README.md:131-132 already records this.

**Why it matters:** Rules out an entire wrong avenue (ath11k + WCN6750 firmware + qcom BT-over-SMD). The `/delete-node/` set is definitive: with the wifi node and the WPSS remoteproc gone, ath11k has nothing to bind to on this board.

**Action:** Nothing to add. Cleanup opportunity to shrink the image and remove dead drivers — add to `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment`: ``` # WCN6750/ath11k is deleted from the board DTS; the AIC8800 is fullmac, # so mac80211 is dead weight too. # CONFIG_ATH11K is not set # CONFIG_ATH11K_AHB is not set # CONFIG_ATH11K_PCI is not set # CONFIG_MAC80211 is not set # CONFIG_BT_QCOMSMD is not set ``` Keep `CONFIG_CFG80211=m` (the AIC fullmac driver needs it). Verify after `make linux-menuconfig` that nothing re-selects MAC80211.
