### [info] aic8800 Buildroot package structure is otherwise sound — validated against the infra and the vendor build  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Builds two separate kbuild passes: `M=${S}/src/USB/driver_fw/drivers/aic8800` and `M=${S}/src/USB/driver_fw/drivers/aic_btusb`, with `unset CFLAGS CPPFLAGS CXXFLAGS LDFLAGS` and explicit `CC=${KERNEL_CC} LD=${KERNEL_LD} AR=${KERNEL_AR}`, then `modules_install DEPMOD=echo INSTALL_MOD_PATH=${D}` (commit 667ee45 documents that `KERNEL_SRC` is empty in bitbake context). Packaging pain there (`nonarch_base_libdir` usrmerge moves, `ALLOW_EMPTY`, letting `kernel-module-split` own the .ko files) is entirely Yocto-specific and has no Buildroot analogue.

**Ours today:** `package/aic8800/aic8800.mk` uses `AIC8800_MODULE_SUBDIRS = src/USB/driver_fw/drivers/aic8800 src/USB/driver_fw/drivers/aic_btusb` + `$(eval $(kernel-module))` + `$(eval $(generic-package))`, with `AIC8800_INSTALL_TARGET_CMDS` copying the firmware and `AIC8800_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE = /lib/firmware`. Registered via `Config.in` ("Dragon Q6A connectivity" menu) and `external.mk`'s glob.

**Why it matters:** I checked the assumptions that could bite: (1) `pkg-kernel-module.mk` in Buildroot 2026.05 does support a multi-entry `_MODULE_SUBDIRS` (it `foreach`es over it for both build and install) and passes `INSTALL_MOD_PATH`/`DEPMOD` via `LINUX_MAKE_FLAGS`; (2) the vendor's top-level `src/USB/driver_fw/drivers/aic8800/Makefile` is `obj-m += aic_load_fw/ aic8800_fdrv/`, so one pass builds both and `aic8800_fdrv` sees `aic_load_fw`'s Module.symvers — our comment is correct; (3) `aic_btusb` uses none of `aic_load_fw`'s 14 `EXPORT_SYMBOL`s (checked mechanically), so the separate pass is safe; (4) `CONFIG_PLATFORM_UBUNTU ?= y` in all three vendor Makefiles, so `-DCONFIG_PLATFORM_UBUNTU` is defined and t…

**Action:** No structural change needed. Firmware payload is 6.6 MB across six chip dirs; the D80 dir is 2.1 MB and contains exactly: `aic_powerlimit_8800d80.txt`, `aic_userconfig_8800d80.txt`, `calibmode_8800d80.bin`, `fmacfw_8800d80_h_u02.bin`, `fmacfw_8800d80_h_u02_ipc.bin`, `fmacfw_8800d80_u02.bin`, `fmacfw_8800d80_u02_ipc.bin`, `fw_adid_8800d80_u02.bin`, `fw_ble_scan_ad_filter.bin`, `fw_patch_8800d80_u02.bin`, `fw_patch_8800d80_u02_ext0.bin`, `fw_patch_8800d80_u04.bin`, `fw_patch_table_8800d80_u02.bin`, `fw_patch_table_8800d80_u04.bin`, `lmacfw_rf_8800d80_u02.bin`. Keep shipping all six dirs (RadxaOS does, via `debian/aic8800-firmware.install`) until `dmesg`/`lsusb` on hardware confirms the exact variant — 4.5 MB of squashfs is not worth an unbootable-WiFi risk on a D80N/D80X2 board. Optional post-bringup trim: replace the `cp -a` with a per-variant copy of `aic8800D80/` only.

**Uncertainty:** The board's exact AIC variant (D80 vs D80N vs D80X2) — settled by `lsusb` / `dmesg | grep chipid` on hardware.

## NPU/TPU (Hexagon CDSP/ADSP, QNN/QAIRT, fastrpc, TEE)

Both systems use the same three-layer stack (kernel-loaded cdsp.mbn/adsp.mbn from linux-firmware → DSP-side shells/skels from linux-msm/hexagon-dsp-binaries @2ba8363 → qualcomm/fastrpc userspace), and the version tuple is identical: cdsp.mbn = CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1 matched with the CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1 shell dir, DTS firmware-name paths qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/{adsp,cdsp}.mbn, and fastrpc ≥1.0.5 (Yocto v1.0.6, ours v1.0.6-7-g706071c, both have the "user allocated pages" mmap fix). The differences are all in *layout, lifecycle and the layers above fastrpc*. Yocto keeps the DSP binaries in the canonical per-domain tree /usr/share/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/dsp/{adsp,cdsp} plus the conf.d YAML that fastrpc resolves from the DT model; we flatten both domains into one /usr/lib/dsp directory, which silently overwrites four CDSP skels with ADSP-version copies. Yocto dr…


### [MAJOR] SPI boot firmware ≥ 6.0.260120 is a hard NPU prerequisite and we ship no way to check or install it  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Image installs q6a-spi-firmware (meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-bsp/q6a-spi-firmware/q6a-spi-firmware_260120.bb): downloads dragon-q6a_flat_build_wp_260120.zip (sha256 cf23a174…), installs flat_build/spinor/dragon-q6a/* to /usr/share/q6a-spi-firmware/dragon-q6a/ and /usr/sbin/q6a-spi-flash; RDEPENDS edl-ng dmidecode. edl-ng_1.5.0.bb installs /usr/bin/edl-ng + /usr/bin/libSystem.IO.Ports.Native.so (prebuilt arm64 .NET). q6a-spi-flash compares `dmidecode -s bios-version` against 6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1 and runs `edl-ng --hostdev-as-target /dev/mtd0 rawprogram rawprogram0.xml patch0.xml`.

**Ours today:** Absent, confirmed by grep: no package/edl-ng, no package/q6a-spi-firmware (package/ = aic8800, qairt-runtime, qcom-dsp-firmware, qcom-dsp-shell, qcom-fastrpc only), no BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE in nerves_defconfig, no q6a-spi-flash in rootfs_overlay. Only trace is BRINGUP.md:22-28, which documents the HOST-side edl-ng v1.4.1 linux-x64 EDL procedure — i.e. a host-PC escape hatch does exist for bringup, it is just not on-device/offline. MTD side is correct (linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:186-194). CORRECTION: the "no CONFIG_DMI_SYSFS" point is wrong and irrelevant — dmidecode reads /sys/firmware/dmi/tables/{smbios_entry_point,DMI}, created by dmi_init() in drivers/firmware/dmi_scan.c:753-803 under CONFIG_DMI, not by CONFIG_DMI_SYSFS (which exports /sys/firmware/dmi/entries). arch/arm64/Kconfig:1881 makes `config DMI` `depends on EFI` `default y`, and linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:118 sets CONFIG_EFI=y over arm64 defconfig (nerves_defconfig:40), so CONFIG_DMI=y/CONFIG_DMIID=y already. Additional real gap the claim missed: our README.md:105-111 documents 0x80000600 as *only* a cdsp.mbn/fastrpc_shell …

**Why it matters:** Boot firmware older than 6.0.260120 makes QTEE reject *unsigned* DSP protection domains with 0x80000600. QNN and fastrpc_test -a v68 both require unsigned PD, so on an un-upgraded board the NPU is dead no matter how correct the rootfs is — and a Nerves device has no apt/host-PC escape hatch.

**Action:** 1) Add BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE=y to nerves_defconfig (verified real: deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/dmidecode/Config.in, `depends on BR2_aarch64 || BR2_i386 || BR2_x86_64`, v3.7, installs /usr/sbin/dmidecode). 2) Do NOT add CONFIG_DMI_SYSFS=y to linux-dragon-q6a.fragment — wrong symbol and unnecessary; CONFIG_DMI is already implied by CONFIG_EFI=y (fragment:118) + arm64 defconfig. No kernel-fragment change is required for this item. 3) package/edl-ng/{Config.in,edl-ng.mk,edl-ng.hash}: EDL_NG_VERSION = 1.5.0; EDL_NG_SITE = https://github.com/strongtz/edl-ng/releases/download/v1.5.0; EDL_NG_SOURCE = edl-ng-linux-arm64.zip; hash file `sha256 ab8a6cd4b8e31df63528bca7722b2d7e48d011006276c5df2105fc4daf8fd35d edl-ng-linux-arm64.zip`. Because buildroot 2026.05 defines no INFLATE.zip (package/pkg-utils.mk:54-63), you MUST supply `define EDL_NG_EXTRACT_CMDS $(UNZIP) -d $(@D) $(EDL_NG_DL_DIR)/$(EDL_NG_SOURCE) endef` (pattern from package/doom-wad/doom-wad.mk:12-16) plus `EDL_NG_DL_TOOLS_DEPENDENCIES = unzip` (unzip is in Dockerfile:20). Set EDL_NG_INSTALL_STAGING = NO, EDL_NG_LICENSE = MIT, install edl-ng and libSystem.IO.Ports.Native.so 0755 into $(TARGET_DIR)/usr/bin. Skip BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE (the binary is a real aarch64 ELF); instead handle the .NET runtime: export DOTNET_SYSTEM_GLOBALIZATION_INVARIANT=1 and DOTNET_BUNDLE_EXTRACT_BASE_DIR=/tmp (rootfs is read-only squashfs) from the wrapper or /etc/erlinit.config, and be prepared to add openssl/libstdc++ if it fails to start — Yocto never validated its runtime deps (INSANE_SKIP file-rdeps ldflags libdir). 4) package/q6a-spi-firmware/{Config.in,q6a-spi-firmware.mk,q6a-spi-firmware.hash}: SITE https://dl.radxa.com/dragon/q6a/images, SOURCE dragon-q6a_flat_build_wp_260120.zip, hash `sha256 cf23a1742ae5d51c451947d82cb21fd3f1c10bbcd621d611f55520673e3f90ca`, same explicit $(UNZIP) EXTRACT_CMDS + DL_TOOLS_DEPENDENCIES, LICENSE PROPRIETARY / REDISTRIBUTE NO, select BR2_PACKAGE_EDL_NG + BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE in Config.in, install flat_build/spinor/dragon-q6a/. into /usr/share/q6a-spi-firmware/dragon-q6a/ and set Q6A_SPI_FIRMWARE_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE = /usr/share/q6a-spi-firmware (Qualcomm-signed non-target ELFs). 5) Copy meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-bsp/q6a-spi-firmware/files/q6a-spi-flash to rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/q6a-spi-flash unchanged (plain /bin/sh, no systemd) but document that on Nerves it must be run non-interactively — `System.cmd("/usr/sbin/q6a-spi-flash", ["-y"])` — since the `read -r answer` prompt has no console to read from. 6) Register both in Config.in and external.mk, and confirm the added payloa…

**Uncertainty:** Cannot confirm from here whether the board in hand already runs 260120. Also unverified whether CONFIG_DMI is auto-selected on arm64+EFI in the 6.18 tree we use; if dmidecode finds no tables, add CONFIG_DMI_SYSFS=y (and CONFIG_DMI=y if the symbol is user-visible).

### [MAJOR] Flat /usr/lib/dsp install overwrites four CDSP skels with mismatched ADSP-version copies  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** hexagon-dsp-binaries installs strictly per-domain: scripts/install.sh do_install() creates ${DSPDIR}/<soc-path>/dsp/<domain>/ from the version dir, driven by config.txt lines 38-39 (`Install: qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a adsp ADSP.HT.5.5.c9-00028-KODIAK-2` and `… cdsp CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1`). Result: /usr/share/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/dsp/adsp/ and .../dsp/cdsp/ as separate directories (FILES:${PN}-radxa-dragon-q6a-{adsp,cdsp} in hexagon-dsp-binaries_20260622.bb). No file is ever shadowed.

**Ours today:** package/qcom-dsp-shell/qcom-dsp-shell.mk copies both version dirs into ONE flat directory: `cp -a $(@D)/$(QCOM_DSP_SHELL_CDSP_DIR)/. $(TARGET_DIR)/usr/lib/dsp/` then `cp -a $(@D)/$(QCOM_DSP_SHELL_ADSP_DIR)/. $(TARGET_DIR)/usr/lib/dsp/`. The two dirs share exactly four filenames — libsysmon_skel.so, libsysmondomain_skel.so, libsysmonquery_skel.so, libstabilitydomain_skel.so — so the ADSP.HT.5.5.c9-00028-KODIAK-2 builds win and the CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1 builds are lost (ADSP-only 27 files, CDSP-only 18 files, 4 colliding).

**Why it matters:** These are DSP-side skels loaded into the CDSP protection domain by libcdsprpc (sysmon/stability-domain queries, DSP capability probing, HAP power). A skel built for a different DSP image version is exactly the mismatch class that produces 0x80000600 / silent session-init failures, which is the single most-documented failure mode for this board. It also makes the /usr/lib/dsp tree unauditable against the frozen tuple.

**Action:** Same as proposed (per-domain install in package/qcom-dsp-shell/qcom-dsp-shell.mk; keep QCOM_DSP_SHELL_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE = /usr/lib/dsp; keep DSP_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/lib/dsp unchanged in rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config:32 and rootfs_overlay/etc/profile.d/qcom-npu-env.sh:3-4; update package/qcom-dsp-shell/Config.in help text and the BRINGUP.md §4 acceptance recipe), plus two additions: 1. Also move the qairt-runtime HTP skels into the CDSP domain dir for auditability — package/qairt-runtime/qairt-runtime.mk currently installs them flat into /usr/lib/dsp (comment at line 7, QAIRT_RUNTIME_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE = /usr/lib/dsp at line 19). They still load via fastrpc's bare-dir fallback, but they are CDSP artifacts; install to $(TARGET_DIR)/usr/lib/dsp/cdsp/ and keep the BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE at /usr/lib/dsp (covers subdirs). 2. Drop the "0x80000600 / silent session-init failure" wording from the Config.in / commit rationale for THIS change; that error class belongs to the shell-vs-cdsp.mbn version mismatch already documented in Config.in, not to the skel overwrite. Justify the skel fix as: the four sysmon/stability skels must be the CDSP-image builds (2.4 MB CDSP libsysmon_skel.so vs 185 KB ADSP one) or DSP-side load of those skels fails inside the CDSP PD. No change needed to erlinit.config / qcom-npu-env.sh: verified at fastrpc 706071c that get_dirlist_from_env() does not append the domain and fopen_from_dirlist() appends SUBSYSTEM_NAME[domain] exactly once, so /usr/lib/dsp is the correct value; and open_shell() does not even read the env var — it uses compiled-in DEFAULT_DSP_SEARCH_PATHS ";/usr/lib/rfsa/adsp;/usr/lib/dsp;" (src/Makefile.am:10).

**Uncertainty:** Whether the four colliding skels are actually opened during a plain fastrpc_test -a v68 run is unproven; the breakage may only show up under sysmon/HAP-power calls (which QNN does make). Fixing the layout is cheap and removes the question.

### [MAJOR] No validated inference runtime above fastrpc — Yocto's proven stack is onnxruntime+QNN EP wheels, ours is bare QAIRT blobs  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Two components, not one. (a) meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-ml/python3-onnxruntime-qnn/python3-onnxruntime-qnn_1.28.0.bb unpacks four pinned manylinux aarch64 cp314 wheels into ${PYTHON_SITEPACKAGES_DIR} via `python3 -m zipfile -e`: onnxruntime-1.28.0 (sha256 bc2565e4…), onnxruntime_qnn-2.4.0 (sha256 35be20e7…, DESCRIPTION: "QNN EP with bundled QNN libs and v68 skels"), flatbuffers-25.12.19, ml_dtypes-0.5.4; RDEPENDS python3-numpy/protobuf/sympy/packaging/coloredlogs + libstdc++; SKIP_FILE_DEPS:${PN}="1" and INSANE_SKIP:${PN}="arch already-stripped ldflags file-rdeps textrel". q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:41-42 installs python3 + python3-onnxruntime-qnn. This supplies the aarch64 HOST-side QNN libs, since only the -hexagon-v68 subpackage of qairt-sdk is pulled in and it has no RDEPENDS on the main package. (b) SEPARATELY, the QAIRT SDK is version-pinned and partly installed: meta-qcom/recipes-ml/qairt/qairt-sdk_2.47.0.260601.bb (SRC_URI[sha256sum]=d3497e110eae82c35a9152a93c0a18bbede402aaf9faa7a97c8079eb0f522b01), and meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:66-72 `MACHINE_ESSENTIA…

**Ours today:** blobs/qairt-runtime/ carries only QAIRT SDK 2.42.0.251225 host libs (libQnnHtp.so, libQnnHtpV68Stub.so, libQnnSystem.so), the v68 skel (usr/lib/dsp/libQnnHtpV68Skel.so) and qnn-platform-validator; libQnnHtpPrepare.so deliberately omitted (blobs/qairt-runtime/README.md). No Python in nerves_defconfig; the intended consumer is an unwritten Elixir NIF (hexagon_tpu) loading pre-compiled QNN context binaries.

**Why it matters:** The Yocto system's NPU claim rests on a specific, downloadable, version-locked runtime; ours rests on an unwritten binding plus a QAIRT version (2.42.0) that neither the Yocto image (which effectively uses the wheel-bundled QNN) nor the Radxa debugging notes (validated 2.37.1) exercised. Without deciding this, "NPU works" has no test on our side beyond fastrpc_test.

**Action:** (a) Align the QAIRT pin rather than inventing one: Yocto's proven board runs QAIRT 2.47.0.260601 skels; blobs/qairt-runtime is 2.42.0.251225. Either re-harvest blobs/qairt-runtime/ from 2.47.0.260601 or record explicitly why 2.42 is kept, and add a "QAIRT/QNN runtime" row to GOAL.md's Frozen version tuple (it currently has none) naming the SDK version, the four vendored files, and the source dirs lib/aarch64-oe-linux-gcc11.2/, lib/hexagon-v68/unsigned/, bin/aarch64-oe-linux-gcc11.2/. (b) Fix or prove the SONAME dependency, which 2.42 predates: readelf shows blobs/qairt-runtime/usr/lib/libQnnHtpV68Stub.so has DT_NEEDED libcdsprpc.so (unversioned) while package/qcom-fastrpc installs SONAME libcdsprpc.so.1. Verify on target that /usr/lib/libcdsprpc.so exists in TARGET_DIR (`ldd /usr/lib/libQnnHtpV68Stub.so` must resolve); if not, either bump to SDK >= 2.45 or add a host-patchelf post-install hook in package/qairt-runtime/qairt-runtime.mk mirroring Yocto's do_patch_qairt_needed_soname. (c) If the Elixir NIF path stays, drop libQnnHtpPrepare.so into blobs/qairt-runtime/usr/lib/ (needed the moment a graph is prepared on-device instead of loaded from a cached context binary) and libQnnHtpNetRunExtensions.so + bin/qnn-net-run if a backend-config (vtcm_mb, htp_arch v68) or bench harness is wanted; no .mk change is needed since QAIRT_RUNTIME_INSTALL_TARGET_CMDS does `cp -a $(@D)/usr $(TARGET_DIR)/`, but the sha256 manifest in blobs/qairt-runtime/README.md must be updated. (d) Note the skel-path divergence in that README: we use flat /usr/lib/dsp (DSP_LIBRARY_PATH/ADSP_LIBRARY_PATH set in rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config:32 and rootfs_overlay/etc/profile.d/qcom-npu-env.sh), Yocto uses /usr/share/qcom/qcm6490/Thundercomm/RB3gen2/dsp/cdsp — Yocto/Radxa-bundle instructions will not transfer verbatim. (e) Add an acceptance step beyond fastrpc_test -a v68 (qnn-platform-validator, or one cached QNN context binary inference) to BRINGUP.md's table. (f) A Python parity path is a decision, not a task: BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON3=y plus BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_NUMPY and BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_PROTOBUF exist, but Buildroot ships no python-sympy or python-coloredlogs package (verify against the 2026.05 tree), so it means new Buildroot packages plus a wheel-unpacking package and a large image delta — surface it, do not do it silently.

**Uncertainty:** Which QNN/QAIRT versions are actually compatible with CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1 is unproven for 2.42.0; only 2.37.1 has board evidence. Yocto's own 2.47.0 SDK skels are installed at a path this board never searches (see the qairt-sdk finding), so its ORT wheel's bundled skels are what would run there.

### [minor] cdsprpcd is started once, before the CDSP may be up, with no restart or device trigger  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** fastrpc_1.0.6.bb (meta-qcom/recipes-support/fastrpc/) enables seven units via SYSTEMD_SERVICE:${PN} (adsprpcd, adsprpcd_audiopd, cdsprpcd, cdsp1rpcd, gdsp0rpcd, gdsp1rpcd, sdsprpcd) and ships 60-fastrpc.rules into ${nonarch_base_libdir}/udev/rules.d. Those two data files are NOT in the Yocto tree (only fastrpc_1.0.6.bb + fastrpc/run-ptest are) — they come from upstream github.com/qualcomm/fastrpc tag v1.0.6, files/60-fastrpc.rules and files/cdsprpcd.service.in. Verified verbatim: the rule is `ACTION=="add", SUBSYSTEM=="misc", KERNEL=="fastrpc-cdsp", OWNER="root", GROUP="fastrpc", MODE="0640", TAG+="systemd", ENV{SYSTEMD_WANTS}+="cdsprpcd.service"` (mirrored for -secure and for cdsp1/adsp/gdsp0/gdsp1/sdsp); the unit has `After=dev-fastrpc\x2dcdsp.device dev-fastrpc\x2dcdsp\x2dsecure.device`, `ConditionPathExists=|/dev/fastrpc-cdsp`, `Type=exec`, `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=5`. Note the units are enabled by default only because nothing sets SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE:pn-fastrpc = "disable" (kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml:76 disables only weston-init), and fastrpc lands in the image transit…

**Ours today:** Confirmed as stated. qcom-coldplug.sh step 3 starts cdsprpcd/adsprpcd once, in the background, gated only on the binary being executable and not already running — no check that /dev/fastrpc-cdsp exists, no retry, no supervisor. qcom-fastrpc.mk removes both upstream 60-fastrpc.rules and usr/lib/sysusers.d/fastrpc.conf, and our replacement rootfs_overlay/etc/udev/rules.d/99-qcom-npu.rules only sets MODE=0666 on fastrpc-*/dma_heap — it has no RUN or start action, so no device-triggered launch exists. Only one erlinit hook exists (--pre-run-exec /usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh). Also worth recording as a deliberate divergence: upstream sets the non-secure nodes root:fastrpc 0640 with an ACL for the fastrpc group; we set 0666 (documented in qcom-fastrpc.mk as the RadxaOS approach), which is looser but has no group/ACL machinery to depend on.

**Why it matters:** Boot-order race: the DSP daemons provide the reverse-RPC listener used for DSP→CPU callbacks. A silent one-shot failure looks identical to a working system until an inference call hangs or falls back, and there is no supervisor to recover it.

**Action:** Do the cheap, correct half only, in rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh: before the daemon loop, poll for the node (`i=0; while [ $i -lt 10 ]; do [ -e /dev/fastrpc-cdsp ] || [ -e /dev/fastrpc-cdsp-secure ] && break; i=$((i+1)); sleep 1; done`), then start cdsprpcd only if /dev/fastrpc-cdsp or /dev/fastrpc-cdsp-secure exists and adsprpcd only if /dev/fastrpc-adsp or /dev/fastrpc-adsp-secure exists. Comment it with the reference-system fact that FastRPC works with zero rpcd processes (/tmp/IMAGE_BUILD.md:113-114), so this is opportunistic, not required. DROP the udev RUN+= suggestion: udev(7) states RUN is for very short-running foreground tasks and udevd kills children that outlive the event — that is exactly why upstream uses ENV{SYSTEMD_WANTS} instead of RUN, and we have no systemd to receive it. If a device-triggered start is genuinely wanted later, the udev rule must only drop a marker (e.g. RUN+="/bin/touch /run/fastrpc-cdsp.ready") with the actual daemon launch done by a detached watcher, or, preferably, supervise it from the Elixir app (a MuonTrap.Daemon child) — that belongs in the application repo, not in this system repo, so surface it as a design note rather than a change here.

**Uncertainty:** Unknown how long after boot /dev/fastrpc-cdsp appears with our =y remoteproc build (Yocto uses modules); the 10 s settle may already be enough in practice.

### [minor] fastrpc_test ships Hexagon DSP6 ELFs into /usr/share with no BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** fastrpc_1.0.6.bb splits them into a fastrpc-tests package with INSANE_SKIP:${PN}-tests += "arch libdir ldflags" and FILES ${bindir}/fastrpc_test, ${libdir}/fastrpc_test/*.so, ${datadir}/fastrpc_test; the image explicitly installs fastrpc-tests.

**Ours today:** package/qcom-fastrpc/qcom-fastrpc.mk sets no QCOM_FASTRPC_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE. Upstream test/Makefile.am installs bin_PROGRAMS fastrpc_test and dsp_check, dist_testlib_DATA = linux/lib{calculator,hap_example,multithreading}.so into $(libdir)/fastrpc_test (AArch64, fine) and nobase_dist_testdsp_DATA = {v68,v75}/lib*_skel.so into $(datadir)/fastrpc_test — those six files are 'QUALCOMM DSP6 Processor' ELFs (verified with readelf -h).

**Why it matters:** Buildroot's check-bin-arch walks the package file list and aborts on foreign-architecture ELFs; the two other blob packages needed exactly this exemption (QCOM_DSP_SHELL_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE, QAIRT_RUNTIME_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE). If it does trip, the build fails after fastrpc installs; if it does not, the exclude is a no-op.

**Action:** Add to package/qcom-fastrpc/qcom-fastrpc.mk: `QCOM_FASTRPC_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE = /usr/share/fastrpc_test /usr/lib/fastrpc_test` (the second entry also covers the prebuilt aarch64 test libs, which are built with a foreign toolchain). While editing, note in the comment that this fastrpc commit (v1.0.6-7-g706071c) also ships /usr/bin/dsp_check — use it as the first-boot readiness probe (it reports DSP online + offload-capable and resolves firmware/skel locations), and document it in BRINGUP.md alongside fastrpc_test.

**Uncertainty:** Buildroot's check-bin-arch source was not available locally, so whether it flags ELFs under /usr/share (as opposed to only bin/lib dirs) is unconfirmed; the project claims current builds are clean, which would mean the exclude is precautionary.

### [minor] libQnnHtpV68Stub.so DT_NEEDED is the unversioned libcdsprpc.so — the symlink and libatomic must survive into the image  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Two explicit fixes: meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-support/fastrpc/fastrpc_%.bbappend adds RPROVIDES:${PN} += "libcdsprpc.so()(64bit)" and hexagon-dsp-binaries_%.bbappend adds RPROVIDES:${PN}-radxa-dragon-q6a-cdsp += "libc++.so.1 libc++abi.so.1" (sha 9242ba1); fastrpc_1.0.6.bb keeps the unversioned .so files in the runtime package (FILES:${PN} += ${libdir}/libcdsprpc.so …, FILES:${PN}-dev:remove = ${FILES_SOLIBSDEV}, INSANE_SKIP:${PN} = "dev-so") with the comment "We need to include lib*dsprpc.so into fastrpc for compatibility with Hexagon SDK"; qairt-sdk even patchelf-rewrites libcdsprpc.so → libcdsprpc.so.1 as a belt-and-braces measure.

**Ours today:** package/qcom-fastrpc is a plain autotools-package (INSTALL_STAGING=YES) so libtool installs libcdsprpc.so, .so.1 and .so.1.x into TARGET_DIR; nothing in the .mk or post-build.sh removes the unversioned symlink. Verified from the blobs: libQnnHtpV68Stub.so has NEEDED libcdsprpc.so (no soname version), libQnnSystem.so has NEEDED libatomic.so.1, all three QNN libs need libstdc++.so.6/libgcc_s.so.1. The CDSP libc++.so.1 / libc++abi.so.1 that QNN skels dlopen on the DSP side are in the CDSP dir and do not collide, so they survive our flat copy.

**Why it matters:** If the unversioned symlink or libatomic is absent, every QNN host library load fails with a plain 'cannot open shared object file' long before any DSP interaction — the exact class of breakage the Yocto side pre-empted at packaging level.

**Action:** Add a one-line assertion to post-build.sh (or a QCOM_FASTRPC post-install hook) that fails the build if the runtime deps are missing, e.g. `for l in usr/lib/libcdsprpc.so usr/lib/libadsprpc.so; do [ -e "$TARGET_DIR/$l" ] || { echo "missing $l (QNN stubs DT_NEEDED it unversioned)"; exit 1; }; done` and likewise check for libatomic.so.1 (if the Nerves external toolchain does not copy it, add BR2_TOOLCHAIN_EXTERNAL_... libatomic or drop libQnnSystem's consumer). Record in blobs/qairt-runtime/README.md that libatomic.so.1 is a hard requirement of libQnnSystem.so.

**Uncertainty:** Buildroot's target-finalize behaviour for unversioned .so symlinks and the Nerves external-toolchain library copy list were not verified on disk; the assertion makes the outcome explicit either way.

### [info] TEE: qteesupplicant is not needed for the NPU, but the Radxa kernel's QSEECOM/TZMEM choices are missing from our fragment  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Yocto's custom kernel delta for TEE is exactly one symbol: meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-platform.cfg (3 lines) sets CONFIG_QCOMTEE=y, wired in by c7fd105. qteesupplicant comes from meta-qcom minkipc_1.2.9.bb (subpackage minkipc-qteesupplicant: /usr/bin/qtee_supplicant, /bin/sfs_config, /lib/udev/rules.d/99-qcomtee-udev.rules, /lib/qtee-tas/* TAs, units qteesupplicant.service + sfsconfig.service, RRECOMMENDS mount-tee-partition), pulled in transitively via packagegroup-qcom-security <- qcom-console-image. Commit sequence dcbf15b -> 7b95684 -> 0f32caa -> 3c045e5 confirmed; final state = CONFIG_QCOMTEE=y, supplicant enabled, /etc/systemd/system/tpm2.target -> /dev/null (the image .bb comment saying qteesupplicant is disabled via SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE in kas is STALE; kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml has no such override). CORRECTION: the Radxa/Deka 6.18.2 arm64 defconfig (QCOM_TZMEM_MODE_SHMBRIDGE / QCOM_QSEECOM / QCOM_QSEECOM_UEFISECAPP / TEE / OPTEE) is NOT part of the Yocto build — Yocto uses meta-qcom's linux-qcom. That defconfig is OUR kernel's base config.

**Ours today:** linux-dragon-q6a.fragment genuinely contains no TEE/QCOMTEE/QSEECOM/TZMEM line (only CONFIG_QCOM_SCM=y at line 16) — but that is not a gap. nerves_defconfig sets BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_CUSTOM_TARBALL to linux-dragon-q6a @ e05c4fae plus BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y, so our base .config is that tree's arch/arm64/configs/defconfig, which already has CONFIG_QCOM_TZMEM_MODE_SHMBRIDGE=y (269), CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM=y (270), CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM_UEFISECAPP=y (271), CONFIG_TEE=y (1707), CONFIG_OPTEE=y (1708). Fragments merge on top, so all five are already in our kernel; our forced CONFIG_QCOM_SCM=y and CONFIG_EFI=y satisfy their dependencies. The only real difference from Yocto is CONFIG_QCOMTEE (absent from the Radxa defconfig), i.e. no /dev/tee0. We also correctly have no qtee userspace, no /lib/qtee-tas, no /var/lib/tee, no tpm2. Separately, UEFISECAPP is irrelevant to our A/B: no efibootmgr/efivar exists anywhere in the tree; grubenv is a FAT file on the ESP created host-side by grub-editenv (post-build.sh), written by fwup fat_write (fwup-ops.conf), and updated by GRUB's own save_…

**Why it matters:** Determination: qtee_supplicant serves QTEE callbacks for secure file storage and loading TAs from /lib/qtee-tas. Nothing in the fastrpc/QNN path uses it — unsigned PD authorization is done by QTEE itself via the SPI boot firmware, and the a660 zap shader is loaded by drm/msm through qcom_scm PAS, not by a userspace supplicant. So omitting it is safe and is the right call for Nerves. What is NOT safe to omit silently is CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM_UEFISECAPP: on QCS6490 that driver is how Linux reaches UEFI variables, which is exactly what efibootmgr / a GRUB env write would need; and QCOM_TZMEM_MODE_SHMBRIDGE is the SCM memory mode the vendor kernel ships for this SoC.

**Action:** 1) Do NOT port minkipc/qteesupplicant — confirmed Buildroot 2026.05 ships optee-client/optee-examples/optee-test but no minkipc and no qcomtee library, so it would mean two new packages (plus qcbor, glib) for a daemon nothing in the fastrpc/QNN path calls. Record the reasoning in GOAL.md/BRINGUP.md so it is not revisited. 2) Do NOT add CONFIG_QCOM_TZMEM_MODE_SHMBRIDGE / CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM / CONFIG_QCOM_QSEECOM_UEFISECAPP / CONFIG_TEE / CONFIG_OPTEE as a "fix" — they are already =y from the pinned arch defconfig (lines 269-271, 1707-1708). At most pin them explicitly with a comment, in the same defensive style already used for CONFIG_R8169 / CONFIG_USB_VIDEO_CLASS / CONFIG_QRTR, so they survive a future kernel bump; that is hardening, not a gap. 3) Optional, low value: add CONFIG_QCOMTEE=m (or =y) purely to get /dev/tee0 for parity/diagnostics — harmless with no supplicant; note it selects QCOM_TZMEM_MODE_SHMBRIDGE (already set) and QCOM_SCM. 4) Drop the "diff .config for a conflicting TZMEM mode" step as written — the defconfig already selects SHMBRIDGE over the Kconfig default GENERIC. The worthwhile version is the generic post-build check we should run anyway: grep the generated .config for the ~15 symbols the fragment forces to =y where the defconfig has =m, to catch olddefconfig demotions.

**Uncertainty:** Whether QCOM_QSEECOM_UEFISECAPP is actually required for our GRUB A/B scheme depends on whether anything writes EFI variables (our design uses grubenv on the ESP, so possibly not). Also unverified whether the mainline 6.18 arm64 defconfig already enables QSEECOM/TEE, i.e. whether these lines are additions or just pins.

### [info] Yocto's qairt-sdk-hexagon-v68 installs the v68 skels to the RB3gen2 path this board never searches — do not copy that layout  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** meta-qcom/recipes-ml/qairt/qairt-sdk_2.47.0.260601.bb installs lib/hexagon-v68/unsigned/* to ${datadir}/qcom/qcm6490/Thundercomm/RB3gen2/dsp/cdsp (package qairt-sdk-hexagon-v68), which is the only qairt package the machine conf pulls (MACHINE_ESSENTIAL_EXTRA_RRECOMMENDS in meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:65-72). The board's fastrpc resolves DSP_LIBS_LOCATION from conf.d/hexagon-dsp-binaries-radxa-dragon-q6a.yaml, whose value is `qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/dsp` — plus the compiled-in defaults ";/usr/lib/rfsa/adsp;/usr/lib/dsp;". The RB3gen2 directory is in none of them, and the main qairt-sdk package (host libQnn*.so, headers, qnn-net-run, libQnnHtpPrepare.so) is not installed at all.

**Ours today:** package/qairt-runtime installs libQnnHtpV68Skel.so to /usr/lib/dsp, which IS on fastrpc's default search path (via the bare-dir fallback in fopen_from_dirlist), and the host libs to /usr/lib. Functionally more correct than the Yocto arrangement.

**Why it matters:** Prevents a wrong 'port the Yocto path' conclusion, and explains why the Yocto image's QNN necessarily relies on the ORT wheel's bundled skels rather than the SDK package. It also pins where our skel must live once qcom-dsp-shell moves to per-domain subdirs.

**Action:** Keep our own placement, but make it domain-explicit alongside the qcom-dsp-shell fix: install the QNN skel to /usr/lib/dsp/cdsp/libQnnHtpV68Skel.so in package/qairt-runtime/qairt-runtime.mk (move blobs/qairt-runtime/usr/lib/dsp/libQnnHtpV68Skel.so → blobs/qairt-runtime/usr/lib/dsp/cdsp/libQnnHtpV68Skel.so and update the sha manifest/source-path notes in blobs/qairt-runtime/README.md). Do not create /usr/share/qcom/qcm6490/Thundercomm/RB3gen2/… anywhere.

**Uncertainty:** It is possible some component on the Yocto image sets ADSP_LIBRARY_PATH to the site-packages or RB3gen2 dir at runtime; nothing in the layer does, and their README only claims fastrpc_test as the validated acceptance test, so on-board QNN inference appears untested there too.

### [info] conf.d YAML machine-name resolution is absent on our side (harmless today, matters if the DSP tree ever moves)  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** hexagon-dsp-binaries also installs /usr/share/qcom/conf.d/*.yaml and schema.json (Makefile install target; the auto-generated package hexagon-dsp-binaries-radxa-dragon-q6a-config is force-added to RDEPENDS by the python() snippet in hexagon-dsp-binaries_20260622.bb). fastrpc reads /sys/firmware/devicetree/base/model → "Radxa Dragon Q6A" → the YAML → prefixes CONFIG_BASE_DIR (/usr/share/qcom) and appends the compiled-in defaults. This is why the Yocto README says 'no env vars needed'.

**Ours today:** We install no YAML and no /usr/share/qcom; parse_config_dir's opendir fails silently and DSP_LIBS_LOCATION stays at the compiled-in ";/usr/lib/rfsa/adsp;/usr/lib/dsp;", which is exactly where we install. We additionally set DSP_LIBRARY_PATH=ADSP_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/lib/dsp in rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config and /etc/profile.d/qcom-npu-env.sh (env values are prepended to DSP_LIBS_LOCATION by get_dirlist_from_env, so this is additive, not overriding).

**Why it matters:** Confirms our env-var approach is sound and that no YAML is required — but the shell lookup (fastrpc_shell_unsigned_3) uses DSP_LIBS_LOCATION *only*, ignoring the env vars, so /usr/lib/dsp must keep working as a compiled-in default. Any future move of the DSP tree out of /usr/lib/dsp silently breaks shell loading unless the YAML is added.

**Action:** No change required now. Add a comment to package/qcom-dsp-shell/Config.in and rootfs_overlay/etc/profile.d/qcom-npu-env.sh stating that /usr/lib/dsp is a *compiled-in* fastrpc default (src/Makefile.am DEFAULT_DSP_SEARCH_PATHS) and that the shell lookup ignores DSP_LIBRARY_PATH. If the tree is ever relocated, either pass --with-config-base-dir and install a YAML, e.g. /usr/share/qcom/conf.d/hexagon-dsp-binaries-radxa-dragon-q6a.yaml containing `machines:\n Radxa Dragon Q6A:\n DSP_LIBRARY_PATH: <relative-path>`, or keep a /usr/lib/dsp symlink.

### [info] Device permissions and firmware file set match; only the audio topology blob and the CMA sizing differ  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** fastrpc's 60-fastrpc.rules uses group-based access: fastrpc-* nodes root:fastrpc 0640 (group created via sysusers.d/fastrpc.conf) and /dev/dma_heap/system via `setfacl -m g:fastrpc:rw`; the Yocto README documents running the inference app as root or joining the fastrpc/dmaheap groups. Firmware for this board comes from linux-firmware 20260622 packages qcom-qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a-audio (qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/adsp*.* plus QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin) and -compute (…/cdsp*.*), with the DSP kernel bits as modules (kernel-module-fastrpc, -qcom-q6v5-pas, -qcom-pd-mapper, -qcom-sysmon in packagegroup-machine-essential). Radxa's 6.18.2 defconfig has CONFIG_CMA_SIZE_MBYTES=32.

**Ours today:** rootfs_overlay/etc/udev/rules.d/99-qcom-npu.rules sets `SUBSYSTEM=="misc", KERNEL=="fastrpc-*", MODE="0666"` and `SUBSYSTEM=="dma_heap", MODE="0666"`, with a chmod belt-and-braces in qcom-coldplug.sh — the same 0666 approach /tmp/IMAGE_BUILD.md §6 validated, and the right call with no sysusers/ACL on Nerves. blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware carries adsp.mbn, adspr.jsn, adspua.jsn, cdsp.mbn, cdspr.jsn at qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/ (matching the DTS firmware-name properties exactly) plus the qcm6490 fallbacks, a660 zap/sqe/gmu, venus and qupv3fw, with SHA256SUMS. Everything DSP-related is =y in linux-dragon-q6a.fragment (QCOM_Q6V5_PAS, QCOM_SYSMON, QCOM_QMI_HELPERS, QCOM_PDR_HELPERS, QCOM_PD_MAPPER, QCOM_FASTRPC, DMABUF_HEAPS{,_SYSTEM,_CMA}, QRTR/QRTR_SMD) with CONFIG_CMA_SIZE_MBYTES=512.

**Why it matters:** These are the parts that are already correct — worth recording so they are not churned. Two small deltas: we do not ship QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin (part of the same upstream ADSP firmware package; audio topology, needed by the ASoC machine driver, not by fastrpc), and our CMA pool is 16x the vendor value.

**Action:** (a) Optionally vendor lib/firmware/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin (and the qcom/qcs6490/ duplicate path) into blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/ and refresh SHA256SUMS — hand off to the audio domain. (b) Re-measure CMA once fastrpc works: mainline sc7280 fastrpc allocates from the dma_heap system heap and the vendor kernel runs with 32 MB, so CONFIG_CMA_SIZE_MBYTES=512 in linux-dragon-q6a.fragment is probably over-provisioned; keep it for bring-up, then trim (it is overridable at boot with cma=). (c) No change to 99-qcom-npu.rules — the 0666 rules are deliberate and correct for BR2_INIT_NONE.

**Uncertainty:** The Radxa DTS additionally reserves adsp_rpc_remote_heap_mem@c6500000 and uses different adsp_mem/cdsp_mem/cdsp_secure_heap base addresses than the Deka DTS we build; whether the extra ADSP RPC remote heap is needed for audio-PD fastrpc (it is not used by the CDSP compute path) was not determined here — DTS domain.

---

## Platform firmware blobs and low-level SoC enablement (QCS6490 / sc7280), excluding GPU-userspace and DSP-runtime

The Yocto side's authoritative firmware list is `packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a{-firmware,-hexagon-dsp-binaries}` plus the SoC-level `packagegroup-machine-essential-qcom-qcs6490-soc` and `packagegroup-qcom-boot-essential`. Our Buildroot blob set (`blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware`) covers roughly 80% of it and is correct for the two remoteprocs, the Adreno zap/SQE/GMU and qupv3fw, but it is missing the audio topology binary `QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin` entirely and it symlinks `qcom/vpu-2.0/venus.mbn` to the wrong Venus blob (`vpu20_p4.mbn`, which upstream links as `vpu-1.0`; sc7280 needs `vpu20_p1.mbn`). We also ship ~16 MB of generic `qcm6490/{adsp,cdsp}.mbn` that the proven system deliberately does not install. Structurally the biggest difference is not files but *load timing and packaging*: the proven system builds the whole remoteproc/fastrpc/clock chain as loadable modules that system…


### [BLOCKER] Audio topology blob QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin is completely absent from our rootfs  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** Same as claimed, with one line-number nit: the RRECOMMENDS entry `linux-firmware-qcom-qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a-audio` is at packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a.bb line 14 (inside `RRECOMMENDS:${PN}-firmware`), not line 13 (line 13 is `linux-firmware-lt9611uxc`). Additional confirmation not in the original claim: the packagegroup actually reaches the image via `MACHINE_ESSENTIAL_EXTRA_RRECOMMENDS += " ... packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a-firmware ..."` at meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:66-72, and nothing in kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml or meta-q6a-nerveshub sets BAD_RECOMMENDATIONS/NO_RECOMMENDATIONS, so the tplg really is on the proven hardware image.

**Ours today:** absent. `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/` contains only adsp.mbn, adspr.jsn, adspua.jsn, cdsp.mbn, cdspr.jsn (see SHA256SUMS, 12 entries, no *-tplg.bin). `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md` does not mention the tplg at all.

**Why it matters:** Without the topology binary the audioreach ASoC card (`q6apm` / `SND_SOC_SC8280XP`) fails topology load and no PCM devices are created — the WCD9380 headset path and the DisplayPort/HDMI audio DAI link (`dp0-dai-link` -> `DISPLAY_PORT_RX_0`) both die. Our defconfig already builds the whole ALSA userspace (`BR2_PACKAGE_ALSA_UTILS*`) for a card that cannot come up.

**Action:** Add the real file plus the upstream symlink to the vendored blob tree, then re-hash with the tree's existing convention (-type f only, run from the dir that contains lib/firmware): ```sh cd /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware BASE=https://gitlab.com/kernel-firmware/linux-firmware/-/raw/main curl -fL -o lib/firmware/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin \ $BASE/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin ln -sf radxa/dragon-q6a/QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin \ lib/firmware/qcom/qcs6490/QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin (cd lib/firmware && find . -type f -exec sha256sum {} \;) > SHA256SUMS ``` (The original snippet's `cd .. && (cd lib/firmware && ...) > SHA256SUMS` runs from `lib/`, fails, and leaves an empty `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/SHA256SUMS` while the real SHA256SUMS stays stale. Also drop `-o -type l`: the existing SHA256SUMS deliberately omits symlinks — qcs6490/{adsp,cdsp,a660_zap}.mbn and vpu-2.0/venus.mbn are symlinks and are not listed — and hashing a symlink just duplicates its target's hash.) Also document the file in `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md` (Layout section + the Refreshing snippet). Relaxing the `*.mbn`/`*.mdt` guard at `package/qcom-dsp-firmware/qcom-dsp-firmware.mk:29` to also accept `-name '*-tplg.bin' -o -name '*.fw' -o -name '*.elf'` is optional hardening only — the .mbn files stay in the tree, so the guard passes today and the tplg is installed by the same `cp -a`; it is not required for this fix. No change to nerves_defconfig is needed (`BR2_PACKAGE_QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE=y` already at line 67).

**Uncertainty:** I did not read the kernel source that builds the request_firmware path, so I cannot say from first principles whether 6.18's audioreach loader asks for `qcom/qcs6490/QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin` or the deep `radxa/dragon-q6a/...` path. Shipping both (real file + symlink, exactly as upstream linux-firmware and the Yocto package do) makes the question moot.

### [MAJOR] qcom/vpu-2.0/venus.mbn points at the wrong Venus blob (vpu20_p4 instead of vpu20_p1)  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** `linux-firmware-qcom-vpu` installs `${firmwaredir}/qcom/vpu/*`, `${firmwaredir}/qcom/vpu-1.0/*` and `${firmwaredir}/qcom/vpu-2.0/*` verbatim from upstream. Upstream WHENCE: `File: qcom/vpu/vpu20_p1.mbn` / `Link: qcom/vpu-2.0/venus.mbn -> ../vpu/vpu20_p1.mbn` (Version VIDEO.VPU.2.0-00055-PROD-1), and separately `File: qcom/vpu/vpu20_p4.mbn` / `Link: qcom/vpu-1.0/venus.mbn -> ../vpu/vpu20_p4.mbn` (Version VIDEO.VPU.1.0-00119). The Q6A DTS enables `&venus { status = "okay"; }` on the mainline `qcom,sc7280-venus` node, whose driver fwname is `qcom/vpu-2.0/venus.mbn`.

**Ours today:** `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware/qcom/vpu-2.0/venus.mbn` is a symlink to `../vpu/vpu20_p4.mbn`, and `qcom/vpu/` contains only `vpu20_p4.mbn` (sha256 a66e86e34dc69a870fe631c73ed4a93d9fcfea6f3f2a4ac02cb118cb505bbbb2 — verified identical to the upstream vpu20_p4.mbn, i.e. the VPU **1.0** image). `vpu20_p1.mbn` is not shipped at all. `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md` documents the wrong mapping (`vpu-2.0/venus.mbn -> ../vpu/vpu20_p4.mbn`).

**Why it matters:** The venus core will hand a VPU-1.0 firmware image to the PAS/TZ authenticator for a VPU-2.0 block. Best case authentication fails and `CONFIG_VIDEO_QCOM_VENUS=m` never produces `/dev/video*`; that kills the whole HW-codec chain we already paid for in the defconfig (`BR2_PACKAGE_GST1_PLUGINS_GOOD_PLUGIN_V4L2`, `BR2_PACKAGE_FFMPEG`, `*_v4l2m2m`).

**Action:** Fetch the right blob and repoint the symlink: ```sh cd /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware curl -fL -o qcom/vpu/vpu20_p1.mbn \ https://gitlab.com/kernel-firmware/linux-firmware/-/raw/main/qcom/vpu/vpu20_p1.mbn ln -sfn ../vpu/vpu20_p1.mbn qcom/vpu-2.0/venus.mbn ``` Keep `vpu20_p4.mbn` only if you also add `qcom/vpu-1.0/venus.mbn -> ../vpu/vpu20_p4.mbn` (mirrors upstream); otherwise drop it to save 1.9 MB per slot. Fix the layout table in `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md` and regenerate `SHA256SUMS`.

**Uncertainty:** I could not read `drivers/media/platform/qcom/venus/core.c` from the pinned tree (not checked out) to re-confirm that `sc7280_res.fwname == "qcom/vpu-2.0/venus.mbn"`. The path itself is not in dispute (it is what our README and the upstream vpu-2.0 symlink both target); what is in dispute is only which blob the symlink resolves to, and upstream is unambiguous.

### [MAJOR] Built-in QCOM_Q6V5_PAS with no initramfs races /lib/firmware; the proven system loads it as a module after rootfs mount  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Right conclusion, wrong evidence chain. The decisive citation is /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/README.md:66-68, which states it outright: "DSP boot firmware in rootfs: `/lib/firmware/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/` (`adsp.mbn`, `cdsp.mbn` = `CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1` + jsn). Remoteproc is modular here, so the Radxa initramfs-hook problem does not apply." That both confirms remoteproc is =m in the proven image AND records that the firmware-before-rootfs failure was already hit on this board by the Radxa vendor-image team. Corrections to the original write-up: - The packagegroup RRECOMMENDS are NOT proof. meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/linux-qcom_%.bbappend replaces the kernel source with `git://github.com/radxa/kernel.git;branch=linux-6.18.2` @ SRCREV 559f4f921a01e5358602153364c618fe2a3e431e and clears the defconfig merge (`KBUILD_CONFIG_EXTRA = ""` plus `:remove:aarch64` of ${S}/arch/arm64/configs/{prune,qcom}.config — "its defconfig is standalone"). The builtin/module split therefore comes from Radxa's vendor defconfig, not meta-qcom. And RRECOMMENDS…

**Ours today:** Gap confirmed by direct reading, with two corrections to the symbol list. linux-dragon-q6a.fragment: :48 CONFIG_QCOM_GENI_SE=y, :67 CONFIG_REMOTEPROC=y, :69 CONFIG_QCOM_PIL_INFO=y, :70 CONFIG_QCOM_Q6V5_COMMON=y, :71 CONFIG_QCOM_Q6V5_PAS=y, :72 CONFIG_QCOM_SYSMON=y, :77 CONFIG_QCOM_PD_MAPPER=y, :78 CONFIG_QCOM_FASTRPC=y. CONFIG_QCOM_MDT_LOADER does not appear in our fragment at all. The =y choice is deliberate and its stated rationale is the bug. Fragment header lines 4-7: "Boot without initramfs: everything between reset and mounting the squashfs root on SD/UFS must be built-in (=y), and the whole FastRPC/remoteproc chain for the Hexagon NPU must be =y so no module loading is needed before the CDSP comes up." Building the driver in does not remove the filesystem dependency — cdsp.mbn/adsp.mbn still live in the slot squashfs (package/qcom-dsp-firmware installs blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/{adsp,cdsp}.mbn + *.jsn) — it only removes the guarantee that a filesystem is mounted when auto_boot fires. No mitigation exists anywhere in the tree: - No initr…

**Why it matters:** `qcom_q6v5_pas` probes with `auto_boot`, which issues `request_firmware_nowait()` for `qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/{adsp,cdsp}.mbn`. As a built-in driver that work item can run before the root filesystem is mounted, and with `CONFIG_FW_LOADER_USER_HELPER` off there is no wait-for-userspace fallback — the result is `Direct firmware load for ... failed with error -2` and both remoteprocs sitting `offline`, so FastRPC/NPU never comes up. Same class of risk for `qupv3fw.elf` under built-in `QCOM_GENI_SE`. This is exactly the kind of failure our QEMU-only validation cannot surface.

**Action:** Reorder: do (b) first as the primary fix, treat (a) as a follow-up that needs hardware validation. Record the choice in GOAL.md next to the "No nerves_initramfs" line, and fix the fragment header comment at lines 4-7, whose stated rationale for =y is wrong (the .mbn files are on the rootfs either way). (b) PRIMARY — additive, zero-risk, no systemd, works whether the chain is =y or =m. In rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh, after the `udevadm settle` block (step 1) and before the `chmod 666 /dev/fastrpc-*` block: # Built-in qcom_q6v5_pas auto_boot can fire before the squashfs root is # mounted, so cdsp.mbn/adsp.mbn load with -ENOENT and the remoteprocs sit # "offline". Retry now that /lib/firmware is available. See # radxa-q6a-yocto/README.md:66-68 ("the Radxa initramfs-hook problem"). for rp in /sys/class/remoteproc/remoteproc*; do [ -w "$rp/state" ] || continue if [ "$(cat "$rp/state" 2>/dev/null)" = "offline" ]; then echo start > "$rp/state" 2>/dev/null || true fi done Writing "start" makes rproc_boot re-issue request_firmware synchronously, which now resolves. Guard with `|| true`/`continue` because the script runs under `set -e`. Add the resulting state to BRINGUP.md's acceptance check (`cat /sys/class/remoteproc/remoteproc*/state` must read "running", matching radxa-q6a-yocto/README.md:93). (a) FOLLOW-UP — mirror the proven config, but the original recipe is wrong in detail: - In linux-dragon-q6a.fragment set `CONFIG_QCOM_Q6V5_PAS=m` (:71) AND delete or flip the pins at :69 `CONFIG_QCOM_PIL_INFO=y`, :70 `CONFIG_QCOM_Q6V5_COMMON=y`, :72 `CONFIG_QCOM_SYSMON=y`. These are select-only symbols; leaving them pinned =y while PAS=m produces a self-contradictory fragment that merge_config/olddefconfig will resolve unpredictably. They cannot be "left to follow". - Do not mention CONFIG_QCOM_MDT_LOADER — it is not in our fragment. - Keep `CONFIG_REMOTEPROC=y`, `CONFIG_QCOM_PD_MAPPER=y`, `CONFIG_QCOM_FASTRPC=y`, `CONFIG_QRTR=y`/`CONFIG_QRTR_SMD=y` (the :210-213 comment about olddefconfig demoting the chain still applies). - The explicit `modprobe qcom_q6v5_pas || true` in qcom-coldplug.sh is belt-and-braces only: eudev coldplug already autoloads it by OF modalias, which is how CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m comes up today. busybox modprobe exists (nerves-common busybox.config:576), so the line is valid, not a no-op. - Validate on hardware before shipping: the same proven layer had to move gpucc/dispcc/msm from =m to =y to fix an adreno SMMU probe-order failure (-110, meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg). The =y/=m split there is per-driver a…

**Uncertainty:** I did not read `drivers/base/firmware_loader/main.c` or `remoteproc_core.c` from the pinned 6.18 tree, so I cannot prove the race fires every boot rather than sometimes. The proven system sidesteps it entirely by using modules, which is the safe thing to copy.

### [MAJOR] SPI-NOR boot firmware >= 6.0.260120 is a hard prerequisite for the NPU; we ship no way to check or flash it  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Accurate as claimed (all quotes verified verbatim in q6a-spi-firmware_260120.bb, files/q6a-spi-flash, edl-ng_1.5.0.bb, q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:47-48, commit ad69735). One caveat the claim inherits uncritically: nothing in the Yocto tree sets `CONFIG_DMI_SYSFS` (grep across meta-q6a-nerveshub/meta-qcom*/kas: no hits), and it is `default n` in drivers/firmware/Kconfig, so on arm64 `dmidecode -s bios-version` in `q6a-spi-flash:10` most likely yields `unknown` and the "already up to date" no-op at line 14 never triggers. Commit ad69735 only verified file presence in the rootfs, not the version-check flow. Do not copy that check verbatim.

**Ours today:** Partly present. `BRINGUP.md` §1 (lines 14-34) already documents the host-side EDL flash with the identical payload invocation (`sudo ./edl-ng --memory spinor --loader prog_firehose_ddr.elf rawprogram rawprogram0.xml patch0.xml`), states "Armbian notes the **latest UEFI firmware is required**", and has a fill-in blank "**Observed UEFI version string** (`QC_IMAGE_VERSION_STRING=BOOT...KODIAK...` from the boot log): ______ → record in `GOAL.md`". `GOAL.md:103` is `| UEFI firmware (board-side, QSPI NOR) | record from bench boot log (Phase 0) |`. Genuinely missing (repo-wide grep for `260120|KODIAKWP|bios_version|bios-version|dmidecode` hits nothing outside BRINGUP.md): the numeric floor `6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1`, the unsigned-DSP-protection-domain rationale, any on-target read of the running version, and the on-device flash payload/tooling.

**Why it matters:** `fastrpc_test -a v68` and every QNN workload use unsigned protection domains. On a board that shipped with older SPI firmware the DSP rejects them regardless of how correct our cdsp.mbn / shell / fastrpc userspace are, so the headline bench acceptance criterion in GOAL.md fails for a reason invisible from our side. The Yocto team hit this and paid for offline remediation on-device.

**Action:** 1. Record the floor in the `GOAL.md` frozen tuple, replacing "record from bench boot log (Phase 0)": `>= 6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1 (required for unsigned DSP protection domains / QNN; older firmware makes the NPU unusable)`. Add the same number + rationale to `BRINGUP.md` §1 next to the existing "latest UEFI firmware is required" line and the Observed-version blank. 2. On-target version read — do NOT rely on dmidecode alone. `CONFIG_DMI=y` is default on arm64+EFI (arch/arm64/Kconfig:2406-2409) which also gives `CONFIG_DMIID=y` (default y), so `cat /sys/class/dmi/id/bios_version` needs no new package and no fragment change. If `dmidecode` is wanted for parity with Yocto, `BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE=y` is valid on aarch64 but ALSO requires adding `CONFIG_DMI_SYSFS=y` to `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment` (default n, absent from arm64 defconfig) because arm64 has no legacy /dev/mem SMBIOS scan. 3. Log it at boot: in `rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh` (erlinit `--pre-run-exec`, no systemd), add e.g. `cat /sys/class/dmi/id/bios_version 2>/dev/null > /dev/kmsg || true` alongside a `board_vendor`/`product_name` read, so every bench log records the firmware version. Optionally surface it in Elixir by reading the same sysfs path. 4. Keep flashing host-side. BRINGUP.md §1 already has the exact EDL command; just pin the payload (`dragon-q6a_flat_build_wp_260120.zip`, sha256 `cf23a1742ae5d51c451947d82cb21fd3f1c10bbcd621d611f55520673e3f90ca`) and the `edl-ng` version there. Reproducing the on-device path (`package/edl-ng/` + `package/q6a-spi-firmware/` registered in `Config.in`/`external.mk`) is optional and lower priority: `/dev/mtd0` writes can brick the board, and its existence is not guaranteed by our kernel config since the DTB comes from the UEFI-supplied FDT — confirm `/dev/mtd0` and `/proc/mtd` on the bench before assuming that path works at all.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the Dragon Q6A units on our bench already ship >= 260120 is unknown; only `dmidecode -s bios-version` on the actual board answers it.

### [MAJOR] Display/GPU clock controllers are modules for us and built-in for the proven system, with a documented SMMU probe-order root cause  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** `meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg` states the root cause verbatim: "Root cause of dead HDMI + adreno -19: gpucc (SC_GPUCC_7280) was =m, so the adreno SMMU's clocks/power-domain were missing at its early probe -> -110, and every later adreno bind failed." It sets `CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y`, `CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y`, `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y`, `CONFIG_DRM=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y`, `CONFIG_QCOM_LLCC=y`, `CONFIG_QCOM_OCMEM=y`. Git shas 89c038c -> 9c9498f -> c1ecf57 are the iteration that landed it on real hardware.

**Ours today:** `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment` lines 219-223 are a contiguous =m block: `CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m` (219), `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m` (220), `CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m` (221), `CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=m` (222), `CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=m` (223) — five symbols, not three. Additionally absent anywhere in the repo: `CONFIG_DRM=y`, `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM`, `CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP`, `CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER`, `CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER`, `CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER`, `CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE`. We do already match on `CONFIG_PHY_QCOM_QMP=y` (:104, not :113) and `CONFIG_QCOM_LLCC=y`/`CONFIG_QCOM_OCMEM=y` (:31-32, not :33-34). Built-in SMMU premise verified: `CONFIG_ARM_SMMU=y`/`CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y` at :44-45. `nerves_defconfig:94` documents the =m assumption in prose and must be updated with the fragment.

**Why it matters:** This is nominally the display agent's territory, but the mechanism is platform-level: `apps_smmu` (`CONFIG_ARM_SMMU_QCOM=y`, built-in) probes at initcall time and needs gpucc-provided clocks/power-domains for the GPU context banks. If gpucc is a module that cannot exist yet, the SMMU's early probe returns -110 and every subsequent adreno bind fails with -19 — a failure that is permanent for the boot, not retried. It also compounds finding #3 (built-in drivers vs late-loaded modules): here the fix runs the *opposite* direction from the remoteproc fix, which is why both need to be reasoned about together rather than applying one blanket rule.

**Action:** In `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment`, delete the whole contiguous block at lines 219-223 (all five =m symbols, including `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m` and `CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR=m` which the original action left in place) and replace it in situ with: CONFIG_SC_GPUCC_7280=y CONFIG_SC_DISPCC_7280=y CONFIG_DRM=y CONFIG_DRM_KMS_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLEDRM=y CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_AUX_BRIDGE=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM=y CONFIG_DRM_MSM_DP=y Leaving no duplicate of any of these later in the file — Buildroot merges via `support/kconfig/merge_config.sh -m` where the LAST duplicate wins, so a surviving `CONFIG_DRM_SIMPLE_BRIDGE=m` at old line 220 silently defeats the fix. Decide explicitly on `CONFIG_DRM_DISPLAY_CONNECTOR`: the Yocto cfg does not pin it (it inherits radxa's standalone defconfig), but the DTB's `/hdmi-connector` node terminates the bridge chain. Leaving it =m while DRM_MSM is built in reproduces the incomplete-chain failure of 89c038c; set it =y unless the radxa defconfig is confirmed to leave it =m. Update the now-stale comment at `nerves_defconfig:94` ("CONFIG_DRM_MSM=m + a660 firmware"). `grub.cfg`: `clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused` should indeed go, but cite 9c9498f ("Debug params (module_blacklist, clk/pd_ignore_unused) removed"), not c84ddad — c84ddad removed `module_blacklist=msm,phy_qcom_qmp_combo`, which we never had. Grep confirms neither param survives anywhere in the Yocto tree. Remove them in the same change as the built-in clock controllers, not before. Drop the `CONFIG_FW_LOADER_USER_HELPER=y` recommendation to an open question: the proven system runs DRM_MSM=y against the same a660 zap/SQE/GMU firmware on a rootfs mounted after initcalls and works, so deferred-probe retry appears sufficient. Do not pre-emptively enable the user-helper path. Unverified locally: our kernel is a different tree (Deka-Embedded-Linux/linux-dragon-q6a @ e05c4fa, `BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y`) than Yocto's (radxa/kernel linux-6.18.2 @ 559f4f9). Confirm against the extracted source that `DRM_KMS_HELPER`/`DRM_DISPLAY_HELPER`/`DRM_GEM_SHMEM_HELPER`/`DRM_AUX_BRIDGE` are still promptless select-only symbols there (in which case those four lines are harmless no-ops that olddefconfig resolves via DRM_MSM's selects, exactly as on the Yocto side) and diff the post-olddefconfig `.config` against the intended values rather than trusting the fragment.

**Uncertainty:** Overlaps the display/GPU agent's domain — coordinate so the firmware-timing consequence of `DRM_MSM=y` is not lost. I also did not confirm which DRM helper symbols the pinned 6.18 tree still requires explicitly vs auto-selects.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-display.cfg:1-20 · radxa-q6a-yocto git shas 89c038c, 9c9498f, c1ecf57, c84ddad · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:219-223 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:33-34 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg</sub>


### [minor] CONFIG_QCOMTEE is the entire content of q6a-platform.cfg and we do not have it  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-kernel/linux/configs/q6a-platform.cfg` is exactly three lines: ``` # QTEE (Qualcomm TEE): creates /dev/tee0 so qteesupplicant and the TEE # trusted apps can run. Without it the tee0 device wait always times out. CONFIG_QCOMTEE=y ``` It is merged via `SRC_URI += "file://configs/q6a-display.cfg file://configs/q6a-platform.cfg"` in `linux-qcom_%.bbappend`. Commits 7b95684 and c7fd105 added it; commits dcbf15b / 0f32caa then had to *disable* `qteesupplicant` and mask `tpm2.target` because those 90 s device waits tripled boot time and broke the OTA validate window. The board DTS backs the TEE with `qtee_mem@c1300000` (5 MB) and `trusted_apps_mem@c1800000` (28 MB) reserved regions.

**Ours today:** absent from `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment`. The pinned tree's `arch/arm64/configs/defconfig` gives us `CONFIG_TEE=y` and `CONFIG_OPTEE=y` (lines 1707-1708) but not `CONFIG_QCOMTEE`, and we have no `qteesupplicant` equivalent to want `/dev/tee0`.

**Why it matters:** Nothing in our image consumes `/dev/tee0` today, so this is not a boot blocker — but the DTS reserves 33 MB for QTEE regardless, and any future secure-storage / attestation / DRM work needs the symbol. Recording it now avoids re-deriving it later. Note the Yocto team's second lesson is the more actionable one for us: they had to actively *suppress* TEE/TPM device waits to protect the A/B validate window, and our `qcom-coldplug.sh` -> `fwup autovalidate` path has the same timing sensitivity.

**Action:** Optional, and only if TEE is wanted: append to `/home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment` ``` # --- Qualcomm TEE (/dev/tee0). Backed by the DTS qtee_mem/trusted_apps_mem # --- carveouts. Nothing in the Nerves image uses it yet; enable when a TA # --- or secure-storage requirement lands. CONFIG_QCOMTEE=y ``` Do NOT add anything that waits on `/dev/tee0` in `qcom-coldplug.sh` — the Yocto side's own git history (dcbf15b) is that such waits cost minutes of boot time and broke OTA validation.

### [minor] We ship ~16 MB of generic qcm6490 adsp/cdsp images that the proven system does not install  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The Q6A packagegroup pulls ONLY the board-specific `linux-firmware-qcom-qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a-{audio,compute}` packages. The generic `linux-firmware-qcom-qcm6490-{audio,compute}` packages (whose FILES globs are `${firmwaredir}/qcom/qc[ms]6490/adsp*.*` and `.../cdsp*.*`) are NOT referenced anywhere for this machine, so `qcom/qcm6490/adsp.mbn`, `qcom/qcm6490/cdsp.mbn` and the `qcom/qcs6490/{adsp,cdsp}.mbn` symlinks never land on the Yocto rootfs. Likewise `linux-firmware-qcom-qcm6490-{ipa,wifi}` (`ipa_fws.mbn`, `wpss.mbn`) are absent — consistent with the DTS deleting `&ipa_fw_mem`, `&wpss_mem`, `&remoteproc_wpss`.

**Ours today:** `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware/qcom/qcm6490/adsp.mbn` (12 099 132 bytes) and `cdsp.mbn` (3 641 344 bytes) are real files, plus symlinks `qcom/qcs6490/adsp.mbn -> ../qcm6490/adsp.mbn` and `qcom/qcs6490/cdsp.mbn -> ../qcm6490/cdsp.mbn`. `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md` calls them "generic Kodiak fallbacks". Nothing references them: the DTS `firmware-name` properties point at `qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/{adsp,cdsp}.mbn`.

**Why it matters:** ~15.7 MB of never-loaded blobs inside the squashfs, i.e. ~31 MB across both A/B slots and in every OTA payload. Worse, the generic images are different versions (`ADSP.HT.5.5.c8-00249.1-KODIAK-1` / `CDSP.HT.2.5.c3-00134-KODIAK-1`) from the board pair the frozen tuple depends on (`ADSP.HT.5.5.c9-00028-KODIAK-2` / `CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1`), so if anything ever falls back to them the CDSP-shell version match breaks and `FASTRPC_IOCTL_INIT_CREATE` returns 0x80000600 — a confusing failure mode our own README warns about.

**Action:** Delete the unused generics and their symlinks, then regenerate SHA256SUMS: ```sh cd /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware rm -f qcom/qcm6490/adsp.mbn qcom/qcm6490/cdsp.mbn \ qcom/qcs6490/adsp.mbn qcom/qcs6490/cdsp.mbn cd .. && (cd lib/firmware && find . \( -type f -o -type l \) -exec sha256sum {} \;) > SHA256SUMS ``` Keep `qcom/qcm6490/a660_zap.mbn` (real) and `qcom/qcs6490/a660_zap.mbn` (symlink) — the DTS `&gpu_zap_shader { firmware-name = "qcom/qcs6490/a660_zap.mbn"; }` needs exactly that pair — and keep `qcom/qcm6490/qupv3fw.elf` (the DTS asks for that literal path). Update the layout table in `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md`.

**Uncertainty:** If Phase-0 bench work ever wants to A/B-test the generic vs board DSP images, keep them out of the shipped tree and fetch them ad hoc instead.

### [minor] qcom/qcs6490/qupv3fw.elf symlink missing (upstream/Yocto ship it; DTS uses the qcm6490 path)  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `linux-firmware-qcom-qcm6490-qupv3fw` FILES is `${firmwaredir}/qcom/qc[ms]6490/qupv3fw.elf*`, so both `qcom/qcm6490/qupv3fw.elf` (real, WHENCE line 10298) and `qcom/qcs6490/qupv3fw.elf` (symlink `-> ../qcm6490/qupv3fw.elf`, WHENCE 10300) land on the rootfs.

**Ours today:** only `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware/qcom/qcm6490/qupv3fw.elf` (57 269 bytes, sha256 9fc1f136...). No `qcom/qcs6490/qupv3fw.elf` symlink.

**Why it matters:** Harmless today: the board DTS asks for the qcm6490 path literally — `&qupv3_id_0 { firmware-name = "qcom/qcm6490/qupv3fw.elf"; }` and the same for `&qupv3_id_1`. It only bites if the DTB ever changes to the qcs6490 spelling (and remember the standing note that on real hardware the DTB may come from SPI-NOR EDK2, not from our `grub.cfg devicetree` line — in which case we do not control which spelling is requested). QUP firmware backs every GENI SE instance, including the `ttyMSM0` debug console, so a miss here is expensive to diagnose.

**Action:** One-line insurance, mirroring upstream: ```sh cd /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/lib/firmware ln -sfn ../qcm6490/qupv3fw.elf qcom/qcs6490/qupv3fw.elf ``` then regenerate `SHA256SUMS`.

**Uncertainty:** Whether mainline 6.18's geni-se actually consumes `firmware-name` (vs relying on XBL/UEFI having already loaded QUP FW) — I could not read the driver. Either way the file is 57 KB and shipping the symlink costs nothing.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/oe-core/meta/recipes-kernel/linux-firmware/linux-firmware_20260622.bb:2535 · /tmp/claude-1000/-home-fermuch-Documents-Dev-AYVU-nerves-system-dragon-q6a/b5e1265e-3d92-4e4c-9105-598d18a6100e/scratchpad/WHENCE:10298-10300 · /tmp/claude-1000/-home-fermuch-Documents-Dev-AYVU-nerves-system-dragon-q6a/b5e1265e-3d92-4e4c-9105-598d18a6100e/scratchpad/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dts:904-912</sub>


### [minor] linux-firmware license/notice files are not installed into /lib/firmware on target  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** Every Q6A firmware package `RDEPENDS` on `linux-firmware-qcom-license` (`FILES = ${firmwaredir}/LICENSE.qcom ${firmwaredir}/qcom/NOTICE.txt`) and the audio package additionally on `linux-firmware-linaro-license` (`FILES = ${firmwaredir}/LICENCE.linaro`), so `/lib/firmware/LICENSE.qcom`, `/lib/firmware/qcom/NOTICE.txt` and `/lib/firmware/LICENCE.linaro` are on the shipped rootfs.

**Ours today:** `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/LICENSE.qcom` and `NOTICE.qcom` exist in the source tree and are declared as `QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE_LICENSE_FILES` in `package/qcom-dsp-firmware/qcom-dsp-firmware.mk:23`, but they sit outside `lib/` and `QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE_INSTALL_TARGET_CMDS` only does `cp -a $(@D)/lib $(TARGET_DIR)/` — so nothing lands on the target rootfs. Also `LICENCE.linaro` (the ADSP audio blob's second licence) is not vendored at all.

**Why it matters:** Redistribution hygiene for a product image: these blobs are "Redistributable, no modification" and the licence text is meant to travel with them. Buildroot's `legal-info` will pick up `LICENSE_FILES`, which covers the manifest, but the on-device copy that the proven system ships is absent.

**Action:** Vendor `LICENCE.linaro` next to the others and install all three, e.g. append to `package/qcom-dsp-firmware/qcom-dsp-firmware.mk`: ```make define QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE_INSTALL_LICENSES $(INSTALL) -D -m 0644 $(@D)/LICENSE.qcom $(TARGET_DIR)/lib/firmware/LICENSE.qcom $(INSTALL) -D -m 0644 $(@D)/NOTICE.qcom $(TARGET_DIR)/lib/firmware/qcom/NOTICE.txt $(INSTALL) -D -m 0644 $(@D)/LICENCE.linaro $(TARGET_DIR)/lib/firmware/LICENCE.linaro endef QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE_POST_INSTALL_TARGET_HOOKS += QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE_INSTALL_LICENSES ``` (add `LICENCE.linaro` to `QCOM_DSP_FIRMWARE_LICENSE_FILES` too).

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/oe-core/meta/recipes-kernel/linux-firmware/linux-firmware_20260622.bb:2332 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/oe-core/meta/recipes-kernel/linux-firmware/linux-firmware_20260622.bb:2465 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/oe-core/meta/recipes-kernel/linux-firmware/linux-firmware_20260622.bb:2722-2723 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/package/qcom-dsp-firmware/qcom-dsp-firmware.mk:22-32</sub>


### [info] packagegroup-qcom-boot-essential ships qrtr/rmtfs/tqftpserv as systemd services; we have none — and on this board most of it is genuinely unnecessary  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `conf/machine/include/qcom-qcs6490.inc:9-13` and `radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:67` both put `packagegroup-qcom-boot-essential` in `MACHINE_ESSENTIAL_EXTRA_RRECOMMENDS`. `packagegroup-qcom.bb:13-17` defines it as exactly `qrtr rmtfs tqftpserv`. `rmtfs_1.3.bb` installs `rmtfs.service` + `rmtfs-dir.service` (SYSTEMD_SERVICE), `tqftpserv_1.2.bb` installs `tqftpserv.service`, both `RDEPENDS += qrtr`. `pd-mapper_1.1.bb` exists in meta-qcom but is NOT in the packagegroup — the kernel module `kernel-module-qcom-pd-mapper` is used instead.

**Ours today:** absent — none of `qrtr`, `rmtfs`, `tqftpserv` exists in Buildroot, and we have no init system to run them (`BR2_INIT_NONE=y`). We do have the in-kernel replacements: `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:77` `CONFIG_QCOM_PD_MAPPER=y`, `:210-212` `CONFIG_QRTR=y` + `CONFIG_QRTR_SMD=y`.

**Why it matters:** Two of the three are dead code on this board and the third is superseded in-kernel, so the apparent gap is not a real one — worth writing down so nobody ports systemd units for it. The Q6A DTS `/delete-node/`s `&remoteproc_mpss`, `&remoteproc_wpss`, `&rmtfs_mem`, `&mpss_mem`, `&wpss_mem`, `&wifi` — so there is no modem and no WCN6750, which is what `rmtfs` (modem remote filesystem) and `tqftpserv` (TFTP-over-QRTR for modem file access) exist to serve. `qrtr`'s daemon is `qrtr-ns`, and the QRTR name service has been in-kernel since 5.7, which `CONFIG_QRTR=y` gives us. The userspace `pd-mapper` is replaced by `CONFIG_QCOM_PD_MAPPER=y`, exactly as meta-qcom itself chose.

**Action:** No action. Record in `GOAL.md` / `README.md` that `packagegroup-qcom-boot-essential` (qrtr/rmtfs/tqftpserv) has been deliberately skipped, with the reasons above, so it is not re-raised as a gap. If a future variant re-enables the modem or WCN6750, `rmtfs` + `tqftpserv` would then need new `package/` entries and launching from `rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh` (there is no systemd analogue).

**Uncertainty:** That the QRTR name service is in-kernel (making `qrtr-ns` redundant) is from my knowledge of net/qrtr, not from reading the pinned tree. Cheap to confirm on-target: `ls /sys/module/qrtr` and check that `qmi` clients (e.g. pd-mapper's service registration) work without any userspace daemon.

### [info] camxfirmware-kodiak (CAMERA_ICP_170.elf) and linux-firmware-lt9611uxc are in the Yocto packagegroup but are dead weight for this board's mainline DTS  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a-firmware` RRECOMMENDS `camxfirmware-kodiak` and `linux-firmware-lt9611uxc`. `camxfirmware-kodiak_1.0.7.bb` installs exactly one blob, `/lib/firmware/qcom/qcm6490/CAMERA_ICP_170.elf` (from a Qualcomm artifactory tarball, prebuilt build 260209.1), plus `/usr/share/doc/camxfirmware-kodiak/LICENSE.QCOM-2.txt`. `linux-firmware-lt9611uxc` installs `/lib/firmware/lt9611uxc_fw.bin` and `/lib/firmware/Lontium/lt9611uxc_fw.bin`. The Yocto side also pulls `kernel-module-lontium-lt9611uxc` via `packagegroup-machine-essential-*-board-generic` and `kernel-module-qcom-camss` via `-qcom-generic`.

**Ours today:** absent (both). We enable no CAMSS at all in the fragment (only `CONFIG_MEDIA_USB_SUPPORT=y` / `CONFIG_USB_VIDEO_CLASS=m` for UVC webcams) and no Lontium bridge driver.

**Why it matters:** Neither is needed and both would be pure image bloat, but the reason is worth recording so nobody "fixes the gap" later: (a) the HDMI bridge on the Dragon Q6A is `compatible = "radxa,ra620"` — a simple-bridge/DP path — not an LT9611UXC; both the mainline-derived DTS and the Radxa 6.18 DTS agree (`hdmi-bridge { compatible = "radxa,ra620"; }`), so `lt9611uxc_fw.bin` can never be requested. It is almost certainly copy-paste from the RB3Gen2 packagegroup. (b) `CAMERA_ICP_170.elf` belongs to the downstream CAMX/ICP camera stack; mainline `sc7280.dtsi` has `cci0`/`cci1` I2C controllers but no CAMSS/ICP node, and neither board DTS instantiates a sensor, so nothing requests it.

**Action:** No action — do not add either firmware. Add a short "deliberately not shipped" note to `blobs/qcom-dsp-firmware/README.md` listing `lt9611uxc_fw.bin` (wrong bridge: board uses radxa,ra620) and `qcom/qcm6490/CAMERA_ICP_170.elf` (downstream CAMX/ICP only; no CAMSS node in mainline sc7280), so the delta against the Yocto packagegroup is explained rather than looking like an oversight.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom-3rdparty/recipes-bsp/packagegroups/packagegroup-radxa-dragon-q6a.bb:11-12 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom/recipes-bsp/firmware/camxfirmware-kodiak_1.0.7.bb · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/oe-core/meta/recipes-kernel/linux-firmware/linux-firmware_20260622.bb:1333 · /tmp/claude-1000/-home-fermuch-Documents-Dev-AYVU-nerves-system-dragon-q6a/b5e1265e-3d92-4e4c-9105-598d18a6100e/scratchpad/qcs6490-radxa-dragon-q6a.dts:77-95 · /tmp/claude-1000/-home-fermuch-Documents-Dev-AYVU-nerves-system-dragon-q6a/b5e1265e-3d92-4e4c-9105-598d18a6100e/scratchpad/dts-radxa.dts:139-140 · /tmp/claude-1000/-hom…</sub>


### [info] The kas base adds qcom_scm.download_mode=1 to the kernel cmdline; our grub.cfg does not  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `meta-qcom/ci/base.yml` `local_conf_header` contains `cmdline: KERNEL_CMDLINE_EXTRA:append = " qcom_scm.download_mode=1"`, so every Qualcomm image built through this chain (including `q6a-nerveshub-image`) boots with SCM download mode armed.

**Ours today:** `grub.cfg` sets `nerves_cmdline="console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 clk_ignore_unused pd_ignore_unused loglevel=4 rootfstype=squashfs rootwait ro"` — no `qcom_scm.download_mode`. We do have `CONFIG_QCOM_SCM=y` (`linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:17`).

**Why it matters:** With `download_mode=1` a kernel panic or watchdog bite leaves the SoC in EDL/crashdump state instead of silently rebooting, which is how you extract a RAM dump from a board that dies before the console is up. That is exactly the failure class our QEMU-only validation cannot cover, so it is valuable for first-boot bring-up. It is also actively hostile in the field: it converts a recoverable crash into a hang requiring host intervention, and it interacts badly with the A/B `booted_once` semantics in `grub.cfg` (a slot that panics in download mode never gets to flip back).

**Action:** Do not add it to the shipped `grub.cfg`. Instead document in `BRINGUP.md` that for first bring-up you edit `\EFI\BOOT\grub.cfg` on the ESP to append ` qcom_scm.download_mode=1` to `nerves_cmdline`, and remove it before any A/B rollback testing (it defeats the auto-fallback path). If a permanent debug variant is wanted, that should be a separate `grub.cfg` selected at burn time, not the production default.

**Uncertainty:** I did not verify the `download_mode` module parameter's exact type/permission in the pinned 6.18 tree; the cmdline spelling above is what the proven system uses verbatim.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom/ci/base.yml (local_conf_header: cmdline) · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:17</sub>


### [info] meta-audioreach is a configured layer but contributes nothing to the shipped Yocto image  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `meta-audioreach` is fetched and enabled by `meta-qcom/ci/qcom-distro.yml` (repos block) and listed in `meta-qcom-distro/conf/layer.conf:27`. The only consumer is `meta-qcom-distro/recipes-products/images/qcom-multimedia-proprietary-image.bb:26`, which conditionally adds `packagegroup-audioreach`. `q6a-nerveshub-image.bb` does `require recipes-products/images/qcom-console-image.bb` (-> `qcom-minimal-image.bb`), not the proprietary multimedia image, so `packagegroup-audioreach` is never installed.

**Ours today:** n/a — we have no audioreach userspace either. Audio goes through the mainline in-kernel `q6apm`/audioreach path: `linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:226-236` (`CONFIG_QCOM_APR=m`, `CONFIG_SOUNDWIRE_QCOM=m`, `CONFIG_SND_SOC_QCOM=m`, `CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC8280XP=m`, LPASS macros, WCD938X) plus ALSA userspace in `nerves_defconfig`.

**Why it matters:** Removes a false lead: the presence of `meta-audioreach` in the reference tree could look like the proven system runs Qualcomm's proprietary AudioReach SPF/AGM userspace, which would be a large porting job with no Buildroot analogue. It does not. Both systems use the same mainline kernel path, and the only userspace artifact that matters is the topology binary — which is finding #1. Our `CONFIG_SND_SOC_SC8280XP=m` is also the right machine driver: the DTS compatible is `qcom,qcs6490-rb3gen2-sndcard`, which that driver matches, and it `select`s `SND_SOC_QDSP6`.

**Action:** No action beyond fixing finding #1. Record in `README.md` that AudioReach userspace (SPF/AGM/ACDB) is not used by the proven system either, so the audio contract is: mainline `q6apm` + WCD9380 + `QCS6490-Radxa-Dragon-Q6A-tplg.bin` + ALSA. One follow-up worth noting: our `nerves_defconfig` comment already flags there is no `alsa-ucm-conf` package in Buildroot, so mixer routing for the WCD938x will need manual `amixer`/`alsaucm` setup once the card enumerates.

**Uncertainty:** That `SND_SOC_SC8280XP` carries the `qcom,qcs6490-rb3gen2-sndcard` compatible is from my knowledge of sound/soc/qcom, not from reading the pinned tree. Cheap on-target check: `dmesg | grep -i sndcard` and `aplay -l`.
