### [info] Hexagon DSP-side binaries use a different on-disk layout than the proven system (/usr/lib/dsp flat vs /usr/share/qcom/... + conf.d yaml)  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** `hexagon-dsp-binaries_20260622.bb` (SRCREV 2ba83638b373c0a6bbb7ecb32f5e2b9dfca2c4ce, same commit we pin) installs `FILES:${PN}-radxa-dragon-q6a-adsp = ${datadir}/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/dsp/adsp` and `...-cdsp = ${datadir}/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/dsp/cdsp`, and a `python()` hook force-adds an RDEPENDS on the dynamically-split `hexagon-dsp-binaries-radxa-dragon-q6a-config` package (`${datadir}/qcom/conf.d/hexagon-dsp-binaries-radxa-dragon-q6a.yaml`) plus `-config-schema` (`${datadir}/qcom/conf.d/schema.json`). `fastrpc_1.0.6.bb` (tag v1.0.6, SRCREV 5f0b6a33...) DEPENDS on `libyaml` and creates `${datadir}/qcom/`; the upstream `configure.ac` defaults `config_base_dir=/usr/share/qcom/`, and `inc/fastrpc_config_parser.h` defines `DSP_LIB_KEY "DSP_LIBRARY_PATH"` — i.e. the yaml supplies DSP_LIBRARY_PATH per board. The machine conf also adds `qairt-sdk-hexagon-v68`, which drops the QNN HTP v68 skels at `${datadir}/qcom/qcm6490/Thundercomm/RB3gen2/dsp/cdsp`.

**Ours today:** `package/qcom-dsp-shell/qcom-dsp-shell.mk` pins the same SRCREV and copies `qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/CDSP.HT.2.5.c4-00004-KODIAK-1/.` and `.../ADSP.HT.5.5.c9-00028-KODIAK-2/.` flat into `/usr/lib/dsp`, and we set the env override in two places: `rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config` (`-e DSP_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/lib/dsp;ADSP_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/lib/dsp`) and `rootfs_overlay/etc/profile.d/qcom-npu-env.sh`. `package/qcom-fastrpc/qcom-fastrpc.mk` pins `qualcomm/fastrpc` @ 706071caca54b9a56d78793c30d04351de5fbd96 on branch `development` (not the v1.0.6 tag Yocto uses) and depends on `libyaml`.

**Why it matters:** Not a defect — the env-var override is a first-class path in the fastrpc source (`DSP_LIBRARY_PATH` takes precedence over the yaml), and both systems pin the same dsp-binaries commit and the same version-matched CDSP tuple. It is worth recording so the layout difference is not later mistaken for a bug, and so the two remaining deltas are visible: (a) our fastrpc is a `development`-branch commit rather than the released v1.0.6 the proven system uses, and (b) our QAIRT blobs are SDK 2.42.0.251225 vs the proven 2.47.0.260601.

**Action:** No blocking action; flag to the DSP/NPU agent. Two things worth aligning if that agent agrees: (1) consider re-pinning `QCOM_FASTRPC_VERSION` in `package/qcom-fastrpc/qcom-fastrpc.mk` to the v1.0.6 tag commit `5f0b6a33a19f3f7f0c06709846752a2caf64e413` to match the hardware-proven build; (2) if we ever drop the env override, we would need to install `/usr/share/qcom/conf.d/hexagon-dsp-binaries-radxa-dragon-q6a.yaml` + `schema.json` from the dsp-binaries tree and keep the upstream `${datadir}/qcom/qcs6490/radxa/dragon-q6a/dsp/{adsp,cdsp}` layout instead of flattening.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the `development`-branch fastrpc commit we pin behaves identically to v1.0.6 for `FASTRPC_IOCTL_INIT_CREATE` on unsigned PDs — only a bench run of `fastrpc_test -a v68` settles it.

The Yocto side has a local defconfig-validation recipe for exactly this class of problem; a `test/check-kernel-config.sh` invoked from CI is the Buildroot analogue.

## system integration — init, services, userland packages, build/test harness

The two systems solve the same problem with opposite integration models. Yocto/qcom-distro is a full systemd distro (INIT_MANAGER = "systemd", pam, rw ext4 rootfs, initramfs, NetworkManager, openssh, rsyslog, persistent journald) where every board-specific behaviour is a systemd unit with explicit ordering and timeouts: q6a-esp-fsck (FAT repair before boot.mount), q6a-boot-select converge (slot convergence + reboot, before nerves_hub_agent), q6a-fw-validate (validate only after the NervesHub agent has been active for up to 6 min + 30 s settle), nerves_hub_agent (an Erlang release run as a service). Our Buildroot system is BR2_INIT_NONE with a read-only squashfs root, no initramfs, no service manager: erlinit is PID 1 and the only pre-userspace hook is `--pre-run-exec /usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh`, which currently does A/B bookkeeping, udev coldplug, DSP node permissions and cdsprpcd in one…


### [BLOCKER] Firmware validation runs before the BEAM starts — a crash-looping release is never rolled back  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Confirmed verbatim. q6a-fw-validate.service:2-9 — `After=nerves_hub_agent.service network-online.target`, `Wants=network-online.target`, `ConditionPathExists=/sys/firmware/efi/efivars`, `Type=oneshot`, `ExecStart=/usr/sbin/q6a-boot-select validate`. q6a-boot-select:71-87 — exits unless booted==active and validated==0, polls `systemctl is-active --quiet nerves_hub_agent.service` 72x5s, `|| exit 0 # stay unvalidated -> will revert next boot`, then `sleep 30`, `fw_setenv nerves_fw_validated 1`, `fw_setenv q6a_boot_attempts 0`, `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default "q6a-${booted}.conf"`. Commit dcbf15b confirms the 2->6 min widening and states the root cause (masked tpm2.target/qteesupplicant; sequential 90 s dead-device waits pushed slot-B's first boot past the 2 min window and caused a spurious rollback). One correction to the claim's framing: `systemctl is-active` only proves the agent's unit process is running — not that it connected to NervesHub. The gate is application-layer liveness plus a 30 s settle window, not a check-in confirmation.

**Ours today:** Mechanics confirmed: fwup.conf:190-191 (`task complete`) arms `nerves_fw_autovalidate=1`; upgrade.a:281 / upgrade.b:341 set `nerves_fw_validated=0` and never clear autovalidate; qcom-coldplug.sh:21-25 runs `fwup -q -t reconcile` then `fwup -q -t autovalidate -d /dev/rootdisk0 /usr/share/fwup/ops.fw`; erlinit.config:53 invokes that script via `--pre-run-exec`, i.e. before the BEAM. autovalidate.a/b (fwup-ops.conf:316-339) set nerves_fw_validated=1 and fat_write grubenv_a_valid/grubenv_b_valid. So reaching the pre-run hook is indeed treated as proof the release is good, and with BR2_PACKAGE_NERVES_HEART (Config.in:8, selected by nerves_defconfig:76) plus `-heart -env HEART_BEAT_TIMEOUT 120` (example/rel/vm.args.eex:42) a wedged BEAM watchdog-reboots into an already-validated slot. But the capability is NOT missing, and one cited detail is wrong: - fwup-ops.conf:246-263 already ships `task validate.a` / `task validate.b`, which set `nerves_fw_validated=1` AND fat_write grubenv_a_valid/grubenv_b_valid to `/EFI/BOOT/grubenv`. - `Nerves.Runtime.validate_firmware/0` already triggers exactly…

**Why it matters:** The entire purpose of the GRUB boot-once budget is to catch a bad *release* (missing app, bad NIF, crash-looping supervisor), not just a bad kernel. Because BR2_PACKAGE_NERVES_HEART is auto-selected (deps/nerves_system_br/package/nerves-config/Config.in line 8) and example/rel/vm.args.eex carries `-heart -env HEART_BEAT_TIMEOUT 120`, a wedged BEAM triggers the QCOM_WDT hardware reset — and the board reboots straight back into the slot we already marked validated. That is an unrecoverable field failure requiring physical/EDL access, on the exact failure mode OTA rollback exists to prevent. Worse, test/qemu-rollback.sh Phase 1 asserts `grubenv validated=1` after boot, so the test currently *en…

**Action:** 1) fwup.conf:191 — change `uboot_setenv(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_autovalidate", "1")` to `"0"` in `task complete`, and update the comment block at fwup.conf:185-189 accordingly. This alone disables premature validation: autovalidate.a/b are gated on `require-uboot-variable(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_autovalidate", "1")` and fall through to `autovalidate.skip`. 2) DO NOT delete the `fwup -q -t autovalidate` call from qcom-coldplug.sh:24. It is already a no-op when the env var is 0, and keeping it preserves the documented opt-in for devices with no application-layer health signal (kiosk/offline builds). Update the comment at qcom-coldplug.sh:15-16 to say autovalidate is now off by default. 3) DO NOT add rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/q6a-fw-validate. Unnecessary: `Nerves.Runtime.validate_firmware/0` -> `Nerves.Runtime.FwupOps.validate/1` -> `fwup -t validate /usr/share/fwup/ops.fw -d /dev/rootdisk0`, which hits our existing `task validate.a`/`validate.b` (fwup-ops.conf:246-263) and fat_writes grubenv_*_valid to /EFI/BOOT/grubenv. The app should call `Nerves.Runtime.validate_firmware()` directly. (Verify at runtime that /dev/rootdisk0p1's ESP is writable from the running slot, since validate must fat_write the ESP — the same write autovalidate performs today, so this is already proven by the QEMU test.) 4) Migration hazard to document: changing fwup.conf only affects newly *burned* devices. Any board already in the field has `nerves_fw_autovalidate=1` persisted in its uboot-env, so it keeps auto-validating until something clears it. Either add `uboot_setenv(uboot-env, "nerves_fw_autovalidate", "0")` to the on-resource blocks of `task upgrade.a` and `task upgrade.b` in fwup.conf (next to the existing `nerves_fw_validated=0` at :281/:341), or ship a one-time `fw_setenv` in the overlay. This is the item the original claim missed entirely. 5) example/lib/example/application.ex: add a small supervised task that calls `Nerves.Runtime.validate_firmware()` after a real health signal. It cannot be the claim's "VintageNet route + NervesHubLink connected" gate as written — example/mix.exs has neither dep. Either add {:vintage_net, ...} / {:nerves_hub_link, ...} explicitly, or (better for a system-under-test repo) gate on a local readiness check the example can actually satisfy and document the production pattern in README.md. 6) README.md:76-79 — rewrite item 4 of "A/B updates with automatic rollback": autovalidate is now off by default; validation requires the app to call `Nerves.Runtime.validate_firmware/0`, which writes both the KV block and /EFI/BOOT/grubenv via ops.fw's validat…

**Uncertainty:** I did not read nerves_runtime's source to confirm validate_firmware/0 never shells out to fwup ops.fw; on stock Nerves systems it writes Nerves.Runtime.KV only. Verify against the nerves_runtime version the app pins before relying on the wording.

### [BLOCKER] SPI/UEFI boot firmware >= 6.0.260120 is a hard NPU prerequisite that our repo never mentions and cannot check on-target  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Accurate as claimed; add the two strongest pieces the claim omitted. radxa-q6a-yocto/README.md:82-88: "**Board prerequisite (not fixable in the image):** the SPI boot firmware must be >= `6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1` - older firmware makes the DSP reject unsigned protection domains (`0x80000600`) and QNN is unusable. Check on the board with `dmidecode -s bios-version`." Commit ad69735 ("Ship edl-ng + SPI boot firmware 260120 for offline on-device flash") states the root cause and the motive: "Boards with SPI boot firmware older than 6.0.260120 cannot use the NPU (DSP rejects unsigned protection domains). Bake the tooling into the image so field units can be upgraded without network access". Note the framing: Yocto bundles the payload for *offline field upgrade convenience*, and explicitly says the prerequisite is not fixable in the image — it is not an image-configuration requirement.

**Ours today:** Partially present, not absent. BRINGUP.md:31-32 already states "Armbian notes the **latest UEFI firmware is required**"; BRINGUP.md:33-34 already asks the operator to record the observed `QC_IMAGE_VERSION_STRING=BOOT...KODIAK...` from the boot log into GOAL.md; BRINGUP.md:106 planned row reads "UEFI firmware | latest Radxa snapshot"; and BRINGUP.md §1 already runs the full `edl-ng --memory spinor --loader prog_firehose_ddr.elf rawprogram rawprogram0.xml patch0.xml` host-side flash of Radxa's snapshot *before* the §2 NPU test. GOAL.md:103 row is indeed blank ("record from bench boot log (Phase 0)"). What is genuinely missing: (1) the exact minimum version string `6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1` and the `0x80000600` symptom anywhere in our repo (grep over the whole tree excluding deps/ and .git/ finds only BRINGUP.md:22-28's host-side edl-ng v1.4.1 note); (2) any on-target way to read the running version — no BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE in nerves_defconfig and no CONFIG_DMI/CONFIG_DMIID in linux-dragon-q6a.fragment (only CONFIG_EFI=y / CONFIG_EFI_STUB=y at 118-119); (3) no bundle…

**Why it matters:** This repo's headline feature is the NPU. On a board with stock/older SPI firmware the CDSP refuses unsigned protection domains and FASTRPC_IOCTL_INIT_CREATE fails — and BRINGUP.md §2 will send the operator chasing the cdsp.mbn / fastrpc_shell version tuple (which is already correct) instead of the actual cause. The Yocto side learned this the expensive way and encoded it; we would repeat it on the bench.

**Action:** Docs-first, and note the fix is host-side, not a build-system change (Yocto's own README:82: "Board prerequisite (not fixable in the image)"). 1. Docs (the real gap): put the exact string in BRINGUP.md §1 — replace the vague "latest UEFI firmware is required" with ">= 6.0.260120.BOOT.MXF.1.0.1-00549-KODIAKWP-1; older firmware makes the CDSP reject unsigned protection domains (error 0x80000600) and FASTRPC_IOCTL_INIT_CREATE / QNN fail". Add the same to README.md's NPU section, fill GOAL.md:103 with that string, change BRINGUP.md:106's Planned cell from "latest Radxa snapshot" to the version, and insert it as step 0 of BRINGUP.md §4 plus a "if fastrpc_test fails, check the SPI firmware version BEFORE re-pinning cdsp.mbn" pointer in §2. 2. On-target version read with zero rootfs cost (preferred over dmidecode): add to linux-dragon-q6a.fragment near the existing EFI block (lines 118-119) CONFIG_DMI=y CONFIG_DMIID=y then read `/sys/class/dmi/id/bios_version` with busybox cat from a rootfs_overlay script (erlinit pre-run) or Elixir. Both symbols are `default y` on arm64 when EFI is on, but assert them since our fragment is merge_config'd onto a base defconfig. 3. Only if you want the exact Yocto CLI: `BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE=y` in nerves_defconfig — symbol verified real at deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/dmidecode/Config.in:1-3 (`depends on BR2_aarch64 || BR2_i386 || BR2_x86_64`), sourced at package/Config.in:493, dmidecode 3.7 generic-package. It adds nothing that step 2 does not already give for the `-s bios-version` case. 4. Do NOT port q6a-spi-firmware/edl-ng on-target. The zip plus the self-contained .NET edl-ng binary (edl-ng + libSystem.IO.Ports.Native.so) would eat a large fraction of the 2 GiB squashfs slot (fwup.conf:65 ROOTFS_A_PART_COUNT=4194304, both slots), against a one-time bench operation already covered by BRINGUP.md §1's host-side EDL flow. /dev/mtd0 does exist on-target (linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:189-194: CONFIG_SPI_QCOM_QSPI=y, CONFIG_MTD=y, CONFIG_MTD_SPI_NOR=y, CONFIG_MTD_BLOCK=y), so it stays a future option if field units ever need offline upgrade — revisit only then.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the >=6.0.260120 requirement gates FastRPC in general or only the QNN/HTP path was not verifiable from the repos — the Yocto DESCRIPTION says "unsigned DSP protection domains (QNN)". Also unverified: whether edl-ng's self-contained .NET binary runs against our glibc/toolchain without ICU.

### [MAJOR] No remote access at all: openssh + baked GitHub authorized_keys + root login have no counterpart, and the Nerves equivalent lives in the app  *(CONFIRMED)*

**Proven system:** qcom-console-image.bb adds `IMAGE_FEATURES += "... ssh-server-openssh"`; qcom-minimal-image.bb adds `allow-root-login`. The image recipe installs `openssh` and `github-ssh-keys` (IMAGE_INSTALL:append, lines 64-72) and sets a dev root password: `PASSWD = "$5$nhf/qZLHxDT2OBqH$XzIw..."`, `EXTRA_USERS_PARAMS = "usermod -p '${PASSWD}' root;"`. recipes-connectivity/github-ssh-keys/github-ssh-keys.bb fetches https://github.com/fermuch.keys at *build* time with `BB_STRICT_CHECKSUM = "0"` and `do_fetch[nostamp] = "1"` (keys change upstream without a version bump) and installs it 0600 as /root/.ssh/authorized_keys. The image also lands a text getty on HDMI for bring-up (`ln -sf /usr/lib/systemd/system/getty@.service .../getty.target.wants/getty@tty1.service`), with weston autostart disabled via the kas override `SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE:pn-weston-init = "disable"`.

**Ours today:** No sshd of any kind: nerves_defconfig selects no openssh, and with BR2_INIT_NONE nothing would start one. The only access is the IEx console on the serial UART (rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config line 12: `-c ttyMSM0`, `-s "/usr/bin/nbtty"`). example/mix.exs deps are nerves, shoehorn, ring_logger, toolshed, nerves_runtime — no nerves_ssh. There is no getty and no second console.

**Why it matters:** Without SSH there is no way to get a shell, pull logs, or push firmware (`mix upload`) on a deployed board — every intervention needs the 3.3 V serial header. On the bench this is fine; in the field it is not. Also note the Yocto side's build-time GitHub key fetch is deliberately non-reproducible, which is exactly the kind of thing that must NOT be replicated in a Buildroot package (post-build.sh curl would break offline/reproducible builds; BR2_REPRODUCIBLE=y is set in nerves_defconfig line 18).

**Action:** Unchanged in substance; tighten to (1) note the system side is already done — nerves_defconfig:76 BR2_PACKAGE_NERVES_CONFIG=y selects BR2_PACKAGE_OPENSSL (nerves_system_br/package/nerves-config/Config.in:5), which makes erlang build --with-ssl (buildroot-2026.05/package/erlang/erlang.mk:124), so :crypto/:ssh are available and NO defconfig change is needed (do not add BR2_PACKAGE_OPENSSH, do not add BR2_PACKAGE_OPENSSL); (2) override BOTH dirs, because nerves_ssh 1.3.0 defaults to /data/nerves_ssh and /data/nerves_ssh/default_user (options.ex:64-65) and we have no /data — the writable app partition mounts at /root (erlinit.config:41 `-m /dev/rootdisk0p4:/root:ext4:nodev:`). Without the override, options.ex:285-296 safe_dir falls back to a tmp dir and host keys are regenerated every boot and differ between slot A and B (Yocto avoids this with the `HostKey /data/ssh/...` lines appended in q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:101-110). So: # example/mix.exs {:nerves_ssh, "~> 1.0"} # 1.3.0 available; pulls ssh_subsystem_fwup for `mix upload` # example/config/target.exs (evaluated on the HOST at firmware-build time) keys = [Path.join(System.user_home!(), ".ssh/id_ed25519.pub"), Path.join(System.user_home!(), ".ssh/id_rsa.pub")] |> Enum.filter(&File.exists?/1) |> Enum.map(&File.read!/1) config :nerves_ssh, authorized_keys: keys, system_dir: "/root/ssh", # host keys, persists across A/B (p4) user_dir: "/root/ssh/user" Drop "/etc/nerves_ssh/authorized_keys" from that list: target.exs runs on the build host, so it would read the developer's /etc, not the target's. If a runtime-supplied key file is wanted, read it in application code (NervesSSH.Options.load_authorized_keys/1 or add_authorized_key/2) from /root, not in config. github-ssh-keys equivalent stays app-side/compile-time as proposed; never a post-build.sh curl (BR2_REPRODUCIBLE=y, nerves_defconfig:18).

### [MAJOR] The validated ONNX/QNN inference stack has no counterpart — and Buildroot 2026.05 ships Python 3.14, so the same cp314 wheels are directly reusable  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** recipes-ml/python3-onnxruntime-qnn/python3-onnxruntime-qnn_1.28.0.bb installs a pinned, validated stack into ${PYTHON_SITEPACKAGES_DIR}: onnxruntime 1.28.0 (cp314 manylinux_2_28 aarch64), onnxruntime_qnn 2.4.0 (cp314 manylinux_2_34 aarch64 — QNN EP with bundled QNN libs and v68 skels), ml_dtypes 0.5.4 (cp314 manylinux_2_28 aarch64), and flatbuffers 25.12.19 (py2.py3-none-any, pure Python — NOT a cp314 wheel). All four have sha256sum pins in SRC_URI and are unpacked with `python3 -m zipfile -e ${DL_DIR}/$w ${D}${PYTHON_SITEPACKAGES_DIR}/`; `inherit python3-dir` supplies the path. RDEPENDS:${PN} = python3-numpy python3-protobuf python3-sympy python3-packaging python3-coloredlogs libstdc++; SKIP_FILE_DEPS:${PN}="1" and INSANE_SKIP:${PN}="arch already-stripped ldflags file-rdeps textrel" for the prebuilt binaries. Two supporting bbappends exist purely so RPM dependency resolution succeeds — both state in their own comments that the runtime is already satisfied: fastrpc_%.bbappend adds `RPROVIDES:${PN} += "libcdsprpc.so()(64bit)"` ("the versioned .so.1 in this package is the same file and…

**Ours today:** nerves_defconfig has no Python at all — `grep -n PYTHON nerves_defconfig` returns nothing. Line 73 enables BR2_PACKAGE_QAIRT_RUNTIME, whose package/qairt-runtime/qairt-runtime.mk copies blobs/qairt-runtime/usr wholesale; that tree contains exactly libQnnHtp.so, libQnnHtpV68Stub.so, libQnnSystem.so, usr/lib/dsp/libQnnHtpV68Skel.so and usr/bin/qnn-platform-validator. package/ holds only aic8800, qairt-runtime, qcom-dsp-firmware, qcom-dsp-shell, qcom-fastrpc. So there is no runtime, no ONNX EP and no way to execute a model on the HTP — the gap is real, and nothing in GOAL.md, README.md, BRINGUP.md or CHANGELOG.md records it as a decision; BRINGUP.md:95 stops at "5. Stretch: `qnn-platform-validator` reports HTP usable (needs `qairt-runtime`)."

**Why it matters:** "NPU-ready userspace" currently means the DSP comes up and fastrpc_test passes; it does not mean you can run a model. Whether that matters depends on the product, but it is the single largest capability gap between the two images and it is not recorded as a decision anywhere. The good news: Buildroot 2026.05's python3 is 3.14.5 (package/python3/python3.mk lines 7-8), so the exact cp314 aarch64 wheels the Yocto recipe pins are ABI-compatible — the port is mechanical.

**Action:** If ONNX inference is in scope: 1. nerves_defconfig: add BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON3=y, BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_NUMPY=y (Buildroot 2026.05 ships 2.4.0 — correct numpy-2.x ABI), BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_PROTOBUF=y, BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_SYMPY=y, BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_PACKAGING=y. Do NOT also set BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_FLATBUFFERS if you unpack the flatbuffers wheel — that double-installs the module into /usr/lib/python3.14/site-packages and pins two versions (Buildroot 25.9.23 vs wheel 25.12.19). Choose one: either BR2_PACKAGE_PYTHON_FLATBUFFERS=y and drop flatbuffers from the wheel list (matches how Yocto handles numpy/protobuf/sympy), or keep the wheel and leave the symbol off. python-coloredlogs does not exist in Buildroot 2026.05; it is optional logging sugar in onnxruntime — drop it. 2. Add package/python-onnxruntime-qnn/{Config.in,python-onnxruntime-qnn.mk} registered in Config.in and external.mk, modeled on package/qairt-runtime. Use a generic-package with explicit *_EXTRA_DOWNLOADS (or per-wheel URL+hash in the .hash file) carrying the same pins as the Yocto recipe, and an INSTALL_TARGET_CMDS that unzips each wheel into $(TARGET_DIR)/usr/lib/python$(PYTHON3_VERSION_MAJOR)/site-packages (verified: pkg-python.mk:67 uses exactly that path). Set *_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE for any Hexagon ELFs the onnxruntime_qnn wheel bundles, mirroring QAIRT_RUNTIME_BIN_ARCH_EXCLUDE = /usr/lib/dsp. 3. PREREQUISITE, not an assumption: onnxruntime_qnn-2.4.0 is a manylinux_2_34 wheel, so the Nerves external toolchain (nerves_defconfig:6, nerves_toolchain_aarch64_nerves_linux_gnu 15.3.0, BR2_TOOLCHAIN_EXTERNAL_CUSTOM_GLIBC) must provide glibc >= 2.34. Verify with `readelf -V`/`strings` on the toolchain's libc before committing to the wheel path; if it is older, the wheel is unusable and only a source build (or Ortex) works. 4. Resolve the QNN library collision explicitly: the onnxruntime_qnn wheel bundles its own libQnnHtp*/libQnnSystem and v68 skels, while BR2_PACKAGE_QAIRT_RUNTIME already installs SDK 2.42.0.251225 copies to /usr/lib and /usr/lib/dsp. Decide which set the loader sees, given DSP_LIBRARY_PATH/ADSP_LIBRARY_PATH=/usr/lib/dsp is baked into rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config and rootfs_overlay/etc/profile.d/qcom-npu-env.sh. Mixing SDK versions between the CPU-side stub and the DSP-side skel is the same class of version-tuple failure GOAL.md already documents for cdsp.mbn vs fastrpc_shell_unsigned_3. 5. Size check: fwup.conf:65 caps each rootfs slot at 2 GiB and fwup.conf:111 has assert-size-lte. python3 + numpy + sympy + protobuf + onnxruntime + the QNN bundle is several hundred MB on top of the …

**Uncertainty:** I did not verify that our package/qcom-fastrpc installs an unversioned libcdsprpc.so symlink, nor that package/qcom-dsp-shell ships libc++.so.1/libc++abi.so.1 — both should be checked before attempting the wheel port.

### [minor] No on-target FAT repair for the ESP, although we write the ESP at runtime more often than Yocto does  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** Verified as stated. recipes-core/q6a-esp-fsck/files/q6a-esp-fsck.service:3-14 has DefaultDependencies=no, After=local-fs-pre.target, Before=local-fs.target boot.mount, ConditionPathExists=/dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP, TimeoutStartSec=60, ExecStart=/bin/sh -c 'umount /boot 2>/dev/null; fsck.vfat -a /dev/disk/by-partlabel/ESP || true'; q6a-esp-fsck.bb:18 RDEPENDS:${PN} = "dosfstools"; enabled via SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE and pulled in at recipes-products/images/q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:49. Commit 3fa9710 says "runs every boot since hard power cuts are the norm". Added context: the `umount /boot` exists because Yocto's own q6a-boot-select mounts the ESP rw at /boot at runtime — that read-write mount, not fwup, is the main dirty-FAT source on that platform.

**Ours today:** nerves_defconfig:59 has only BR2_PACKAGE_HOST_DOSFSTOOLS=y (host-side, for image creation). There is no BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS, so no fsck.fat/fsck.vfat on target, and nerves-common busybox has no fsck applet (deps/nerves_system_br/board/nerves-common/busybox.config:563 `# CONFIG_FSCK is not set`) — verified absent by grep over the whole tree. Correction to the original framing: our runtime ESP exposure is SMALLER than Yocto's, not larger. We never mount the ESP (rootfs_overlay/etc/fstab is empty; erlinit.config:41 mounts only /dev/rootdisk0p4:/root), and every runtime ESP write is a 1024-byte in-place overwrite of a preallocated GRUB env block — post-build.sh:17-33 builds grubenv_{a,b}{,_valid} with `grub-editenv create` (fixed 1 KiB), consumed by grub.cfg:56,60 `save_env` and fwup-ops.conf:253-263,324-338 `fat_write(${BOOT_PART_OFFSET}, "/EFI/BOOT/grubenv")`. Same size, same cluster chain, no FAT metadata churn. By contrast Yocto mounts the ESP read-write from Linux (meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select:43-47 `mount -t vfat -o rw "${disk}p1" /boot`,…

**Why it matters:** A hard power cut in the middle of a fwup fat_write leaves the FAT dirty or the grubenv chain damaged. GRUB's `load_env` then fails or reads garbage, and the recovery path for a bad ESP on this board is EDL — not reflashing an SD card. Yocto treats hard power cuts as the norm on this hardware; nothing in our system defends against them.

**Action:** Keep the three steps as written — they are valid (symbols BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS / BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS_FSCK_FAT exist in deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/dosfstools/Config.in:1,17; dosfstools.mk:36-41 installs /sbin/fsck.fat + fsck.vfat symlink; CONFIG_TIMEOUT is a real busybox symbol, unset in nerves-common) — but treat them as low-priority parity with the reference platform, and verify /dev/rootdisk0p1 exists in the erlinit pre-run window on real hardware (nothing in our tree uses p1 today; only p2/p3/p4 in fwup.conf:33 and fwup-ops.conf reconcile). The `-e` guard makes a wrong device name a silent no-op, so confirm it or the whole thing is dead code. Add the change that actually addresses the stated failure mode, which fsck.fat cannot: grub.cfg:27 calls `load_env` unguarded and then reads $validated/$booted_once/$boot. Harden it so an unreadable or torn grubenv degrades deterministically instead of reading garbage, e.g. if ! load_env ; then set boot=0 set validated=1 set booted_once=1 fi (pick the policy deliberately — forcing validated=1 disables one-shot rollback for that boot but guarantees a boot; the alternative is to fall through to slot A unvalidated). A 1 KiB in-place grubenv overwrite torn by a power cut leaves the FAT structurally clean, so `fsck.fat -a` will report success and change nothing.

**Uncertainty:** I did not confirm the exact install path Buildroot uses for fsck.fat (/sbin vs /usr/sbin) — the script should probe both, as it already does for cdsprpcd.

### [minor] Persistent partition contract differs (/data vs /root) and nothing persists an SSH host identity across A/B slots  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** files/wic/q6a-ab.wks.in declares `part data --ondisk mmcblk0 --fstype=ext4 --label=data --align 1024 --size 8` (last partition). The image recipe's ROOTFS_POSTPROCESS_COMMAND (q6a_image_tweaks) creates the mountpoint and appends to fstab: `LABEL=data /data ext4 defaults,x-systemd.growfs 0 0`. fwup/radxa-dragon-q6a.fwup sets NERVES_FW_APPLICATION_PART0_TARGET=/data with devpath /dev/disk/by-partlabel/data. What persists there: SSH host keys — `printf 'd /data/ssh 0700 root root -\n' > ${libdir}/tmpfiles.d/q6a-data-ssh.conf` plus three `HostKey /data/ssh/ssh_host_{rsa,ecdsa,ed25519}_key` lines appended to /etc/ssh/sshd_config, so "slot A/B present the same identity" and sshdgenkeys creates missing keys there on first boot (commit 29a06d7). Note the NervesHub agent's own state does NOT persist: its unit sets WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/nerves-hub-agent, which is on the per-slot rootfs.

**Ours today:** fwup.conf:35 `define(NERVES_FW_APPLICATION_PART0_TARGET, "/root")` with p4 "app" (expand = true) at fwup.conf:145-152; erlinit mounts it via rootfs_overlay/etc/erlinit.config:41 `-m /dev/rootdisk0p4:/root:ext4:nodev:`. Confirmed: no /data directory or mount exists anywhere (single repo-wide hit is a comment in install-to-disk.sh:16), and the rootfs is read-only squashfs (fwup.conf:39 ROOTFS=rootfs.squashfs), so /root is the only writable persistent path. Additionally — and the claim understates this — our system contains NO SSH server at all: no BR2_PACKAGE_OPENSSH/BR2_PACKAGE_DROPBEAR in nerves_defconfig, no /etc/ssh, no host keys in rootfs_overlay/etc, and none in deps/nerves_system_br/board/nerves-common/{skeleton,rootfs_overlay}. SSH on Nerves comes from the app's nerves_ssh dependency, so host-key placement is an application config decision, not a missing system file. install-to-disk.sh is not installed into $TARGET_DIR by post-build.sh; it ships only via mix.exs:118 package_files as a host-side helper.

**Why it matters:** Two concrete consequences. (1) Any doc, script or Elixir code carried over from the Yocto side that writes /data (BRINGUP-style `/data/app.fw`, install-to-disk.sh's own example `./install-to-disk.sh /data/app.fw /dev/nvme0n1`) will silently write into the read-only squashfs overlay or fail. install-to-disk.sh line 16 already contains that stale /data path. (2) When we add nerves_ssh, its host keys must be on p4 or the device's SSH fingerprint changes on every A/B switch — the exact problem Yocto fixed by moving HostKey to /data.

**Action:** Keep /root as the single persistence contract (already consistent across fwup.conf, fwup-ops.conf and erlinit.config — do not touch NERVES_FW_APPLICATION_PART0_TARGET). Then: (a) Fix the stale comment at install-to-disk.sh:16 — `./install-to-disk.sh /data/app.fw /dev/nvme0n1` should read `/root/app.fw`. Note this is a read path in a HOST-side script (the script is not installed into the target rootfs), so this is cosmetic doc hygiene, not a runtime fix. (b) Add a short "Persistence" section to README.md: rootfs is read-only squashfs; /root (p4, ext4, expand=true) is the only writable, A/B-shared location; /tmp and /run are tmpfs; /etc is immutable. (c) When the application adds nerves_ssh, set `system_dir: "/root/ssh"` in the app's config so the host identity survives A/B switches (the Yocto equivalent of HostKey /data/ssh/*), and create the 0700 dir from the app's Application.start (erlinit has already mounted p4 and Nerves.Runtime.Init formats it if corrupt). No change is required in this Buildroot repo for that; optionally ship an empty /root is not possible (p4 is formatted at first boot), so the dir must be created at runtime. Explicitly do NOT replicate /data: the Yocto side's /data lifecycle depends on systemd (`x-systemd.growfs` in fstab, tmpfiles.d for /data/ssh, sshdgenkeys.service for key generation), which has no analogue under BR2_INIT_NONE. A second erlinit -m line would also fight Nerves.Runtime.Init, which expects one app partition at NERVES_FW_APPLICATION_PART0_TARGET.

### [minor] No network configuration anywhere: the WiFi credential-injection pattern and NetworkManager have no counterpart wired up  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** packagegroup-qcom-utilities-network-utils is pulled in by the base image, not our layer: q6a-nerveshub-image.bb does `require recipes-products/images/qcom-console-image.bb`, and meta-qcom-distro/recipes-products/images/qcom-console-image.bb:11 lists `packagegroup-qcom-utilities-network-utils`. Its full RDEPENDS (packagegroup-qcom-utilities.bb:54-69) is ethtool, hostapd, iperf2, iproute2, iproute2-tc, iw, networkmanager, openssh-scp, openssh-ssh, paho-mqtt-c, rsync, smbclient, tcpdump, wowlan-udev; kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml:73 removes only iperf2. Credentials are baked at image time by q6a_image_tweaks (q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:118-137) into /etc/NetworkManager/system-connections/${WIFI_SSID}.nmconnection (dir 0700, file 0600, key-mgmt=wpa-psk, autoconnect=true, ipv4/ipv6 method=auto). Creds live in the gitignored kas/wifi.yml (.gitignore:27; wifi.yml.example committed) because kas-container drops host env vars (ec7dfea). Correction to the claim: at HEAD the recipe uses BITBAKE expansion `${WIFI_SSID}` — commit ae4362c's braceless `$WIFI_SSID` (shell expansion of BB_ENV_PASSTHROUGH) w…

**Ours today:** The Buildroot/system half is COMPLETE for vintage_net, not half-present: nerves_defconfig:82-91 (wpa_supplicant nl80211/AP/WPA3/EAP/WPS/CLI/passphrase, wireless-regdb, iw), nerves-common busybox.config already enabling ip/ipaddr/iplink/iproute/iprule/ipneigh (lines 921-934), udhcpc (1011), udhcpd (1004), ifconfig (898), IPv6 (857) — so BR2_PACKAGE_IPROUTE2 is unnecessary; kernel CONFIG_CFG80211=m + CONFIG_RFKILL=y (linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:181-182), which is sufficient because package/aic8800 is a full-mac cfg80211 driver (package/aic8800/Config.in:15), plus CONFIG_VIRTIO_NET=y (fragment:128) and built-in r8169. What is missing is only the demo application: example/mix.exs has no vintage_net/vintage_net_ethernet/vintage_net_wifi/nerves_time/nerves_ssh, and example/config/target.exs (8 lines) sets just ctty + RingLogger. example/lib/example.ex declares itself a "Minimal smoke-test payload… only job is to prove the boot chain reaches a live BEAM", so this is an intentionally bare demo, and README.md:105-128 already documents the intended vintage_net_ethernet/vintage_net_wifi story. Re…

**Why it matters:** Without networking there is no NervesHub check-in, no SSH, no NTP — and therefore no way to implement the app-side validation gate that finding #1 requires. It also means the QEMU smoke test's `-netdev user` NIC is never exercised beyond enumeration, so the r8169/virtio path is untested end to end.

**Action:** No Buildroot change is needed — this is entirely an example-app/docs task, and it should be filed as such rather than as a system gap. 1. example/mix.exs: add {:vintage_net, "~> 0.13"}, {:vintage_net_ethernet, "~> 0.11"}, {:vintage_net_wifi, "~> 0.12"}, {:nerves_time, "~> 0.4"} (all verified to exist: 0.13.12 / 0.11.2 / 0.12.9 / 0.4.12), or {:nerves_pack, "~> 0.7"} (0.7.2) which bundles those plus nerves_ssh + mdns_lite + nerves_motd. Also add :vintage_net to shoehorn's init list in example/config/config.exs alongside :nerves_runtime. 2. example/config/target.exs: mirror the kas/wifi.yml pattern with build-time env vars (host-side `mix firmware` sees plain env, unlike kas-container): wifi = case {System.get_env("WIFI_SSID"), System.get_env("WIFI_PSK")} do {nil, _} -> [] {_, nil} -> [] {ssid, psk} -> [{"wlan0", %{type: VintageNetWiFi, vintage_net_wifi: %{networks: [%{key_mgmt: :wpa_psk, ssid: ssid, psk: psk}]}, ipv4: %{method: :dhcp}}}] end config :vintage_net, config: [{"eth0", %{type: VintageNetEthernet, ipv4: %{method: :dhcp}}} | wifi] 3. Document the two persistence caveats instead of only the happy path: vintage_net's persistence_dir default is /root/vintage_net (deps/vintage_net/mix.exs:39) and /root is the ext4 app partition (fwup.conf:33-35), so (a) a unit that already has persisted config ignores newly baked application config — build-time creds are effectively first-boot-only, exactly like the Yocto nmconnection file; (b) fwup.conf:224 `raw_memset(${APP_PART_OFFSET}, 256, 0xff)` in the `complete` task invalidates /root, so a factory flash loses persisted WiFi — which is why an env-var fallback is worth keeping rather than relying on a one-time VintageNet.configure/2 from IEx. 4. Optional parity items with the Yocto image, all app-side: nerves_ssh for the openssh replacement (Yocto additionally persists host keys under /data; the Nerves analogue is nerves_ssh's authorized keys + the /root app partition), nerves_time for systemd-timesyncd. wowlan-udev, NetworkManager and hostapd have no Nerves analogue and should not be ported: vintage_net owns wpa_supplicant directly and vintage_net_wifi covers AP mode using busybox udhcpd, which is already enabled. 5. If you want the QEMU smoke test to actually exercise the NIC, add a DHCP-lease/ping assertion to test/qemu-smoke.sh after the IEx banner check — today it only greps for the console prompt (test/qemu-smoke.sh:172).

**Uncertainty:** Exact current version constraints for the vintage_net family were not verified against hex.pm; treat the `~>` values above as placeholders to check.

### [minor] NervesHub is wired into the Yocto image as a system service; on our side it is an application dependency that does not exist yet  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** meta-nerves-hub's nerves-hub-agent_0.1.0.bb builds joaohf/nerves_hub_agent (SRCREV c0795fbb, MIX_ENV=yocto) as a full Erlang release into /usr/lib/nerves-hub-agent, with a bitbake-fatal check that NERVES_HUB_LINK_PRODUCT_KEY and NERVES_HUB_LINK_PRODUCT_SECRET are set at build time, RDEPENDS fwup busybox boardid libubootenv-bin ca-certificates, and both a systemd unit and a sysvinit script. The unit: `Wants=network-online.target`, `After=network-online.target remote-fs.target`, User/Group=root, WorkingDirectory=/var/lib/nerves-hub-agent, `ExecStart=/usr/lib/nerves-hub-agent/bin/nerves_hub_agent start`, `Restart=on-failure`, `RestartSec=5`, `LimitNOFILE=65536`. Our layer's bbappend adds `RDEPENDS:${PN} += "q6a-boot-select"` (so validation tooling is always present) and seds NERVES_HUB_HOST into config/yocto.exs when it differs from devices.nervescloud.com. Secrets reach bitbake via `BB_ENV_PASSTHROUGH_ADDITIONS:append = " NERVES_HUB_LINK_PRODUCT_KEY NERVES_HUB_LINK_PRODUCT_SECRET NERVES_HUB_HOST"` in the kas local_conf_header, plus kas/keys.yml.example for FWUP_SIGN_ENABLE / FWUP_PRIVA…

**Ours today:** Nothing named nerves_hub_link exists on our side: example/mix.exs:28-43 lists only nerves, shoehorn, ring_logger, toolshed, nerves_runtime and the system itself, and neither example/config/config.exs nor example/config/target.exs configures :nerves_hub_link. Confirmed absent. But the OS-level side is complete, not a gap: BR2_PACKAGE_CA_CERTIFICATES=y (nerves_defconfig:55) and BR2_PACKAGE_NERVES_CONFIG=y (nerves_defconfig:76) which selects BR2_PACKAGE_FWUP, BR2_PACKAGE_UBOOT_TOOLS (whose BR2_PACKAGE_UBOOT_TOOLS_FWPRINTENV is default y, so fw_printenv/fw_setenv ship) and BR2_PACKAGE_BOARDID — deps/nerves_system_br/package/nerves-config/Config.in:3-12; /etc/boardid.config and /etc/fw_env.config are in rootfs_overlay. Busybox NTPD (added by meta-nerves-hub's busybox_%.bbappend for the agent) is also not needed: nerves_hub_link 2.x depends on nerves_time non-optionally, so SNTP happens in the BEAM. The autovalidate hand-off is ALREADY documented on our side — README.md:77-79, fwup.conf:186-189, fwup-ops.conf:313, CHANGELOG.md:70 all state that nerves_fw_autovalidate=0 is the NervesHub mod…

**Why it matters:** This is the biggest structural difference in the domain and it is a genuine design decision, not a porting gap: everything the Yocto unit expresses (ordering after network-online, restart-on-failure, LimitNOFILE, root privileges) is either free or meaningless under erlinit — the OTP supervision tree handles restarts, and the BEAM already runs as root with erlinit's limits. But it also means the system artifact can never ship NervesHub; if the application forgets it, the device has no OTA path and finding #1's validation gate has nothing to key off.

**Action:** No Buildroot package — correct. Revised, concretely: (1) Do NOT add {:nerves_hub_link, ...} to example/mix.exs. example/ is the CI harness (.github/workflows/ci.yml builds it, then test/qemu-smoke and test/qemu-rollback.sh run against example/_build/dragon_q6a_dev/nerves/images/example.fw); NervesHubLink pulls mint/slipstream/x509/nerves_time/whenwhere and starts a Configurator at app boot, which in QEMU has no network and needs `connect: false` (cf. recamera-tracker/config/host.exs:9, config/target.exs:217,252). Instead put the snippet in README.md as an opt-in example for downstream apps: {:nerves_hub_link, "~> 2.11"} plus `config :nerves_hub_link, host: System.get_env("NERVES_HUB_HOST", "devices.nervescloud.com"), shared_secret: [product_key: System.fetch_env!("NERVES_HUB_LINK_PRODUCT_KEY"), product_secret: System.fetch_env!("NERVES_HUB_LINK_PRODUCT_SECRET")]` in the app's config/target.exs — the direct analogue of the kas env passthrough (kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml:54-61). The shared_secret shape is verified real (nerves_hub_link configurator.ex:90). (2) README.md section "Using with NervesHub": state that the agent is an application dependency inside the same BEAM erlinit starts (no OS service, so Wants=network-online / Restart=on-failure / User=root have no counterpart and need none), and that the system already provides fwup, boardid, fw_printenv/fw_setenv, ca-certificates and nerves-heart. Cross-reference the existing autovalidate text (README.md:77-79) rather than re-documenting it. (3) Firmware signing — the accurate flow, not "mix firmware + NERVES_FW_PRIV_KEY" (that env var does not exist in Nerves; no signing option in example/deps/nerves/lib/mix/tasks/*): generate a keypair with `fwup -g`, sign the built artifact post-build with `fwup -S -s <priv.key> -i example.fw -o example-signed.fw` (or `mix nerves_hub.firmware sign --key <name>` from nerves_hub_cli), and verify on-device via NervesHubLink's `fwup_public_keys` (configurator.ex:49; streaming_updater.ex:68-79 forwards them to fwup) or `fwup -a --public-key-file` for manual applies. Yocto's equivalent is meta-fwup/classes/image_types_fwup.bbclass:51-52 (`--private-key-file`/`--public-key-file`, plus `-V` verify) driven by kas/keys.yml.example:9-12. Note explicitly that fwup.conf cannot itself mandate a signature — enforcement is a property of the applier's arguments — so the requirement must live in the app config and in whatever CI signs the release. (4) New, missing from the original action: decide whether to match LimitNOFILE=65536. Our erlinit.config sets no rlimits and `ulimit -n` in…

**Uncertainty:** nerves_hub_link's current major version and its exact shared-secret config keys were not verified against hex docs.

### [minor] Thermal management: thermald cannot be ported (x86-only in Buildroot) and our kernel fragment pins no THERMAL symbols at all  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** The install list, URM versions and systemd watchdog are all confirmed verbatim (q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:51-53 `thermald \ userspace-resource-manager \ userspace-resource-manager-extensions \`, :57 the profile-utils remove; userspace-resource-manager_0.4.3.bb / userspace-resource-manager-extensions_0.3.8.bb with `inherit cmake pkgconfig systemd`, the `state-detector systemd` PACKAGECONFIG and `SYSTEMD_SERVICE:${PN} = "urm.service"`; qcom-base.inc `# Activate systemd watchdog` / `WATCHDOG_RUNTIME_SEC:pn-systemd = "30"`). The correction is that meta-oe's thermald is NOT the x86-only Intel daemon the claim assumes. /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-openembedded/meta-oe/recipes-bsp/thermald/thermald_2.5.11.bb declares `COMPATIBLE_HOST = '(i.86|x86_64|aarch64|arm).*-linux'` and applies three in-tree patches, one of which is `0003-Add-ARM-backend-and-enable-ARM-platform-detection.patch`, alongside `0001-Refactor-Intel-specific-logic-into-separate-files.patch` and `0002-Invoke-parser_init-before-platform_match.patch`. So on this board thermald is a working ARM userspace th…

**Ours today:** WRONG on the central point. linux-dragon-q6a.fragment already has an explicit thermal block — line 203 is literally `# --- cpufreq / thermal / PMIC ADC (board thermal zones) ---` followed by CONFIG_ARM_QCOM_CPUFREQ_HW=y (204), CONFIG_QCOM_LMH=y (205), CONFIG_QCOM_SPMI_ADC5=y (206), CONFIG_QCOM_SPMI_ADC_TM5=y (207), CONFIG_QCOM_SPMI_TEMP_ALARM=y (208) — plus CONFIG_QCOM_TSENS=y at line 40. Three of the nine symbols the claim wants added are already present verbatim, and the cpufreq driver the cooling device rides on is present too. The claim's stated evidence ("grep for THERMAL returns only CONFIG_QCOM_LMH=y") is false twice over: a literal grep for THERMAL in that file returns nothing at all, and the real thermal symbol set is four lines. CHANGELOG.md:77-82 ("PMIC ADC/thermal zones, LMH, cpufreq") is accurate, not an overclaim. The actual, much narrower gap: the thermal *core* symbols are unpinned and inherited from BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_USE_ARCH_DEFAULT_CONFIG=y (nerves_defconfig:40) — CONFIG_THERMAL, THERMAL_OF, THERMAL_GOV_STEP_WISE, CPU_THERMAL, CPU_FREQ_THERMAL, DEVFREQ_THERMAL. Beca…

**Why it matters:** On mainline sc7280 the real throttling work is kernel-side (DT thermal zones + cpufreq/devfreq cooling + LMh), so losing thermald is acceptable — but only if those kernel options are actually =y. This is the same argument the repo already used for `QRTR=y` ("guards the CDSP chain against silent =m demotion"): a defconfig change upstream could silently demote thermal support and a fanless QCS6490 under sustained NPU/GPU load would then run unthrottled. Nothing in our test harness would notice.

**Action:** Do NOT package thermald or URM — but state the honest reason: it is portable (meta-oe's 0001/0002/0003 patches, incl. `0003-Add-ARM-backend-and-enable-ARM-platform-detection.patch`, are in our sibling tree and Buildroot's x86 gate is just a Config.in line we could drop in a local package/thermald), and we are declining it because it drags in dbus + dbus-glib + upower + libevdev + libxml2 and ships only a systemd unit, for a job mainline sc7280 does in-kernel. Kernel fragment: only the FRAMEWORK symbols are missing. Do not re-add what is already there (lines 40, 204, 206, 207, 208). Append next to the existing block at linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:208: ``` # --- Thermal core: pin explicitly. QCOM_TSENS (line 40) is tristate and # --- depends on THERMAL_OF; a defconfig demotion would silently take our # --- pinned TSENS with it (same reasoning as CONFIG_QRTR=y). CONFIG_THERMAL=y CONFIG_THERMAL_OF=y CONFIG_THERMAL_GOV_STEP_WISE=y CONFIG_CPU_THERMAL=y CONFIG_CPU_FREQ_THERMAL=y CONFIG_PM_DEVFREQ=y CONFIG_DEVFREQ_THERMAL=y ``` Omit CONFIG_THERMAL_DEFAULT_GOV_STEP_WISE=y: it is a choice member and would flip the default governor away from the arm64 defconfig's selection on a board we have never thermally profiled. Decide it after reading the produced output/build/linux-*/.config, not before. CONFIG_PM_DEVFREQ is required for DEVFREQ_THERMAL (Adreno/LLCC devfreq cooling) — verify it is not already =y from defconfig before adding, to keep the fragment minimal. Bench step (this part of the claim is a genuine gap — nothing in test/ or BRINGUP.md mentions thermal at all): add to BRINGUP.md §4 `cat /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone*/type` + `cat /sys/class/thermal/thermal_zone*/temp`, expect the sc7280 tsens zones (cpu*-*-usr, gpuss-*-usr, aoss*-usr, ...), then a `stress-ng --cpu 8 --timeout 300s` soak while watching `/sys/devices/system/cpu/cpufreq/policy*/scaling_cur_freq` drop, to prove cooling devices bind. Add BR2_PACKAGE_STRESS_NG=y to nerves_defconfig for that (dev-only; note it in README if it stays in production images). Record URM as an explicitly-declined design decision.

**Uncertainty:** Exact symbol names/defaults for the 6.18 arm64 defconfig were not checked — some of the listed options may already be =y there, in which case pinning them is a no-op guard (which is the point). CONFIG_QCOM_SPMI_ADC_TM5 vs the newer ADC-TM binding should be confirmed against the board DTS.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-products/images/q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:51-53,57 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom/recipes-support/userspace-resource-manager/userspace-resource-manager_0.4.3.bb:13-28 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom-distro/conf/distro/include/qcom-base.inc:36-37 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/thermald/Config.in:3 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:38,136,205 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/CHANGELOG.md:77-82</sub>


### [minor] Our QEMU harness proves less on-target than Yocto's: no /proc/cmdline, fwup or boardid assertions  *(corrected)*

**Proven system:** test/qemu-boot-test.py drives the guest interactively with pexpect: expects `Linux version`, then `login:`, sends root/root, expects `# `, then runs commands through a `; echo END_$?` sentinel with an ANSI/OSC-3008 stripper (`ANSI = re.compile(r"\x1b\][^\x1b]*(?:\x1b\\|\x07)|\x1b\[[0-9;?]*[ -/]*[@-~]")`) and records four checks: /proc/cmdline contains `root=PARTLABEL=rootfs-a`; `systemctl is-system-running` (running = PASS, degraded = WARN "expected under QEMU: no GPU, no hub", anything else = FAIL); `fwup --version` returns 0 and parses a version; `boardid` returns 0 and prints 12 hex chars. It also derives the ESP offset instead of hardcoding it (`sgdisk -i 1 <disk>` -> `First sector: N` * 512), patches the loader entry idempotently via mcopy, copies the image before booting (`cp --reflink=auto --sparse=always -L`, never boots the deploy artifact rw), and truncates a fresh 64 MiB edk2-vars.fd each run.

**Ours today:** test/qemu-smoke.sh is log-grep only (:195-207: "Booting slot A", "Linux version 6\.18", erlinit, "Interactive Elixir|iex\(", plus WARN-only "EXAMPLE APP UP") — it executes and asserts nothing on-target. But test/qemu-rollback.sh already IS an on-target assertion suite and already runs in CI (.github/workflows/ci.yml:128-135): it boots, erlinit's --pre-run-exec runs /usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh which executes `/usr/bin/fwup -q -t reconcile|autovalidate -d /dev/rootdisk0 /usr/share/fwup/ops.fw` (coldplug:22-24), and the test asserts the resulting on-target state (rollback.sh:182-184 grubenv validated=1, uboot-env nerves_fw_validated=1, nerves_fw_active=b; :198 reconcile flipped nerves_fw_active back to a). That is functionally stronger than Yocto's `fwup --version`. What we genuinely lack vs Yocto: (i) no assertion that `boardid` works on-target (it is only used implicitly by erlinit for the hostname, rootfs_overlay/etc/boardid.config + erlinit.config `-d /usr/bin/boardid`), (ii) no direct assertion of which slot's rootfs actually mounted (our cmdline uses a probed `root=PARTUUID=`, not …

**Why it matters:** The v0.5.3 bug (busybox ASH_CMDCMD off, so `command -v fwup` failed and the reconcile/autovalidate calls silently never ran on any boot) survived from v0.4.0 because nothing ever executed anything on-target and the rollback test had never run in CI. A single on-target `fwup --version` / `fwup -t status` assertion would have caught it immediately. The hardcoded ESP offsets will silently break the tests if BOOT_PART_OFFSET in fwup.conf ever changes.

**Action:** 1) Add a boot self-test to example/lib/example/application.ex, but source the slot from fwup rather than /proc/cmdline, and go through nerves_runtime (already a dep) so fwup's framed output is parsed correctly: `IO.puts("SELFTEST cmdline: " <> File.read!("/proc/cmdline"))` (informational only — grub.cfg:98-105 / test/grub.cfg.qemu:47-53 emit `root=PARTUUID=$nerves_root_uuid` probed at boot, so it can never name slot A/B) `IO.puts("SELFTEST fwstatus: " <> inspect(Nerves.Runtime.FwupOps.status()))` -> `{:ok, %{active: "a", next: "a"}}`; this is the real slot assertion. Do NOT use `System.cmd("/usr/bin/fwup", ["-t","status",...])` capturing stdout — fwup's info() is not on stdout without `--framing` (see example/deps/nerves_runtime/lib/nerves_runtime/fwup_ops.ex:180-195 and the `{:warning, "a"}` parser at :147-150); that check would match "" and always FAIL. If you insist on a raw call, pass `--framing` or `stderr_to_stdout: true`. `IO.puts("SELFTEST boardid: " <> elem(System.cmd("/usr/bin/boardid", []), 0))` `IO.puts("SELFTEST root: " <> File.read!("/proc/self/mountinfo"))` (or compare the dev of "/" against /dev/rootdisk0p2) — busybox here has no `findmnt` applet, so don't shell out to it. 2) Promote to hard checks in test/qemu-smoke.sh: `check "fwup status reports slot a" "SELFTEST fwstatus: .*active: \"a\""`, `check "boardid works on target" "SELFTEST boardid: [0-9a-f]{12}"`. Keep the cmdline line as warn_check (it proves a PARTUUID root, nothing more). 3) Derive the ESP offset from fwup.conf instead of hardcoding, WITHOUT adding sgdisk: `ESP_OFFSET=$(( $(sed -n 's/^define(BOOT_PART_OFFSET, *\([0-9]*\)).*/\1/p' "$REPO_DIR/fwup.conf") * 512 ))`. sgdisk is only in shell.nix; the CI job never enters it — ci.yml:114-122 apt-installs just qemu-system-arm/qemu-efi-aarch64/mtools plus the fwup .deb, and both scripts re-exec `nix-shell -p qemu fwup mtools` (qemu-smoke.sh:36, qemu-rollback.sh:42). If you do want sgdisk, you must also add `gdisk` to that apt list and `gptfdisk` to both `nix-shell -p` lines. Factor find_edk2 (+ the QEMU invocation and the grubenv/uboot-env probes) into test/lib.sh; find_edk2 is byte-identical in both scripts. 4) Fix qemu-smoke.sh:126-127 to `rm -f "$VARS"; truncate -s 64M "$VARS"` so the "fresh each run" comment becomes true (matches qemu-rollback.sh:170). 5) Assert DISK_SIZE covers the layout instead of relying on the comment (1 + 32 + 2048 + 2048 + 512 = 4641 MiB minimum vs DISK_SIZE=6144). 6) Process fix, which is what actually let the v0.5.3 bug ship: never tag a release without CI having executed test/qemu-rollback.sh on that…

**Uncertainty:** busybox's findmnt is not enabled in the nerves-common config, so the root-device assertion should read /proc/self/mountinfo or /proc/cmdline rather than shelling out to findmnt.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/test/qemu-boot-test.py:22-23,46-76,128-162 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/qemu-smoke.sh:104-116,190-208 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/test/qemu-rollback.sh:35-36,132-155 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/CHANGELOG.md:12-23 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/busybox.fragment:1-10</sub>


### [minor] A crashed or failed boot leaves no evidence on disk (Yocto explicitly added persistent logs for exactly this)  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** q6a_image_tweaks installs /etc/systemd/journald.conf.d/10-persistent.conf with `[Journal]\nStorage=persistent` — commented "DEBUG (bring-up): persistent journal so failed boots leave logs on disk" (commit c8e063d). The distro also sets `VIRTUAL-RUNTIME_base-utils-syslog = "rsyslog"` and the image adds `post-install-logging` + `tools-debug` IMAGE_FEATURES.

**Ours today:** Nothing persists. example/config/config.exs sets `config :logger, backends: [RingLogger]` — an in-RAM ring buffer. Kernel messages go to the serial console only (grub.cfg cmdline `console=ttyMSM0,115200n8 ... loglevel=4`). The rootfs is read-only squashfs; the only writable location is /root (p4), which nothing writes logs to.

**Why it matters:** On the bench you can watch the UART, but a field device that reboots at 03:00 leaves zero forensic trail — and with the hardware watchdog + nerves_heart resetting a wedged BEAM, silent reboot loops are exactly the scenario you need logs for. This interacts with finding #1: after a rollback there is currently no way to learn *why* the new slot failed.

**Action:** Two cheap steps. (1) In rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh, after the app partition is available, snapshot the previous boot's kernel log — but note /root is mounted by erlinit *after* the pre-run hook, so do this from Elixir instead. (2) Application-side: add a small persistent log writer on /root, e.g. `{:ring_logger, "~> 0.11"}` (already a dep) plus a periodic `RingLogger.save("/root/log/ring.log")` and, at startup, `File.write("/root/log/last-dmesg.txt", elem(System.cmd("dmesg", []), 0))` with simple rotation (busybox dmesg is enabled: CONFIG_DMESG=y in the nerves-common config). Document the location in README next to the /root persistence contract. If you want kernel-panic capture across resets, evaluate ramoops/pstore (CONFIG_PSTORE_RAM) with a reserved-memory region in the DTS — a separate, larger piece of work.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-products/images/q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:90-92 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom-distro/conf/distro/include/qcom-base.inc:39-40 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/example/config/config.exs:10 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:75 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/deps/nerves_system_br/board/nerves-common/busybox.config:609</sub>


### [minor] No way to read or change UEFI boot state from the OS (no efibootmgr/efivar, no CONFIG_EFIVAR_FS)  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** The image installs `efibootmgr` (IMAGE_INSTALL:append) and `efivar` arrives via packagegroup-qcom-utilities-support-utils. q6a-boot-select uses the EFI side directly: both units gate on `ConditionPathExists=/sys/firmware/efi/efivars`, and the script calls `bootctl --esp-path=/boot set-default`/`set-oneshot` to steer systemd-boot.

**Ours today:** nerves_defconfig selects neither efibootmgr nor efivar, and linux-dragon-q6a.fragment has CONFIG_EFI=y / CONFIG_EFI_STUB=y (lines 117-119) but no CONFIG_EFIVAR_FS, so /sys/firmware/efi/efivars is not even mounted. Slot selection lives entirely in /EFI/BOOT/grubenv on the FAT ESP, so nothing in the normal boot path needs efivars.

**Why it matters:** Mostly fine — and arguably better, since grubenv-on-FAT is one authority instead of two. But it means install-to-disk.sh's workflow ("Afterwards pick the boot medium in the UEFI setup (boot order)") can only be completed by hand at the UEFI menu with a keyboard and display. After provisioning an M.2 from a running SD-booted device you cannot make the board boot the NVMe remotely.

**Action:** Only if remote boot-order control is wanted: add `CONFIG_EFIVAR_FS=y` to linux-dragon-q6a.fragment, `BR2_PACKAGE_EFIBOOTMGR=y` (pulls BR2_PACKAGE_EFIVAR + popt automatically) to nerves_defconfig, and mount efivarfs from rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh (`mkdir -p /sys/firmware/efi/efivars; mount -t efivarfs none /sys/firmware/efi/efivars 2>/dev/null || true`). Otherwise, document in README/install-to-disk.sh that boot-medium selection is a manual UEFI-menu step and that our A/B slot state is grubenv-only (no EFI variables involved) — which is a deliberate simplification versus Yocto's bootctl-based converge.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-products/images/q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:71 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom-distro/recipes-products/packagegroups/packagegroup-qcom-utilities.bb:80-90 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select.service:6 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/linux-dragon-q6a.fragment:117-119 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/install-to-disk.sh:18 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/deps/nerves_system_br/buildroot-2026.05/package/efibootmgr/Config.in:1-9</sub>


### [minor] Both fwup layouts assume 512-byte logical sectors; the Yocto machine conf at least flags the UFS case  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf line 53: `QCOM_VFAT_SECTOR_SIZE ?= "512"` with the comment "SD card uses 512-byte sectors; override to 4096 for UFS targets". Their bootenv is a real GPT partition (p4, 1 MiB) and fw_env.config was made media-relative at runtime by q6a-boot-select (`printf '%s %s %s\n' "${disk}p4" 0x0 0x20000 > /etc/fw_env.config`), after commits showed by-partlabel symlinks and a hardcoded/32-bit-truncated offset both failing (330a601, ccb29e0, 47560a5, 30a00aa — the last bumped fwup 1.13.2 -> 1.16.0 because uboot_env.block_offset is uint32_t and their env at ~6.8 GiB overflowed).

**Ours today:** fwup.conf puts the KV store in a raw region outside any partition (`UBOOT_ENV_OFFSET 64`, `UBOOT_ENV_COUNT 16`) and rootfs_overlay/etc/fw_env.config hardcodes the matching `/dev/rootdisk0 0x8000 0x2000 0x200 16` — the 0x200 field is the flash sector size, i.e. 512. All partition offsets in fwup.conf/fwup-ops.conf are in 512-byte blocks. README claims one image works from "microSD / UFS / eMMC ... NVMe on M.2", and install-to-disk.sh burns to arbitrary block devices. Our env sits at 32 KiB so the fwup <1.14 uint32 overflow bug cannot bite us (and require-fwup-version="1.12.0" is satisfied by the 1.13.1 in CI/Dockerfile).

**Why it matters:** On a 4096-byte-logical-sector device (some UFS/eMMC configurations) fwup's block arithmetic and the 0x200 sector size in fw_env.config no longer describe the medium, so the GPT, the raw KV region and fw_printenv/fw_setenv can all land in the wrong place — with the KV store being the silent failure (boardid falls back to macaddr, fwup's require-uboot-variable checks miss, upgrades refuse or misbehave). The Yocto side carries an explicit knob and a comment; we carry an unqualified claim in README.

**Action:** Cheap: qualify the claim and add a bench check. In README's Storage row and the Boot architecture section, state that the layout assumes 512-byte logical sectors (validated on microSD; verify `cat /sys/block/<dev>/queue/logical_block_size` before burning UFS/eMMC). Add to BRINGUP.md §3 a pre-flash step printing logical_block_size for the target medium, and a post-flash check `fw_printenv nerves_fw_active` + `boardid` (which is also the on-target assertion finding #9 asks the QEMU test to make). If a 4096-byte target is ever required, both fwup.conf and fwup-ops.conf offsets plus rootfs_overlay/etc/fw_env.config's sector-size field need a coordinated review — they are already flagged "must be kept in sync" in both files.

**Uncertainty:** Whether the Dragon Q6A's eMMC/UFS actually presents 4096-byte logical sectors was not determined — the Yocto comment implies it can. This needs a bench measurement, not a code change, until then.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-qcom-3rdparty/conf/machine/radxa-dragon-q6a.conf:52-53 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select:18-22 · radxa-q6a-yocto git 30a00aa, 47560a5, ccb29e0, 330a601 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup.conf:57-58,11 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/rootfs_overlay/etc/fw_env.config:8-11 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/README.md (Storage row)</sub>


### [info] q6a-boot-select `converge` has no analogue and needs none — but we also inherited no retry budget for a new slot  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** q6a-boot-select `converge` (lines 49-70) exists because systemd-boot's default entry and the fwup KV store are two independent authorities that can disagree: if the booted slot != nerves_fw_active it either (a) re-persists the intended slot with `bootctl set-default` + `set-oneshot` and reboots (when validated=1), or (b) burns one retry — `fw_setenv q6a_boot_attempts $((attempts + 1))` + `set-oneshot` + reboot, while attempts < 1 — or (c) gives up and reverts nerves_fw_active to the slot that actually booted. The unit runs early (`DefaultDependencies=no`, `After=local-fs.target`, `Before=nerves_hub_agent.service`). The script also rewrites /etc/fw_env.config for the actual boot disk at every boot, resolving the disk via `findmnt -n -o MAJ:MIN /` then `/sys/dev/block/<maj:min>` (deliberately kernel-provided, no udev timing dependency — commits be5f6d8, eabd2b1, eef6a55) and keeps `<slot>.nerves_fw_application_part0_devpath` in sync with the media (commit d6ab42b).

**Ours today:** GRUB reads /EFI/BOOT/grubenv itself, so there is exactly one authority and the converge case cannot arise; fwup-ops.conf's `reconcile.a`/`reconcile.b` (matched by `require-path-on-device("/", "/dev/rootdisk0p2|p3")` vs nerves_fw_active) covers the only real divergence, and rootfs_overlay/etc/fw_env.config is static because erlinit's /dev/rootdisk0 symlink is already media-agnostic (and the squashfs is read-only, so runtime rewriting is impossible anyway). What we do NOT have is Yocto's `q6a_boot_attempts` retry: grub.cfg gives a fresh slot exactly one boot, and if it misses validation the *next* boot flips permanently to the other slot and marks it validated=1.

**Why it matters:** Our design is genuinely simpler and should be kept. The retry difference is a real behavioural delta though: a single transient first-boot failure (e.g. a slow SD card, a one-off DSP probe timeout) permanently rolls back a good release on our system, whereas Yocto retries once. With finding #1 fixed (validation gated on app health) the probability of a transient miss goes up, so a retry budget becomes more valuable.

**Action:** Keep the current architecture; document it in README as an intentional divergence ("GRUB is the single slot authority; there is no bootctl/EFI-variable convergence step and no dynamic fw_env.config rewrite — /dev/rootdisk0 is already stable"). If a retry budget is wanted, it fits naturally in grub.cfg: add a `tries` variable to the grubenv blocks generated by post-build.sh (`grub-editenv $BINARIES_DIR/grubenv_a set tries=0`) and, in the `validated != 1 && booted_once == 1` branch, decrement/count before flipping `boot`. That change must be mirrored in post-build.sh (all four grubenv variants), grub.cfg, test/grub.cfg.qemu and test/qemu-rollback.sh's write_grubenv helper.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select:8-32,49-70 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-core/q6a-boot-select/files/q6a-boot-select.service:1-14 · radxa-q6a-yocto git be5f6d8, eabd2b1, eef6a55, d6ab42b, ccb29e0, 330a601 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/grub.cfg:48-62 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/fwup-ops.conf:281-305 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/rootfs_overlay/etc/fw_env.config:11 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/post-build.sh:17-33</sub>


### [info] Masked units (tpm2.target, qteesupplicant) have no analogue, but the underlying lesson — never block boot on a device that may not appear — applies to our one waiting call  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** q6a_image_tweaks masks tpm2.target with `ln -sf /dev/null ${sysconfdir}/systemd/system/tpm2.target`, commented: "TPM device nodes never appear on this platform; tpm2.target's 90s device waits triple boot time and break the OTA validate window". Commit dcbf15b: dev-tpm0/tpmrm0/tee0 never appeared, the sequential 90 s waits pushed slot-B first boot past the then-2-minute validate window and triggered a spurious rollback — the fix was masking plus widening the window to 6 min. qteesupplicant was disabled via SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE instead of masking, because masking broke `systemctl preset-all` (commit 0f32caa); that override was later dropped entirely (commit 3c045e5) once CONFIG_QCOMTEE created tee0 and the service ran fine. The image recipe comment records both facts.

**Ours today:** Nothing to mask: with BR2_INIT_NONE there is no unit graph and no device-wait semantics. The only bounded wait in our boot is `udevadm settle --timeout=10` in rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh line 33; everything else in that script is `|| true`-guarded and non-blocking, and the cdsprpcd/adsprpcd launches are backgrounded.

**Why it matters:** The Yocto experience is a warning about the failure mode, not a config to port: a boot-time wait that is invisible on a working board can silently exceed the OTA validation window and cause spurious rollbacks. Our current script is well-behaved, but the pre-run hook is a single serial choke point in front of the BEAM — anything added there (the ESP fsck from finding #3, for example) directly delays the app and must be bounded.

**Action:** No change required. Add a comment at the top of rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh stating the invariant: "Everything here runs before the BEAM. Every step must be bounded and non-fatal — an unbounded wait here delays the app and (once app-gated validation lands) can cause a spurious rollback." Enforce it for the new fsck step via `timeout 60` (finding #3). Also worth recording: our system takes the opposite stance on QTEE/TPM — we ship no tee/TPM userspace at all (the tpm2-tss-engine package exists in nerves_system_br but is not selected), so the whole class of problem is absent.

<sub>evidence: /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/radxa-q6a-yocto/meta-q6a-nerveshub/recipes-products/images/q6a-nerveshub-image.bb:94-99 · radxa-q6a-yocto git dcbf15b, 0f32caa, 7b95684, c7fd105, 3c045e5 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/rootfs_overlay/usr/sbin/qcom-coldplug.sh:27-53 · /home/fermuch/Documents/Dev/AYVU/nerves_system_dragon_q6a/deps/nerves_system_br/package/tpm2-tss-engine/Config.in</sub>


### [info] kas local_conf_header overrides: what each one means for us (most need no analogue; two do)  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** kas/radxa-q6a-nerveshub.yml local_conf_header, entry by entry: `nerveshub` — BB_ENV_PASSTHROUGH_ADDITIONS for NERVES_HUB_LINK_PRODUCT_KEY/SECRET and NERVES_HUB_HOST, because bitbake sanitises the environment; `slim` — `DISTRO_FEATURES:remove = "virtualization kvm"` to drop packagegroup-qcom-virtualization + packagegroup-container (qemu/libvirt/docker/containerd), which qcom-base.inc adds by default; `kernel` — PREFERRED_PROVIDER_virtual/kernel=linux-qcom + PREFERRED_VERSION 6.18% because "mainline-next 7.1 has no AIC8800 WiFi path; 6.18.y matches the vendor kernel"; `drop-iperf2` — `RDEPENDS:packagegroup-qcom-utilities-network-utils:remove = "iperf2"` (commit 54c4630 notes the earlier `pn-` attempt was wrong: pn- takes the recipe name, not the package name); `weston-off` — `SYSTEMD_AUTO_ENABLE:pn-weston-init = "disable"` so bring-up lands on a tty1 getty over HDMI; `parallelism` — BB_NUMBER_THREADS=10 / PARALLEL_MAKE=-j 10 to match the container's cgroup limits (kas-container --runtime-args "--cpus=10 --memory=16g" in devenv.nix); `workarounds` — `PTEST_ENABLED:pn-icu = "0"` because …

**Ours today:** Buildroot has no analogue for most of these by construction — we never select virtualization/containers, Buildroot builds no ptests, and there is no weston. The kernel pin has a direct equivalent: nerves_defconfig lines 33-43 pin BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_CUSTOM_TARBALL_LOCATION to Deka linux-dragon-q6a @ e05c4fa (mainline 6.18.0), for the same reason (the out-of-tree AIC8800 driver in package/aic8800 builds against 6.18). Build parallelism is governed by the Nerves Docker build_runner configured in mix.exs (build_runner_config docker: {"Dockerfile", "fermuch/nerves-dragon-q6a-builder:latest"}) with no explicit -j cap. The build-time-secret problem is not solved on our side either — mix.exs `env:` only passes TARGET_ARCH/CPU/OS/ABI/GCC_FLAGS into the container.

**Why it matters:** Two items are actually transferable. (1) The kernel-version rationale should be recorded identically on both sides so nobody "upgrades" us to 7.x and silently loses WiFi — our nerves_defconfig comment says "Frozen tuple (see GOAL.md)" but not "AIC8800 has no path on 7.x". (2) The secrets-into-the-container problem is a live constraint if we ever need build-time secrets in the *system* build (they are not needed today: WiFi PSK and NervesHub keys belong to the application build, which runs on the host).

**Action:** (1) Extend the comment above BR2_LINUX_KERNEL_CUSTOM_TARBALL_LOCATION in nerves_defconfig to state the constraint explicitly: "Do not move past 6.18.y: package/aic8800 (Radxa's out-of-tree AIC8800D80 driver) has no 7.x compat path — same reason the Yocto reference pins PREFERRED_VERSION_linux-qcom = 6.18%." (2) Add a note to README's Building section that the Docker build_runner only forwards the variables listed in mix.exs `env:`, so any future build-time input must be added there explicitly (the exact class of bug kas/wifi.yml exists to work around). (3) No action for slim/iperf2/weston-off/PTEST/mariadb/perf — record in the comparison doc that they are Yocto-distro artifacts with no Buildroot counterpart, so future audits do not re-litigate them.

### [info] Userland inventory delta: what Yocto ships that we do not, and which of it matters  *(unverified)*

**Proven system:** q6a-nerveshub-image.bb CORE_IMAGE_BASE_INSTALL + IMAGE_INSTALL:append, minus what the base qcom-console/minimal images drag in. Deliberate GUI/media stack (weston, weston-init, wayland-utils, weston-xwayland, packagegroup-qcom-utilities-gpu-utils, libopencl-mesa, mesa-vulkan-drivers, mesa-demos, igt-gpu-tools-tests, gstd, gstreamer1.0 + base/good/bad + python, libcamera(-gst), libv4l, media-ctl, v4l-utils, yavta, opencv(-apps), alsa-utils-{alsatplg,alsaucm,aplay}). Ops/debug: fastrpc-tests, dmidecode, stress-ng, thermald, userspace-resource-manager(+extensions), q6a-spi-firmware, aic8800-usb, efibootmgr, openssh, github-ssh-keys, fwup-fw-env, q6a-boot-env, q6a-boot-select, plus from the base images: kernel-modules, resize-rootfs, zram, fwupd, libgomp, packagegroup-qcom-security, bluez5 tools, gdb/strace/valgrind, e2fsprogs(+e2fsck/mke2fs/resize2fs/tune2fs), ethtool/hostapd/iproute2(-tc)/iw/NetworkManager/openssh-scp/openssh-ssh/paho-mqtt-c/rsync/smbclient/tcpdump/wowlan-udev, dtc/efivar/file/less/ltrace/procps/tinyalsa/trace-cmd/usbutils, rsyslog. Explicitly removed: packagegroup-qco…

**Ours today:** nerves_defconfig covers the equivalents that matter for a headless single-app device: e2fsprogs (minus fsck), gptfdisk/sgdisk, ca-certificates, libdrm(+tests)/mesa3d freedreno/kmscube, alsa-lib + alsa-utils (alsactl/alsamixer/alsaucm/amixer/aplay/speaker-test), libv4l(+utils), gstreamer1 base/good/bad + libav, ffmpeg(+ffprobe, GPL, swscale), wpa_supplicant/wireless-regdb/iw, aic8800, the four NPU packages, nbtty, erlang 28, nerves-config (which pulls erlinit, boardid, fwup, host-fwup, uboot-tools, openssl, ncurses, nerves_heart, squashfs). Missing and worth adding: dosfstools+fsck_fat (finding #3), dmidecode (finding #2), thermal kernel pins (finding #8). Missing by design: weston/wayland/XWayland (we render fullscreen KMS/GBM), opencl/vulkan, opencv, python3, gstd, libcamera, NetworkManager, openssh, rsyslog, fwupd, zram, resize-rootfs, bluez5 userspace (BT is BlueHeron-from-Elixir per README), gdb/strace/valgrind/perf/ltrace/trace-cmd, smbclient/paho-mqtt-c/hostapd/tcpdump/ethtool/usbutils/dtc/less/procps.

**Why it matters:** Most of the delta is Yocto being a general-purpose distro versus us being a single-app appliance — that is the whole point of Nerves and should not be closed. But three of the absences are functional (ESP fsck, dmidecode/firmware-version visibility, thermal pins) and are covered as separate findings. A few debug tools are cheap and would materially help bench work, given the rootfs is read-only and you cannot apt-install anything at 2 a.m.

**Action:** Add to nerves_defconfig for bench work (all small, all aarch64-clean): ``` BR2_PACKAGE_DMIDECODE=y # SPI/UEFI firmware version (NPU prerequisite) BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS=y # ESP repair BR2_PACKAGE_DOSFSTOOLS_FSCK_FAT=y BR2_PACKAGE_STRESS_NG=y # thermal/soak testing, the one profile-utils tool Yocto kept BR2_PACKAGE_USBUTILS=y # lsusb: AIC8800 enumeration on the bench BR2_PACKAGE_ETHTOOL=y # r8169 link state BR2_PACKAGE_DTC=y # dtc/fdtdump: confirm the DTB UEFI actually handed us ``` Do NOT add openssh, NetworkManager, rsyslog, fwupd or thermald (no init to run them, or x86-only). Record in README a short "what this system deliberately omits versus the Yocto reference" list so the omissions read as decisions rather than oversights.

**Uncertainty:** Each added package's exact Buildroot symbol dependencies were not individually verified beyond dmidecode, dosfstools, stress-ng and efibootmgr.
